Names and intended roles for animations 97 and 142 have no effect on play; the chosen clip simply plays. Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): play whichever clip the picker returns.

- Variant values listed in the source are 97, 115, 116, 117, 118, 119 and 142. The values 115 to 119 are the five idle variants of Behavior 8.
- Each tick the chooser returns the current idle variant. When the hold counter is set, it is drawn as a whole number of ticks from 120 to 600. When it runs out, the chooser picks a new variant among 115 to 119.
- A spider-side test can select variant 142 instead of the 115 to 119 range. The test, and the rule for variant 97, are not described here (Open question 45).

**Death (High).** A spider in state 16 whose death animation (58) is playing and has finished gets its despawn flag set, once per tick. The nest sets this flag for a registered spider in that condition and clears it when the spider is released again (SPEC-0017, Behavior 9). The region update skips the spider’s update while this flag is set, and the shared drawable test suppresses its rendering (SPEC-0006; SPEC-0046). The spider remains registered in its active slot in the material examined; the flag is cleared when the nest releases it. The spider's own corpse countdown of Behavior 36 and the release rules for hidden spiders are in SPEC-0017 (Open question 14). Animation 58 of the spiders is a one-shot clip of 35 keys whose finished flag is first seen 137 ticks after the request, so the despawn flag is set 137 ticks after the spider enters state 16 (SPEC-0017, section 9). There is no return path out of state 16 other than the valve's revive (above); Open question 47 is closed except for the object-removal question, whose default is in Open question 14.

**Agro sub-states of a spider (High).** A spider in agro runs the common agro machine (Behavior 44). Its outer sub-states 1, 3 and 4, which turn toward the hero and play the hold animations 22 or 155, 25 or 156 and 31 or 132, are the rush, keep-distance and strafe archetypes of every class-17 enemy, not a spider-specific spawn rule; the spyder rows use approach routine 4 (strafe). A revived spider (base actor state 1, class state 19) enters agro through the setter, so it uses its row's approach routine like any other agro enemy (Open question 46, resolved).

### 13. Attack sequencer (agro outer sub-state 2) and projectile spawners (Medium-High for the states and transitions; High for the spawn rule of state 2 and the spawners)

While the agro outer sub-state is 2, one attack runs through a sequencer of sixteen states, numbered 0 to 15. States 10 and 11 have no writer and are never reached. The enemy keeps the selected slot k (Behaviors 7 and 14) and reads that slot's row values. The sequencer runs once per tick. It starts in state 0 (no charge), 6 (charge) or 13 (loopable slot).

Slot animations: A = 33 + 5k (wind-up), B = 34 + 5k (strike), C = 35 + 5k (follow-through), D = 36 + 5k (blocked), E = 37 + 5k (recovery). Each is checked against the enemy's own table before it is played, and a missing animation is skipped (Behavior 20; Layout section 6). With the slot's orient flag (row u16 0x174 + 2k) set, the enemy aims at the hero at weight 1.0 in states 1 to 3. Speed words: wind-up 0x1BC + 4k (states 1 and 6), strike 0x1CC + 4k (state 2), recovery 0x1DC + 4k (state 5); they are stored directly to P before the same-update clip re-time, so the selected row value sets the clip rate for that update (Layout section 2; Behavior 1; Edge cases).

**Phase waits (High).** "Play X to its end" means: if the enemy's current animation id is not X, request X and stay; otherwise stay until the progress gate of the current id's clip passes, that is progress at least 0.99 (SPEC-0005, Clip progress), and move on in the tick it passes. The gate is read in the behaviour step, before that tick's animation pass, so it sees the cursor and fraction left by the previous tick. A phase whose id has no clip (Layout section 6) is skipped in the same tick with no wait. Nothing else ends a phase early: no animation event, no other threshold. A new attack choice cannot start until the end path has run and the recovery countdown has run out (Behavior 7, step 4), so it cannot start before E (or C, or B when they are the last phase present) has passed its gate.

**Play rate in the sequencer (High).** R = character speed scale (+0x3D4, 1.0 for every character) times P (+0x8B8). P is reset to 1.0 at the start of each update, status factors then act on it, and the sequencer replaces it with the slot's wind-up, strike or recovery word in states 1, 2, 5, 6 and 7. In states 3, 4, 8, 9, 12, 13, 14 and 15 nothing replaces it, so it is 1.0 unless a status factor applies. No other row value and no spawn value scales R. With all three words 1.0 (the Rogue, row 153) and speed byte 2, the clip speed is 0.25 keys per tick, 4 ticks per key (SPEC-0005, Playback 1a).

**Phase durations at R = 1 (High for the arithmetic; Medium-High for the exact tick offsets).** Let s be the tick in which the sequencer requests a clip (the clip starts in that tick's animation pass). In tick s + j the behaviour step sees cursor v × j (v = the clip speed, before the end rule clamps it). The pass of the tick on which the strike lands still runs at the strike speed (it was stored before the strike test); the passes from the next tick run at 1.0 (state 3 stores no speed word). A monster weapon cylinder is centred on its attachment anchor (its base is the anchor minus half its length along the axis); the shared hero-style reach routine used by the Werelupe King passes a base point P0 = A − 1.5·D1 instead (SPEC-0018 base convention). The first touching tick of a weapon window is usually not the first window tick; the per-kind and per-boss reach tables give it by distance.
- One-shot clip of F keys with speed byte 2: the cursor reaches F − 1 after 4(F − 1) passes, the next pass sets the finished flag, and the gate passes in tick s + 4(F − 1) + 1. With F = 21: s + 81 (in tick s + 80 the progress is (20 + 0.75) / 21 = 0.988, below 0.99). For F of 25 keys or more the 0.99 progress gate passes one tick earlier, on s + 4(F − 1) (speed byte 1: s + 2(F − 1) + 1 below 50 keys, s + 2(F − 1) from 50; SPEC-0005).
- Looping clip: the gate passes when (cursor + fraction) / F first reaches 0.99 (the fraction term of SPEC-0005, Clip progress). With F = 15 and speed byte 2: tick s + 59 (cursor 14.75, fraction 0.5, progress 1.017; tick s + 58 gives 0.983).
- A move to the next state made with "then state n" takes effect in the next tick, except where this list says "start B and go to state 2" (same tick). The end path runs in the tick E passes its gate, or, when E is missing, in the first tick of state 5 or 12.

- **State 0, approach and aim.** For a melee slot with d above the melee range (0x1FC + 4k): gait 0 and pursue the hero; when the hero's position or the steer target is off the walk mesh, go on to the facing test; otherwise stay. In all other cases (a missile slot, or within range): when the hero and the steer target are both on the walk mesh, go to state 1 at once; otherwise the facing test: a facing cosine of at least 0.95 (about 18 degrees) goes to state 1, a lower one turns toward the hero (blend 0.1) and stays. d and the cosine are both horizontal (High): the direction to the hero has its height difference dropped and is then normalised, d is the length before that, and the cosine is its dot product with the enemy's facing; a hero on another level, above or below, counts as standing at the same height. The turn is the aim rule of SPEC-0040 Behavior 8 with weight 0.1, so with a still hero the angle to him shrinks by roughly a tenth of itself per tick: 1 tick from 20 degrees, 5 from 30, 9 from 45, 18 from 90 and 39 from 170 degrees to the 0.95 mark (18.2 degrees). **What ends state 0 (High for the absence of any state 0 timer; Medium-High for the list).** The state has no timer, no counter and no give-up of its own, and the leash (Behavior 23) does not run in agro outer sub-state 2. With the hero and the steer target on the walk mesh, state 0 lasts one tick for a missile slot or a hero within the melee range, and for a melee slot out of range it lasts until the enemy has walked in to the melee range (the facing test is not used). Off the mesh it ends when the horizontal angle is within 18.2 degrees, which a facing enemy reaches within a few ticks, so a hero in range but on another level does not hold the enemy in state 0. The only case that holds it for good is a hero whose horizontal offset is zero (directly above or below), where the direction is the zero vector and the turn leaves the facing unchanged. The other ways out of outer 2 during state 0 are the ordinary interrupts of the agro machine: the lost-sight test (outer 9; it fires whenever the two sight points differ in height by more than 50.0 units or a wall is between them, so a hero on a level more than about 50 units above or below ends the attack within a tick; it does not run for a grab-and-throw slot), a melee dodge or block in states 0, 1 and 5 and a missile defence, a hit reaction (outer 10), a knockdown or death, and a hero that stops being attackable (the attack choice stops, but a running attack carries on). The recovery countdown and the cooldowns play no part in state 0.
- **State 1, wind-up.** Play A to its end; then start B and go to state 2 (no B: state 5).
- **State 2, strike (High for the spawn rule).** B plays. A slot with the orient flag also aims at the hero at weight 1.0 each tick, except while a grab holds the hero (Behavior 15); a missile slot always aims at the hero each tick at the row's aim turn weight (0x84 on the ground, 0x88 for a flyer), whatever the orient flag says (Medium-High). Melee slot: the strike test (below) runs each tick: a hit goes to 3, a blocked strike to 4; with no contact, B finishing goes to 5. Missile slot (type 1): while the spawn-pending flag is set, it is cleared on the first tick the slot's spawn event is not active. When the flag is clear and the spawn event is active, one spawner runs (below): row flag 0x02 selects the lobbed summon; otherwise the flight mode selects spawner 0, 1 or 2, and any other flight mode spawns nothing. The shot counter then goes up by one, even when the projectile pool was full and nothing was created. If the counter is below the slot's spawn count (0x33C + 4k), set the pending flag and stay in state 2; otherwise go to state 3. A spawn count of 0 still fires one projectile (the counter 1 is not below 0). If B finishes first, go to state 5. The slot's spawn event is bit 0x20 of the current clip frame's event word (the sequencer tests that mask at all three sites); the lower bits only choose the emit point. The shot counter can reach the spawn count only when the strike clip has that many separate release-bit windows; the sequencer leaves state 2 when the clip's gate passes. Row 221 slot 3 (spawn count 5, one window) lobs one seed per attack; the live cap is the spawn entity count of 5.
- **State 3, follow-through.** Play C to its end, then state 5. With no C: when B passes its gate, state 5. Otherwise, a strike-window word (0xBFC) controls re-entry. The strike window is open while the current frame of the playing clip has any of the frame bits 0x1, 0x2, 0x4, 0x8 or 0x10 (SPEC-0005, Table B frame bits); the strike test sets the word to 1 whenever the window is open. In state 3, a closed window, or an open window on a frame that also has bit 0x20, sets the word to 0. An open window with the word at 0 on a melee slot goes to state 2, clears the hit list and sets the word to 1. So each separate open window of B allows one more strike: a B clip with three windows can hit the hero three times in one swing (High).
- **State 4, blocked.** Play D to its end, then state 5. When the slot has no blocked clip D, the sequencer goes from state 4 to state 5 in the tick state 4 runs, and the end path runs in the following tick: a blocked strike ends two ticks after the blocked hit tick.
- **State 5, recovery.** Play E to its end, then the end path. With flag 0x08 (grab-and-throw, Behavior 15) the recovery animation is skipped.
- **Defence while the sequencer runs (R-0149).** The sequencer is the stretch in which a keep-away kind stands still. The melee defence (dodge or block) is evaluated only in sequencer states 0, 1 and 5, so a swing that lands in the strike, follow-through, blocked or charge states cannot be dodged; the missile defence is evaluated in every state except 5. A defence that does start abandons the sequence (outer 5 to 8 replace outer 2). Closing in while such an enemy is in its strike states is therefore the one time a melee hero is certain not to be dodged, whatever the hold.
- **State 6, charge wind-up.** The start point = the enemy's position. Play A to its end; then start B and go to state 7 with the tick counter at 0 (no B: state 12). This tick requests B and sets state 7; state 7 first runs on the next tick. Each tick of state 6 the enemy aims at the hero with the turn weight (row 0x84) whether or not the slot has the orient flag, and stores its position as the start point, so the start point is the position on the tick B is requested. Only state 6 aims.
- **State 7, charge run.** The tick counter goes up by one, and t = the distance travelled from the start point. Charge kinds 0, 1 and 3 run the strike test (hit: state 8; blocked: state 9); kind 2 runs no strike test. For every kind, a tick counter above 100 with the enemy's speed at most 0.001 goes to state 12. That speed is the length of the position change of the previous motion tick (3D, in world units per tick, 0.001 per tick being 0.06 units per second at 60 Hz), measured after the root step, ground, character push and navigation-mesh confinement of that tick: it is neither the clip's root step nor a velocity, so an enemy pinned against a wall or a mesh border (clip root step 0.333 per tick but nothing moved) leaves state 7 on the first pass with the counter at 101 or more, while a slide along a wall at 30 degrees still moves about 0.17 per tick and does not end by this rule (it ends by the distance or a strike). The value read on a pass is the displacement of the previous tick, because the character update runs before the motion tick within a tick (High for the field, Medium-High that the confinement push-back is inside it, Medium that a wall contact leaves exactly 0). The pass does not return after setting state 12 here; the kind tests that follow run and also end in state 12 or return with it already set. Answered from the game code; the original was not run. Kinds 0 and 1: t at least the charge distance goes to 12. Kind 2: the hero within the melee range goes to 8; otherwise t at least the charge distance goes to 12. Kind 3: while t is below the charge distance and the hero is beyond the melee range, it keeps running only while the body's height above the ground (the ground gap: 0 while the floor-contact flag is set) is more than 0.1, and otherwise goes to 12; t at least the charge distance, or the hero within the melee range, goes to 12 as well. So a kind-3 charge continues only while the body is airborne (High for the control flow; this is a ground gap, not a speed). No kind steers during the run (the sequencer calls no aim in state 7); only state 6 aims. Charge kinds 0 and 1 clear the enemy's collidable flag on every state-7 tick (the sequencer sets it again at the start of every call), so the enemy is not solid during their run; kinds 2 and 3 never clear it, so a kind-2 or kind-3 charger stays solid in states 7 and 8 and the hero's body push shoves a hero who stands in its path.
- **State 8, charge hit.** Kinds 0 and 1: play C; t at least the charge distance, or the blocked flag (0xCC4), goes to 12. Kind 2: play C with the strike test each tick; the sequencer ignores the test's result (a guarding hero still takes the blocked path: reduced damage and the block reaction), so a kind-2 charge never goes to state 9; C finished goes to 12. Kind 3: C finished goes to 12.
- **State 9, blocked charge.** By the slot's blocked-charge kind (0x2AC + 4k): 0 plays D and goes to 12 when t is at least the charge distance; 1 goes to 12 when D has finished; 2 starts hit reaction kind 2 (outer 10, Behavior 44) with the stun countdown (0xBF0) = trunc(float[row 0x28C + 4k, row 0x27C + 4k)) and the stun type (0xC60) = row 0x29C + 4k; any other value starts hit reaction kind 3. Resource 530 has 45 used class-17 charge slots: blocked-charge kind 0 on 38, kind 2 on 3, and kind 4 on 4; kind 1 does not occur. Thus the shipped charge slots use the kind-4 fallback to reaction kind 3, while the kind-1 timing branch has no known class-17 row instance.
- **States 10 and 11.** No writer; never reached.
- **State 12, charge end.** Play E, then the end path.
- **Flyers in states 7, 8, 9 and 12.** A flying character's row speed (Behavior 48) is added along the facing in sequencer states 7, 8, 9 and 12 whatever animation plays, and, for a Can Fly 1 row only, the height target is the hero's height plus 2.0 in those states (SPEC-0040 Behavior 14D; a Can Fly 2 row keeps its usual height rule, Behavior 51 item 4). The charge run therefore moves at the sum of the row speed and the clip's root travel scaled by the strike word, and state 8 keeps moving at the row speed.
- **State 13, loop approach.** While d is above the melee range, pursue the hero; otherwise go to state 14 with the countdown at 60 ticks.
- **State 14, loop strike.** Play B. When the countdown is 0, the hero is damaged directly and the countdown is reloaded with int[row s32 0x4AC + 4k, row s32 0x4BC + 4k]; otherwise the countdown goes down by one. The slot flag 0x20 selects the fallback victim reaction: clear requests base state 17 with a selected animation; set calls the victim's additional attack-reaction method, which can reset the hero attack sub-state to 13 when its checks pass (Layout section 2; SPEC-0036). The flag's design-facing name remains unknown. When d is above row 0x4CC + 4k, go to state 15. The direct damage is the enemy's attack damage to a hero (blocked argument 0, no shield block) and goes to the hero's take-hit with the blocked flag equal to slot flag 0x20. State 13 hands over with the countdown at 60, so the first damage is on the 61st tick of state 14; later intervals are the drawn value plus 1.
- **State 15, loop end.** Play E, then the end path.

**End path.** Outer sub-state 0; the recovery countdown = int[int(row 0x68), int(row 0x64)]; the slot cooldown = int[int(0x21C + 4k), int(0x20C + 4k)] again, so the slot cooldown is set twice: at the choice and here (Edge cases).

**Strike test (High for the flow, the contact rule, the zero-damage rule and the knockback; Medium-High for the lane details of the posed frames).** The test runs once per tick from the sequencer: in state 2 for a melee slot, in state 7 for charge kinds 0, 1 and 3 and in state 8 for kind 2. It reports no contact, a hit or a blocked strike. In order:

1. No selected hero: no contact. With flag 0x08 the grab machine runs instead of steps 2 to 6 (Behavior 15).
2. Window. The enemy must be attack-active: any of the table-B bits 0x1 to 0x10 is set on its current frame, as the previous tick's animation pass left it (SPEC-0005, Table B frame bits). Otherwise no contact. When the window is open the strike-window word (0xBFC) is set to 1 first (state 3 reads it).
3. Guard. The hero guards when its guard predicate holds (action state 20, Roberta in attack sub-state 6 or 7, or the guard timer above 0; SPEC-0036, Guard timer and per-attacker block). A hero that does not guard, or any hero when the slot has flag 0x01, takes the open path (step 4). A guarding hero takes the blocked path (step 5) only when dot(hero facing, attacker facing) is at most -0.5, or when it is Roberta spinning (sub-state 6 or 7), which blocks from every direction. A guarding hero with the dot above -0.5 takes the open path: the strike lands in full and the hero flinches as if it were not guarding.
4. Open path. When the hero's id is already in the attacker's hit list, no contact, and the weapon test is not made. Otherwise the weapon test below runs; a miss is no contact. On contact the damage is computed with blocked argument 0 (SPEC-0036, Enemy attack damage to a hero).
5. Blocked path. When the hero's id is in the hit list, no contact. Otherwise the weapon test runs; a miss is no contact. On contact: a spinning Roberta gets a blocked result at once, with no damage, no take-hit, no hit-list entry and no effect except the block spark (effect 221 at the hero's aim point) on every overlapping tick. Any other guard: the hero's id is added to the hit list, the hero plays its block reaction, and the damage is computed with blocked argument 1 (the shield block is subtracted).
6. Zero damage. When the computed damage is not above 0 and the strike is not a grab throw, nothing further happens: no take-hit, no hit effect, no theft, no Mote drain, no status roll and no knockback. On the open path the result is no contact and the hit list stays unchanged, so the strike is simply tested again on the next tick of the window (armour, the petpet factor or a shield can reduce a small damage word to 0). On the blocked path the result is a blocked strike, and the hero is already in the list.
7. Landing (damage above 0, or the grab throw), in this order: the hero's take-hit with the whole-number damage, the attacker's id and the blocked argument (1 when the slot has flag 0x20, otherwise the path's value of 0 or 1); an attacker under status 11 (poison attack) poisons the hero (SPEC-0029, Behavior 27); a hero left with health below 1 sends the enemy to state 10; the hero's id is added to the hit list (a second time on the blocked path, so the list then holds it twice); the hit spark (effect 220 at the hero's aim point); then the effects of the slot's flags in the order theft (0x04), Mote drain (0x80), status roll, and last the knockback.
8. Result. A landed strike on the open path reports a hit (the sequencer goes to state 3, or state 8 in a charge). A blocked result, whether or not it dealt damage, reports a blocked strike (state 4, or state 9 in a charge of kind 0, 1 or 3) and plays a positioned sound cue from the hero's hit-sound bank at the hero. No contact keeps the sequencer where it is, except that in state 2 a finished strike clip (progress at least 0.99) then goes to state 5.

**Weapon test: the contact rule (High).** Weapon N (N = 1 to 5) is tested on a tick only when bit 2^(N - 1) of the enemy's current frame is set; the weapons are tried in order N = 1 to 5 and the first overlap ends the test. Weapon N supplies the finite-cylinder inputs: radius = the monster row word at +4(N - 1), full height = the row word at +0x14 + 4(N - 1) (SPEC-0040 Behavior 12A; the schema names of the two fields are swapped), centre at the translation row of the enemy's attachment frame N - 1 and axis along that frame's Z row. The frames are the ones the enemy's last animation pass posed (SPEC-0005, Attach points and carrying). The static contact test is the asymmetric rule in SPEC-0018: for near-parallel axes it checks the axial intervals and summed radii; in the general nonparallel case the second input uses the three-edge rule described there. The weapon is tested against every hurt capsule of the hero, in bone order. The hero's capsules are the skeleton bones with a hurt length above 0, posed from the hero's bone frames of the previous pass (SPEC-0036, Hurt shapes and hit list). Tor, in all eight outfits, has nine: eight of length 0.367 and radius 0.367 on bones 0, 1, 2, 3, 24, 25, 27 and 30, and one of length 0.823 and radius 0.431 on bone 4. Roberta, in all eight outfits, has one: length 1.112, radius 0.436, on bone 0. No other test is made: there is no distance gate, no facing or arc test (the arc belongs to the attack choice only), no hittable query, no sight line and no height rule; the hero's body cylinder is not used. A weapon with both size inputs zero is a point. Consequence: actual reach depends on the contact branch and the posed frames; simply adding radii is valid only in the parallel branch. Test vector (synthetic, parallel axes): weapon radius 3.0, full height 1.0, centre (10, 1, 0), axis (0, 1, 0); Roberta's capsule with base (11, 0, 0) and axis (0, 1, 0): the lateral distance is 1.0 (at most 3.0 + 0.436) and the axial offset of the centres is 0.444 (at most (1.0 + 1.112) / 2), so the weapon touches; with the base at (13.6, 0, 0) the lateral distance 3.6 is above 3.436 and it does not.

**Effects of a landed strike.** The strike never calls the hero's hittable query, so a hero in the hurt state or in state 48 can be struck again (the hit list of the attack is the only limit). Flag 0x01 makes the strike unblockable (step 3). Flag 0x04 performs the theft described in SPEC-0029 Behaviors 11a and 11b. When the status id (0x36C + 4k) is not -1, the handler draws an integer from 0 through 100 inclusive and applies the status only when the draw is strictly below the row chance (0x37C + 4k); chance 100 succeeds on 100 of 101 outcomes, and chance 0 never succeeds. The status duration is int[int(0x39C + 4k), int(0x38C + 4k)] / 60 whole seconds. This differs from the projectile-on-hero status roll in SPEC-0029 Behavior 9, whose comparison includes the threshold. The hit also knocks the hero back in the direction normalize(9.0 x facing + 12.0 x up), with the slot's knockback value (0x35C + 4k) as the velocity magnitude. Character-record velocity is expressed in units per 1/30-second step (SPEC-0040 Behavior 14A), so this value is per-step, not units per second; the shared integrator multiplies velocity by 1/30 each tick. With horizontal unit facing, the normalized direction has horizontal magnitude 0.6 and vertical component 0.8 (SPEC-0036 Knockback on the hero). Flag 0x80 drains Motes (amount 0x42C + 4k): the amount is taken from each of the hero's three equipped attack items that is not permanently owned, and an equipped item that is then out of stock, or that no longer exists, is unequipped (Medium). The knockback is applied only while the hero's health is still above 0 after the hit, and only when the slot's knockback value is above 0; a grab throw puts a surviving hero in state 48 regardless (Medium-High). A hit that leaves the hero at 0 HP sends the enemy to state 10 (victory pose). With flag 0x08 the grab-and-throw (Behavior 15) replaces the strike.

**Projectiles.** The projectiles go into the enemy projectile pool of SPEC-0029 Behavior 9 (one global pool of ten slots, first free slot, a full pool creates nothing), which moves them, tests hits and releases them. The missile count (0x33C) is the spawn count of state 2.

**Spawners of state 2 (High).** k is the attack slot. Common to all four spawners:

- Launch point: the enemy's emit point for the spawn-event bit that fired. The bits are tested in the order 1, 2, 4, 8 and 16; the first one set selects emit point 0, 1, 2, 3 or 4 (bit 1 gives point 0, bit 2 point 1, and so on). Bit 0x20 and any other word select point 0 (High). Test vector: event word 0x26 selects point 1; event word 0x20 selects point 0. The emit point is the translation row of attachment frame k from the enemy's last animation pass (the frames the strike test reads), so it includes the strike clip's root travel and turns with the enemy. The first move and hit test happen in the spawn tick (SPEC-0029 Behavior 13a).
- Aim point = target point + e w. w is a random horizontal unit vector, from x and z each drawn uniformly in [-1, 1] and normalized, drawn in the order e, then x, then z (High). e = U[0, row inaccuracy 0x30C + 4k], plus 5.0 when the enemy is outside the camera cone (dot(camera forward, unit(enemy - camera)) < 0.85), plus 3.0 when the attack manager has the enemy waiting in line (Behavior 45).
- Owner = the enemy id; element = row 0x3C; damage = row 0x34C + 4k times the enemy damage scale (1.0; x 0.5 under curse, status 1; x 1.5 under strength, status 4), floored at 0.
- Radius = row 0x2EC + 4k. Roll = row 0x32C + 4k. Spin rate = row 0x31C + 4k. Visual kind = row 0x43C + 4k. Six effect ids = the row u16 words at 0x46C + 2k + 8j (j = 0 to 5).
- Area: radius row 0x45C + 4k, duration trunc(row 0x44C + 4k) ticks. Status: id row 0x36C + 4k, chance trunc(row 0x37C + 4k), value trunc(U[row 0x38C + 4k, row 0x39C + 4k] / 60).
- Trail effect (visual kinds below 5): entry n of the enemy's 11-entry model-part pointer table, with n = 1, 7, 8, 9 or 10 for a shot counter of 0, 1, 2, 3 or 4 (other counts give 1). These are zero-based pointer indices, not projectile effect IDs. Flight mode 1 always uses entry 1. A null entry draws nothing for that shot; the Plant Gunner's four-shot missile (row 220, slot 2) finds null entries 1, 7, 8 and 9 in its body-only model set, so its shots are invisible but move, hit and do their damage (SPEC-0029, Shots with no part; from the code and disc data, not run in the original; High). In resource 530, 16 missile slots have visual kinds below 5; six have at least one of their six separate projectile effect IDs not equal to -1. Thus those IDs are not universally empty for this missile subset. This is a count of the data; reachability and whether each effect emits depend on the attack path.
- Ignore-world flag = row flag 0x10 (flight modes 0 and 2).

| Spawner | Projectile kind | Target point | Velocity | Lifetime (ticks) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Flight mode 0 | 0 (straight) | hero position + (0, 2.5, 0) | unit(aim - launch) x v | trunc(range / v) |
| Flight mode 2 | 17 (homing) | hero position + (0, 2.5, 0) | unit(aim - launch) x v | 2 x trunc(range / v) |
| Flight mode 1 | 1 (gravity, area on landing) | hero position | ballistic, ramp speed s | trunc(60 T) + 1 |
| Row flag 0x02 | 8 (lobbed summon) | hero position | ballistic, speed v | trunc(60 T) + 1 |

v = the launch speed (0x2CC + 4k) in units per tick; range = 0x2FC + 4k in units.

**Ballistic launch (High).** d = the horizontal distance from the launch point to the aim point; dy = aim.y - launch.y. For flight mode 1, u = 1 - (d - bmin)/(bmax - bmin) with bmin = row 0x18C + 4k and bmax = row 0x17C + 4k (the slot's trigger band), and s = v + u^2 (v_end - v) with v_end = row 0x2DC + 4k; so s = v_end at bmin and s = v at bmax, and u is not clamped. The lobbed summon uses s = v. Then T = d / s ticks, the horizontal velocity is s x unit(horizontal offset), and vy = (0.5 g' T^2 + dy) / T with g' = 32 / 60^2 (SPEC-0029 Behavior 9). The lifetime trunc(60 T) + 1 multiplies a flight time already in ticks by 60 (a unit slip in the original; reproduce it).

**Lobbed summon (row flag 0x02; High).** Kind 8, visual kind 23, damage 3.0 and radius 1.0 (fixed; kind 8 never touches a character). The projectile carries the slot's spawn key (row u16 0x3CC + 2k); it is aimed once, at the release, at the hero's feet plus the scatter of the spawners (for row 221 slot 3 the row inaccuracy is 0, so the offset is 0, 5.0 outside the camera cone, plus 3.0 while waiting in line), from the slot's emit point (point 0 for a frame whose only spawn bit is 0x20), with the slot's launch speed as its horizontal speed. This spawner does not copy the row's spin rate (row 221 slot 3 holds 1.0): the shot's spin is 0, so its forward axis stays the launch direction for the whole flight. The effect ids it carries are 2127 (flight), -1, 412, -1, 412 and 412 (SPEC-0029 Behavior 9, six effect ids). When it lands, touches the world (ground, static mesh, collision primitive or object) or its owner is no longer found among the bound characters, the keyed summon-reserved record spawns at the shot's position at that moment, facing along the shot's forward axis (SPEC-0029 Behaviors 9 and 31). An expiry in the air and a world start spawn nothing. The same flag also gates the slot in the attack choice by the count of the characters bound to the enemy as their summoner (Behavior 7, step 5e), corpses included until released; both readings hold. The whole chain for the Spore Monster is in SPEC-0064, "Seed spawn, step by step". On disc the missile slots with this flag are row 211 slot 0 (Minion Wizard, Minion Grunt) and row 221 slot 3 (Spore Monster, Flower Monster).

### 14. Counter-attack choice (High)

The counter-attack choice runs every agro tick, right after the normal choice (Behavior 7), in any outer sub-state. It has no attackable test, no recovery test and no outer sub-state gate, and it does not count the cooldowns down. It reads the row's Counter Attack Trigger (0x22C + 4k) and Counter Attack Chance (0x23C + 4k) (Layout section 5).

1. Walk the table slots 0 to 3 (1 to 4 when slots are counted from 1). A slot is eligible when all of these hold:
   a. its type is not 0xFFFF, and its cooldown is below 1;
   b. trigger min < d < trigger max (strict), as in Behavior 7 step 5b;
   c. the facing cosine is above the melee arc, as in Behavior 7 step 5c (horizontal: the same helper gives the direction and the distance in step b, with the height difference dropped);
   d. its trigger code holds:
      - 1: the active hero's recover window is open this tick (below);
      - 2: after a dodge: outer sub-state 5 or 6, and the recover window;
      - 3: after a block: outer sub-state 7 or 8, and the recover window;
      - 4: sequencer recovery: outer sub-state 2 with the sequencer in state 5;
      - 5: the sequencer is in state 4 and L1 is held in pad slot 0 (mask 0x0004 in the active hero's current held-button word; SPEC-0033, Controller map). The word is rebuilt from the controller report each input pass, so this is a held-input condition, not an animation event or persistent hero flag. The hero's control-return flag at +0x3D0 is a separate boolean and is not read here. Thirty-two active class-17 row slots on disc use code 5;
      - 6: hit reaction: outer sub-state 10, evaluated on every tick of the reaction, so the chance over a reaction is 1 - (1 - c)^N for N eligible ticks (c = the chance word over 101 values): near 1 for chances of 25 and more, but only 94 % for a chance of 5 over a 57-tick reaction, and less when the reaction's own slide carries the enemy out of the slot's band (the Spore Monster's slide of 10.41 units takes it out of 15 units: SPEC-0064, Spore Monster, Defence and counters);
      - 0, -1 and any other value: never.
2. The last eligible slot of the walk wins: a later eligible slot replaces an earlier one.
3. If the winning slot is not already the selected slot, one roll int[0, 100] (101 values) is drawn for the tick. When the roll is below the chance, the slot becomes the selected slot, its cooldown = int[int(0x21C + 4k), int(0x20C + 4k)], and the sequencer starts in state 0 (charge type -1) or state 6 (charge type set). A chance of 0 never passes; a chance of 100 passes on every roll except 100.

Recover window (High): table-B event bit 0x800 on any of the active hero's playing clips this tick (SPEC-0005, Table B frame bits). Only the hero reports it; the same query on any other character reports none. Tor's swings (ids 14 to 20 and 27) carry it; Roberta's clips never do, so trigger codes 1 to 3 never fire while Roberta is the active hero.

The distances are not redrawn, so a counter charge runs to the charge distance of the last draw (Edge cases). The loopable start state 13 is not used by this path. The "states" named by the trigger codes are agro outer sub-states and sequencer states, not class states; the choice never enters class state 18.

### 15. Grab-and-throw strike (row flag 0x08) (Medium)

A slot whose flags (0x3AC + 4k) have bit 0x08 replaces the melee strike of the sequencer with a grab-and-throw. On disc 10 used class-17 slots have the bit. The machine is described step by step in SPEC-0029 Behavior 23 (its states 0 to 4); in outline:

1. Grab: when the hero is within the slot's melee range (0x1FC + 4k) and the facing cosine is above 0.5, and the sight path is clear, the hero is put in base state 53 (held).
2. Hold: while the hero is held, a periodic effect with the values 1.0 and 0.7 (the grab rumble, SPEC-0033) runs every 61 ticks. Its timer is the run-time word 0x2DD54C (boot value -1): one global countdown shared by all enemies, not a per-enemy field. The countdown is loaded with 60 (the frame-rate word as an integer); on each held tick, if the countdown reads 0 the effect fires and the countdown is reloaded with 60, and otherwise it falls by 1. It therefore counts 60 down to 0 and fires on every 61st tick (SPEC-0029 Behavior 23).
3. Throw: the hero's motion-record velocity becomes normalize(15.0 x facing + 8.0 x up) x the slot's throw speed (row 0x41C + 4k). This is an initial velocity in units per second; the shared motion step advances position by velocity x 1/30 each tick at 60 ticks per second (SPEC-0040 Behavior 14A). Later gravity, contact and movement constraints affect the resulting trajectory. (High for the velocity field and unit conversion.)

With bit 0x08 set, the sequencer skips the recovery animation of state 5, and the lost-sight test of the agro machine does not run while the attack runs (Behavior 44). Entering agro outer sub-state 2 writes the grab machine directly to state 1; state 0 is restored when the next attack slot is selected. The four grab-point event selectors read the enemy's current clip and choose its attach transform (SPEC-0029 Behavior 23). The hero's held-state handler requests animation 143 every tick and asks every actor in the level's actor list the same yes-or-no question; it remains in base state 53 while any actor answers yes, and returns to base state 1 on the first tick on which none does. The enemies answer yes only while they are in agro outer sub-state 2, in sequencer state 2 (the strike state) and the current attack slot has the grab flag 0x08 set. The question does not identify or compare against the enemy that initiated the grab, so any enemy that is striking with a grab slot holds the hero. The heroes and every other actor kind keep the base answer, which is always no (High; Medium that no non-class-17 actor reaches the sub-state words). Test vector: hero in state 53, one enemy with outer sub-state 2, sequencer state 2 and slot flag 0x08: the hero stays in state 53. On the tick the sequencer leaves state 2, with no other grabbing enemy, the hero is in base state 1. The same enemy in sequencer state 2 with a slot whose flag 0x08 is clear does not hold the hero. The row field 0xE0 is not a cooldown of this strike: it is the defence hold time of the defence reactions (Behavior 44).

**Grab timing and damage (High).** The grab machine runs from the strike test on every tick of sequencer state 2. In the tick it seizes the hero, the enemy requests the slot's follow-through clip C (id 35 + 5k) at the strike speed word, so the sequencer's own strike clip B is replaced for the rest of the swing and clip C plays for the whole hold, because the sequencer stays in state 2 until the throw. The throw needs event bit 0x20 on the enemy's current clip frame; the hold ends on the tick that bit is first seen (Swamp Tentacle slot 2: clip C key 28 at speed 1.5 gives s + 76, where s is the tick B was requested). The strike test returns a hit only on the tick the machine reaches its throw state, and that hit is a take-hit of damage 0 (which puts the hero in action state 48) plus the steal roll for the Thug. A grab deals no damage on any row, whatever damage word the slot lists. The sequencer then plays the rest of clip C at rate 1.0 and skips the recovery clip. The hold therefore always runs on clip C, and every shipped grab slot throws on its C clip's release bit: Minion Brute (clip 6, key 23: throw on s + 93), Thug (clip 18, key 25: s + 101), Plant Monster (row 219, clip 6, key 28: s + 113), Swamp Tentacle (s + 76). The held hero is carried: every hold tick the machine sets the enemy's carry slot to him and the enemy's model step places him at the enemy's attach point for the current clip frame (SPEC-0029 Behavior 23; SPEC-0005, Attach points and carrying; High). His position at the seize tick is kept as the first last good position. A failed choose step puts the machine in its recover state, which requests the slot's recovery clip E (id 37 + 5k) from the next tick when the table has it; the strike state then waits for the gate of that clip (the current clip) and moves to state 5, and the end path runs on the following tick (Medium for the failure path).

### 18. Band factors by hero action state (High)

Two factors, F1 and F2, are looked up by the hero's current action state (SPEC-0036). F1 scales the notice band (Behavior 3) and the emerge distance of state 8 (Behavior 40); F2 replaces F1 in the notice band when the sight line is blocked (Behavior 3). The values are boot data words: F1 is the f32 at 0x2DD5A4 + 4i and F2 the f32 at 0x2DD5C0 + 4i, with i = 0 for action states 2 and 5, 1 for 3 and 9, 2 for 4, 6 and 10, 3 for 21 and 41, 4 for 23, 5 for 22 and 6 for 1. Any other action state uses the fixed constants F1 = 1.0 and F2 = 0.5.

| Hero action state | F1 | F2 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 0.7 | 0.0 |
| 2, 5 | 0.75 | 0.25 |
| 3, 9 | 1.5 | 0.75 |
| 4, 6, 10 | 1.75 | 0.9 |
| 21, 41 | 0.5 | 0.3 |
| 22 | 0.5 | 0.0 |
| 23 | 0.3 | 0.0 |
| other | 1.0 | 0.5 |

An F2 of 0.0 makes the blocked-sight band empty, so in those hero action states an enemy without the always-has-line-of-sight flag cannot notice the hero through an obstacle.

### 19. Animation codes by stage, Draik Skeleton lookups (High)

These lookups belong to the Draik Skeleton (class 12; SPEC-0029 Behavior 6), not to the common enemies: the only routines that call them are the Draik's own attack handlers (reached through its attack dispatch), and no class-17 routine calls them. Each maps the Draik's stage value to an animation index. Stage values 1 to 5 are used by the five-entry lookups; the four-entry lookups cover stages 1 to 4, and stages 2 and 4 are never entered. Any other index returns -1 (no animation). Roles (High for the tables and the callers; Medium-High for the labels): group A is the start animation of the fire and ice breath, group B its held animation and group C its end animation, by stage; group D (index 1 gives 7) is the claw; group E (index 3 gives 0x20, index 5 gives 0x35) is the stomp; and two five-entry lookups not listed below give the turn animation (10, 0x18, 0x22, 0x2F, 0x37) and the fire and ice spit (6, 0x13, 0x1F, 0x2C, 0x34) by stage.

| Group | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A | 3 | 0x10 | 0x1C | 0x29 | (none) |
| B | 4 | 0x11 | 0x1D | 0x2A | (none) |
| C | 5 | 0x12 | 0x1E | 0x2B | (none) |
| D | 7 | 0x14 | (none) | (none) | (none) |
| E | (none) | (none) | 0x20 | (none) | 0x35 |

Group B is group A with every code one higher, and group C is group A with every code two higher. The main-family table gives a concrete clip-index mapping for several shipped Werelupe kinds: IDs 3, 16, 28 and 41 resolve to clips 17, 21, 0 and 26 for Werelupe Bashers and Elites, and to clips 16, 21, 0 and 26 for Werelupe Bone Throwers. These IDs select entries in the character record's bound main clip bank. For Poison Spyders, IDs 3, 16, 28 and 41 resolve to clips 13, 29, 3 and 8 in all 28 checked placements. For all 86 scanned Drackonack records of type 66, variant 0 (monster row 144), the same IDs resolve to clips 11, 23, 21 and 18 in a 40-entry main clip bank. For Minion Archer (monster type 80), IDs 3, 16, 28 and 41 map to clip entries 21, 21, none and 9 in all 145 scanned character records across 34 resources and variants 7 to 13. Test vector: ID 3 -> clip 21; ID 16 -> clip 21; ID 28 -> no clip; ID 41 -> clip 9. For variant 7, the active fourth attack slot uses animation IDs 48 to 52; all 37 checked character records select clip entries none, 14, none, 9 and none respectively. For Minion Warrior (monster type 82), the 85 checked character records give IDs 3, 16, 28 and 41 as clips 1, 26, 3 and 9 in 83 records; the variant-6 record maps them to 1, 31, none and 9, and one variant-0 record maps them to 0, 31, none and 8. In these tables, 4095 means no clip. These are clip-entry mappings, not visual action labels; mappings for other codes and common-enemy kinds remain open (Open question 49).

### 20. Attack and emerge codes from state words (High)

(a) Attack code. The selected attack slot (Layout section 1, object offset 0xA24, value 0 to 3) selects a code. Group 1 gives 0x21, 0x26, 0x2B and 0x30 for values 0 to 3: these are the slot wind-up animations A (33 + 5k) of the sequencer. Group 2 gives 0x24, 0x29, 0x2E and 0x33: these are the slot blocked animations D (36 + 5k) (Behavior 13). The code is returned only when the animation is present in the enemy's animation table; otherwise the result is -1, and the sequencer skips that animation.

(b) Emerge code. The default code is 10. When either of two per-class tests passes (the flyer test), the code is 0xA5 (165). Otherwise the selector word (Layout section 1, +0xC8 of the spawn record) selects the code: 0 gives 10, 1 gives 0x85 (133), 2 gives 0x86 (134), 3 gives 0x87 (135) and 4 gives 0x88 (136). These are the emerge animations of state 8 only, not idle variants; the alarm-group check always requests animation 10. The matching hide pick of state 8 is 9, or 128 to 131 by the same selector (Behavior 40).

### 21. Action-19 gate (Medium)

The gate is called by the common-enemy pre-step when the retarget countdown reaches 0 (Behavior 2, step 4). It returns 1 without a class test when the current class state is not 19. In class state 19, it calls the class-17 predicate: the predicate blocks when agro outer sub-state 2 and sequencer state 0 to 3 are both active; outside that range, a nonzero object word at 0xE58 also blocks. The gate inverts this result: a block skips target reacquisition; a pass performs the navmesh pick and sets state 22 directly. The word 0xE58 is the attack-active latch of the Black Knight's swing handlers and is always 0 for common enemies (Open question 54).

### 22. Spacing predicate (Medium)

A candidate point passes only when its horizontal distance is at least 5.0 units from the hero and from every other non-null object in the shared pool. The test uses the horizontal-distance lane of the shared position helper, which ignores height. The enemy itself is not compared with itself.

### 23. Agro tail: route flag and leash (High)

The tail runs at the end of each agro tick (Behavior 44, step 5).

1. In outer sub-states 1, 3 and 4 only: the route flag (0xAFC) is set to whether a route to the hero exists.
2. With no route: if one slot's trigger band holds d and the selected slot index is 1, the enemy faces the hero and stops; in normal play this branch does not run, because the normal choice sets the selected slot to -1 earlier in the same tick. It can run only while the hero is not attackable (Behavior 7, step 1): that step returns before it clears the selected slot, so the slot of the last attack stays selected, and if that was slot index 1 the branch is live in outer sub-states 1, 3 and 4 until the hero is attackable again (Medium-High; Edge cases). Otherwise the leash counter (0xB50) goes up by one when the enemy's current walk waypoint is at least 15.0 units from the hero, and is set to 0 when it is closer.
3. With a route, or in any other outer sub-state: the leash counter is set to 0.
4. When the leash counter is above 180 ticks (3 seconds), it is set to 0 and the setter runs with 0 (the default case, Behavior 5).

Test vector: outer sub-state 4, no route, the walk waypoint 15.0 units from the hero: the counter is 181 after 181 ticks and the enemy leaves agro on that tick. With the waypoint 14.9 units from the hero the counter stays 0. With a route the counter stays 0 at any distance.

### 24. Distance-hide gate (High)

The gate runs every agro tick, from step 3 of the agro machine (Behavior 44), only while the outer sub-state is 1 (rush) and the class state is not 14. When the row's distance hide flag (u16 0xA0) is nonzero, the hero's distance d is above the row's hide distance (0xA4), and a roll int[0, 100] is below the row's hide chance (0xA8), the enemy enters state 14 (retreat home and hide, Behavior 38). The roll is drawn again every tick the other tests pass. It is a closed integer draw (0 to 100, both included) on the shared random generator, whose state is at 0x39BE50 (SPEC-0008; Open question 53).

Rows with the flag: rows 9, 11, 237, 238 and 239 use approach routine 1 (for example the tentacles), so the gate is live for them. Rows 233 to 236 (spyders) use approach routine 4, so the gate never fires for them; the flag only makes state 8 hide in place (Behavior 40).

Test vector: flag 1, hide distance 5, chance 30, outer sub-state 1, d = 8: a roll from 0 to 29 (30 of the 101 values) enters state 14; a roll of 30 or more does not. At d = 4 the gate never fires. The same row in outer sub-state 4 never fires.

### 25. Proximity retarget (High for the gate, point and target update; Medium-High for the distance-threshold role)

In the state-5 side routine, when the timer has expired and the stored positive scalar r (object offset 0xC9C) is above 0, let P be the character’s position, F its forward basis, and Q its current offset point (object offset 0xCA0). If the horizontal XZ distance between P + 6F and Q is less than r, set A = P + 3r × unit3D(P − Q), store A at object offset 0xAD0, and pass A to the walk-target setter (SPEC-0056). The comparison is strict; equality does not retarget. The trigger ignores Y, while the normalized displacement uses X, Y and Z. The setter updates the navigation target and clears its arrival flag; the movement pass follows separately. The scalar and point are supplied by the CharacterSetAvoidPoint world-pack setting: index n of the world's 0x50-byte record table (the type-11 table also used for locators) holds the point Q in the first three floats at record offset 0x30 and the scalar r in the fourth float (offset 0x3C); the index is a plain table index, not a user id. Exactly one script calls it: the Park Shrine pack (the pack whose stage-4 arm trigger starts a control thread) applies index 33 to the six Evil Harris, entity ids 16 to 21, with Q = (0.4049, 10.1515, 1043.5057) and r = 9.0 for all six. Q lies at the centre of the arena, at the same x and z as the idle Harris of the second region (0.405, 9.641, 1043.506) and 0.51 higher; the six wanderers stand 31 to 46 units away. In shipped play the call has no effect (High): the thread makes the six calls in one step, right after it queues the load of the Harris region (region index 4) and before that load has started, so no character with those ids exists yet and each call finds none and does nothing. The scalar stays 0 for every character, the gate above is never tested, and a player never sees a retarget. The rewrite needs no avoid point (Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): keep the scalar at 0 for every character and omit the rule, or keep it dormant). Were it applied, Harris at (0.4, 9.64, 1030) facing +Z would test the point (0.4, 9.64, 1036) at horizontal distance 7.5 below 9, and retarget to about (0.4, 8.62, 1003.0). The scalar acts as a distance threshold in the same coordinate units as P and Q: it is compared with a horizontal length and scales a 3D position displacement. Its design-facing label (a radius versus a clearance) has no effect on play. The factor 3.0 in A = P + 3r × unit3D(P − Q) is a spatial multiplier; it is separate from class 35’s wait of tick-rate × 3.0 (Open question 28).

Test vector: P=(0,0,0), F=(1,0,0), Q=(6,0,0), r=1 gives gate distance 0 and target A=(-3,0,0). With Q=(7,0,0), the gate distance is exactly 1, so the strict comparison does not retarget.

### 26. Value tables (lookups High; meaning unknown)

Seven tables of floats are read by 1-based lookups, where index 1 is the first entry. The lookups rooted at 0x2DA6A0 and 0x2DA8F8 are not common-enemy tuning: the first is read by the class-12 Draik breath standoff handlers as the 10.0 lower bound, paired with the adjacent 15.0 upper-bound table; the second is read by the class-12 Draik stomp handler as the 7.0 approach boundary (SPEC-0029, Draik behavior). The two lookup families use the current Draik stage as their index. The remaining tables of the family are also Draik attack-band and standoff tables, indexed by Draik stage: the breath abort distance (0x2DA6C8, 25.0), the claw standoff pair (0x2DA77C, 0x2DA790), the wind approach limit (0x2DA808) and the pairs of attack-band limits at 0x2DA678, 0x2DA68C, 0x2DA704, 0x2DA718, 0x2DA754, 0x2DA768, 0x2DA7CC, 0x2DA7E0, 0x2DA8BC and 0x2DA8D0. Every user is a Draik handler (SPEC-0029, Draik behavior; Open question 50).

### 34. Death hook (High for the branches, the flyer test and the coin split)

Numbers in Behavior 34 to 46 are decimal (state numbers, animation numbers and tick counts). Row and object offsets are written in hex, as elsewhere in this spec.

When an enemy's health falls to 0 or below from a hit, the damage routine sets health to 0 and calls the monster's death hook with the enemy and two flags set to 1. The hook reads the enemy's monster row.

1. Reanimation test. Read the row's reanimation flag (0x15C).
   - Flag 0 (106 of the 109 class-17 rows): if the row's class id is not 1, 32 or 11, the base actor state (0x144) is set to 16 (dead) through the character class's base-state setter, which first copies the old base state to the previous-base-state word (object offset 0x7FC) when the two differ (High). The class state is not changed. The meaning of the class id test is not confirmed. The Undead Draik (class 12) rewrites base state 1 at once and then adds 1 hit point back, so its health reads 1 after its killing hit (SPEC-0029, Section 6.10).
   - Flag nonzero (rows 224, 226 and 227): if the reanimation countdown (object offset 0xD14) is at its idle value of -1, draw a whole number of ticks from the reanimation min (0x160) to the reanimation max (0x164), both included, store it as the countdown, and set the class state to 16 (Behavior 35). If the countdown is not -1, nothing changes. A second death while reanimating does not restart the countdown.
2. Grounding of a flyer. If the first flyer test passes (the row's can-fly value 0x94 is 1 and the retarget countdown, object offset 0xBF4, is 0 or less), draw a decimal number of ticks from the ground timer min (0x9C) up to, but not including, the ground timer max (0x98), cut down to a whole number, and store it as the retarget countdown. The maximum is therefore not reached. A positive countdown makes that test fail, so a can-fly 1 enemy counts as grounded from its death on. The think loop that would count the countdown down is not stepped for a dead enemy (Behavior 1), so it stays grounded as a corpse (High).
3. Death countdown: set once, in part C of step 6. Step 1 changes a state or sets a countdown only on a first death tick (a row with flag 0 keeps the reanimation countdown at its idle value -1, and a row with the flag sets it only from -1), so part C covers every such case with the same value.
4. There is no separate removal step. The helper that takes the enemy's position and the word at 0xC28 is the coin split of part D below, and 0xC28 is the carried Neopoints (Open questions 64 and 66, resolved).
5. All 14 status timers are cleared (SPEC-0040). This runs last, on every call.
6. Drop and payout. The payout runs only when the hook is called with its flag set, and only on the enemy's first death tick: the tick on which the base state was not 16 before the call, or the reanimation countdown was at its idle value -1. It reads the direct drop item (enemy object offset 0xA50; SPEC-0029, Layout section 1) and the drop placement ordinal (the signed 16-bit word at data offset 0xB0 of the enemy's spawn record, object offset 0xA44). The parts run in this order:
   - A. Direct item. If the direct drop item is 0 or more, spawn that item at the enemy's position and set the direct drop item to -1. Part B is then skipped.
   - B. Placement. Otherwise use the drop placement ordinal. It is valid only when it is in range of the enemy's region's placement list; an ordinal outside the list does nothing. For a valid ordinal, the placement's key decides:
     - Key 0 or more: spawn the item named by the key at the enemy's position. The fixed count is not used.
     - Key -1: if the placement's group is valid and its money roll succeeds (SPEC-0021), the money amount M is taken from the group's money range. If there is no money amount, M is the placement's fixed count, but only when that count is above 0. If M is above 0, split M into coins at the enemy's position (100, 25, 10, 5, 1) and stop. Otherwise roll the group's item (SPEC-0021) and spawn the item if one is given.
     - A group of -1 has no money roll and no item roll. Only the fixed count can pay coins.
   - C. Death countdown. Set the death disappear countdown (object offset 0xD24) to the row's death disappear timer (row offset 0x80), in ticks. This runs on every first tick, after A or B.
   - D. Carried Neopoints. Split the carried Neopoints (enemy object offset 0xC28) into coins at the enemy's position (100, 25, 10, 5, 1), then set the carried Neopoints to 0. This runs on every first tick, after A or B, whether or not A or B paid anything. If the carried value is 0, nothing spawns. The carried value grows only in the item steal branch of an attack (SPEC-0029, Behavior 11b). The Neopoint steal does not add to it.

The coins of part B and the coins of part D are separate payouts. A key -1 placement pays its own coins (or an item when it pays no coins), and the carried Neopoints are split on the same tick (Open question 79, answered).

The hook is also called with a flag of 0 on the reset path. Steps 1, 2 and 5 still run; parts A to D are replaced by setting the death countdown (0xD24) to 0 (High).

The coins of part D are all spawned at the enemy's position; the split adds no spacing between them (High).

Row values (resource 530, class 17, 109 rows). Death disappear timer values: 1 (17 rows), 4 (8), 5 (7), 6 (7), 10 (5), 60 (1), 180 (11), 200 (2), 240 (2), 300 (14), 320 (3), 360 (6), 420 (1), 510 (14), 600 (11). The reanimating rows are 224 (Skeleton Archer): countdown 3600 to 18000 ticks, disappear timer 320; 226 (Dumb Skeleton): countdown 8000 to 12000 ticks, disappear timer 240; 227 (Skeleton Fighter): countdown 12000 to 18000 ticks, disappear timer 240.

Confidence: High for the order of the payout parts A to D and their conditions, the flyer grounding and the base-state write; Medium-High for the money-roll details (SPEC-0021 rules). The reanimation ranges and the death disappear values are confirmed on disc.

### 35. Reanimating enemies: class state 16 (High for the steps; Medium-High for the rise rule)

State 16 is entered only through the death hook (Behavior 34) for rows with the reanimation flag. Its animations are 58 (lying), 153 (lying, while flying) and 85 (rise). In this state, "flying" is true when either flyer test passes: the row's can-fly value is 1 and the retarget countdown is 0 or less, or the can-fly value is 2. The death hook of Behavior 34 uses only the first.

While its class state is 16 the enemy is not a hit candidate: the hit filter of the character class refuses class state 16 (High). While the reanimation countdown is also above 0, the dead test of streaming counts it as dead, so its kill bit is set on every such tick (Behavior 47).

Each tick:
1. Countdown phase (reanimation countdown at object offset 0xD14, 0 or more): play 153 when the enemy is flying, otherwise 58. Subtract 1 from the countdown and stop.
2. Otherwise (countdown below 0): look up the rise animation 85 in the enemy's animation table.
   - If the enemy has no animation 85, it heals to full health at once and goes to agro (state 19).
   - If the rise is not the current animation: play 85 when the clear-area test passes (the hero is at least 5.0 units away, and no other live member of the shared character pool is closer than 5.0 units to the enemy; the enemy itself is skipped). Otherwise play 58 or 153 as in step 1 and wait.
   - If the rise is current and not finished: wait.
   - When the rise is finished (animation progress reaches 0.99): set health to its maximum (with the heal notification) and go to agro (state 19). Completion runs the agro setter, so the risen enemy broadcasts to its alarm group and has all cooldowns cleared. The reanimation countdown is left at -1, so a second death draws a new countdown and pays the drop again. A reanimating corpse stays bound to its object (its death-disappear countdown never runs) and counts toward the block's active cap until the streaming rules release it.

Confidence: High for the countdown, the missing-animation path and the clear-area rule (5.0 units to the hero and to every other pool member); Medium-High for the distance measure, which is the shared distance helper (Open question 40).

A reanimating enemy never enters base state 16, so the dead-state update below never runs for it and its death disappear countdown (set by part C of Behavior 34) never runs down. It is never released for being dead; only the distance and cell rules of streaming release it (SPEC-0006) (High).

### 36. Dead state and corpse removal, non-reanimating enemies (High)

When the base actor state is 16, the per-tick update of the enemy does this every tick:
1. Set the actor's sub-object flag to 1.
2. Play 153 when the enemy is flying (either flyer test of Behavior 35), otherwise 58. A can-fly 1 enemy was grounded by the death hook (Behavior 34, step 2), so only can-fly 2 rows play 153 as a corpse. The request is repeated every tick but does not restart the clip: the request store keeps the main id when it is unchanged, and the hookup starts a main clip only when its layer does not already hold it (SPEC-0040, Behavior 3D, step 4). The only path that forces a restart, the take-hit hook's force-start (Behavior 37, step 13), stops at step 2 for a dead enemy. So the death clip plays once from the tick of death (High; Open question 85, resolved).
3. Once the death clip reaches a progress of 0.95, the disappear countdown goes down by 1 each tick. The countdown (object offset 0xD24) started at the row's death disappear timer. This test looks up the action (58, or 153 for a flying corpse) in the character's own main table without the clip-0 fallback that the animation request uses: an action with no entry gives no clip, no playback record holds it, and the test is false on every tick. A character whose table lacks the corpse action (the Plague Serpent's 29-entry table has no action 58; SPEC-0016) therefore never counts down, never sinks and is never released as dead; it keeps looping clip 0 until something else removes it (High). A common enemy whose table lacks the action would behave the same way.
4. Sink. When the enemy is not flying and the countdown, after that tick's decrement, is below the death disappear timer minus int(half the tick rate) (30 ticks at 60 ticks per second), the corpse's world position moves down by 1/180 unit along world up (0, 1, 0), and the sink value at object offset 0xD28 grows by the same 1/180. So a corpse sinks 1 unit in 180 ticks (3 seconds) and keeps sinking until it is released. A flying corpse does not sink (Open question 63, resolved).
5. When the countdown is below 0, the target and pool searches treat the enemy as gone. It is not targetable and it drops out of the live count; streaming then releases it (SPEC-0006).

The think routine is not stepped for a dead enemy (Behavior 1), so its class state stays at its previous value.

Confidence: High for steps 1 to 5, including the single play of the death clip in step 2.

### 37. Take-hit hook (High for the order, state changes and first-hit damage multiplier)

The hook runs on every hit, after the damage is computed. In order:
1. Script hold. If the enemy's script-hold word (object offset 0xC54) is nonzero, stop. The word is set when a level script hands a character whose class id is 6 back to its own behaviour, and cleared when it hands back any other class; it is cleared at creation. The same word also keeps the character out of the hit filter. For a class-17 enemy it stays 0 (High).
2. If the base actor state is 16 (dead), stop.
3. Health bar. The enemy becomes the target of the single enemy name and health bar, shown for 10 seconds (SPEC-0023). Its name comes from the spawn record (up to 31 characters).
4. Grab release. If the enemy's grab phase word (0xC08) is 2 and the selected hero's base state is 53 (held), the hero's base state is set to 1 through its setter (High). The grab-and-throw machine (Behavior 15; SPEC-0029 Behavior 23) writes phase 2 at the moment it puts the hero in state 53, and phase 3 when it lets go normally. So any hit that reaches this step on the enemy that is holding the hero frees the hero at once. The hook does not change the phase word itself (Open question 84, resolved).
5. Ghost absorb. If the enemy's row is a ghost row (0x138 nonzero), the enemy is in its ghost phase (0xC38 is 0) and no reveal is running (0xC48 is 0), the hit is absorbed: no damage, no reaction, no doubling, no hit count and no alert flag. The reveal starts instead (0xC48 set to 1, reveal timer 0xC4C = a whole number of ticks from row 0x154 to row 0x158, both included) and the hook stops (SPEC-0029 Behavior 33; rows 143, 215 and 222; Open question 65, resolved).
6. The attacker id is stored at object offset 0x428. If the class-state word is not 19 (agro), the incoming damage value is doubled, the class state is set to 19 through the class-state setter, so its full entry work runs (Behavior 5: outer sub-state 0, defence hold 0, recovery countdown 0, combat timer, all four cooldowns 0, attack-manager entry; step 7 then writes outer sub-state 10), and the enemy is added to the attack-manager list (Behavior 45). This transition occurs before the status/extra-flag reaction gates and the undamageable damage gate in step 8. The doubling and the state transition happen whether or not the undamageable flag is set (the flag only skips the hit-point loss of step 8; R-0146, High); later hits then see state 19 and are not doubled. Thus the multiplier is tied to entering agro, not necessarily to the first damaging hit.
7. Hit reaction. This happens only if status timer 0 (stun, object offset 0x808) is zero or less and the caller's blocked argument (the extra flag) is zero. A blocked swing with no damage never calls the hook at all. The agro outer sub-state becomes 10 with a hit-reaction kind (Behavior 44):
   - Flying enemy (the row's can-fly value is not 0) with the retarget countdown below 1: clear the stored flag (0x8C0). Then by the row's flying hit value (0xAC): 1 gives kind 7; 0 gives kind 6; 2 gives kind 7 if the caller's second argument is nonzero, otherwise 6; any other value gives no reaction. A hit with flying hit value 1 therefore grounds a Can Fly 1 flyer for the row's ground timer range (kind 7, Behavior 44).
   - Otherwise: if the stored flag is 0, kind 0; if it is set, the flag is cleared and the kind is 5.
   A new reaction is ignored while outer 10 runs kind 2 with stun type 1 (Behavior 44).
8. The damage is applied only when the undamageable flag (+0x7F4; set by SetEntityUndamageable, otherwise 0) is zero. Example: from class state 0 with HP 20 and incoming hit amount 4, a clear flag causes entry to state 19 and subtracts 8 HP; with the flag set, the same first hit still enters state 19 but subtracts no HP, and a later hit amount 4 after the flag clears subtracts 4 because the enemy is already in state 19.
   - Hero hits that deal 0 (R-0146, R-0150; High). A hit reaches this routine only when the hero's damage figure is above 0 or was absorbed (SPEC-0036, Damage), so a swing whose figure truncates to 0 makes no call: no reaction, no alert state, no hit count. An undamageable enemy is the opposite case: the figure is normal (for example 40 from the Altadorian Sword, 7 from the Squire's Sword, defence 0), the reaction and alert state run, and only the hit-point loss is skipped. In pack 1398 the five Drackonacks and Giant Drackonacks of region 1399 are held in that state for the whole time the module runs (SPEC-0043, 1398), so a build that logs the hit-point change shows 0 and hit points stay at 55 or 300; the same kinds in every other pack lose hit points normally (a Giant Drackonack of pack 714 takes 40 per plain swing of sword 105). The player is not meant to kill them.
9. A separate countdown (+0xE2C) is set to 90 ticks and counted down each tick; nothing in the class-17 code reads it, so it gates no damage.
10. If the enemy's own base actor state (0x144) is 25, the base actor state is set to 1 (High).
11. Face the attacker. The enemy is turned toward the attacker with weight 1.0 through the character's aim method. The attacker is found from its id: 0 or 1 is the selected hero; 2 is the second hero when that slot holds a live character, otherwise the selected hero; 3 and up index the list of extra attackers in order (3 is its first entry), falling back to the selected hero when the index is past its end (Medium-High).
12. The hit counter (object offset 0xA2C) goes up by 1. When the hit-count threshold (object offset 0xA30) is not -1 and the count reaches it, the enemy's invulnerable word (0x7F0) is set to 1, and from then on the hit filter refuses it. Creation sets the threshold to -1 and nothing else in the boot code writes it, so in play the threshold is never reached (High; Open question 76, resolved). The same invulnerable word is set and cleared by a level-script command and by some bosses.
13. If status timer 0 (stun) is zero or less, the enemy's force-start word is set (SPEC-0040, Behavior 3D, step 3): on the next animation hookup the requested clips start again from their first key even when the same clips are already playing. So an unstunned enemy that is hit while its requested clip plays sees that clip begin again (High).
14. The alert flag (+0xABC) is set to 1. The pre-step consumes it (Behavior 2, step 3).
15. If the row's anger level (0x134) is nonzero, the anger counter (+0xC20) goes up by 1. The anger counter feeds the anger rule of Behavior 7, step 5d (threshold int(anger counter x 0.05)), and the normal choice clears it when it picks such a slot.

Confidence: High for the order and the state changes; Medium for the doubling reading; Medium-High for the attacker lookup of step 11.

### 38. State 14, retreat home and hide (High for the entry; Medium-High for the phases)

Entered only by the distance-hide gate (Behavior 24). The state uses a phase word at 0xC04.
- Phase 0: play 81 until it finishes, then go to phase 1.
- Phase 1: walk home with animation 125. The walk-home target is the home point, and the walker requests the idle pick (animation 0) when it is within 0.25 of its target. The test "animation 0 is current" below is true on the tick after that request, so an enemy that is already home leaves phase 1 at once: a Tentacle already at home goes from the sink straight to the rise on the first or second tick. When no route to the hero exists and the enemy is within 3.0 units of its home point, it enters state 8 hiding (Behavior 40), and on that tick its position is set to its home point (a jump of up to 3.0 units; Medium-High). When the hero is within 5.0 units (and a route to the enemy's own position exists), or animation 0 is current, it plays 82 and goes to phase 2.
- Phase 2: when 82 has finished: if the hero is within 20.0 units, go to agro (state 19); otherwise run the setter with 0.

### 39. State 17, map search (Medium; arrival tests Medium)

Entry: the map search of Behavior 9 runs each tick when the row's map search flag (0x11C) is nonzero and its cooldown is zero. The chosen target is an object index.

Each tick:
1. If the hero is closer than the row's greedy hostile range (0x168), go to the default state (step 6).
2. Type 1 target: look up the object by its index. If it is missing, reset (step 6). Otherwise let d be the distance from the enemy to the object.
   - If d is below 1.0 (arrival): if the object's type word is below 0xBF or its subtype is 0x11, consume it, setting its consume flag to 4 for a type 1 target (not confirmed). Clear the target index and play the idle pick (Behavior 8).
   - Otherwise steer toward the object and play the locomotion pick.
3. Type 0 target: the same as type 1, except that the consume flag is 0 (not confirmed).
4. Other types: do nothing.
5. After steering or arrival, if the target index is still set, stay in the state. Otherwise reset.
6. Reset: the default state, with the target index and the object index both set to -1.

Rows with a map search type: type 0 on rows 156, 157, 158, 231 and 232 (rows 156 to 158 have chance 0.0 and greedy range 0.0, so their roll never passes and the search is inert; rows 231 and 232 have chance 50, search distance 100 and greedy range 10); type 1 on rows 152 and 153; types 2 to 8 on rows 225 (type 2), 197 (3), 198 (4), 201 (5), 199 (6), 216 and 217 (7), and 213 (8).

Confidence: High for the rows; Medium for the arrival and consume tests.

### 40. States 8 and 9, emerge and approach a named target (High for state 8; Medium for state 9)

State 8 pre-step (sub-state word at 0xBC0). Each sub-state runs on its own tick: the distance test and sub 1 on one tick, the emerge request and sub 2 on the next, the gate test from the tick after.
- Sub 0: if the active hero's horizontal distance (height ignored) is strictly below the spawn record's f32 at 0xC0 times F1 (Behavior 18), set sub 1. Emerge distance 0 never passes; see the wake routes below. F1 is looked up from the hero's base state; an idle hero (state 1) gives 0.7, so an emerge distance of 40.0 becomes 28.0.
- Sub 1: the emerge animation: 165 for flyers; otherwise by the spawn record word at 0xC8: 0 gives 10, 1 to 4 give 133 to 136, any other value gives 10 (Behavior 20). If the animation is missing, go to agro (19). Otherwise play it and set sub 2.
- Sub 2: when the animation has finished, go to agro (19).

State 8 handler (sub 0): if the enemy is within 0.35 units of its home point, or the row's distance hide flag (0xA0) is nonzero, it stands at home playing the hide pick (9, or 128 to 131 by the same selector word) and stops steering. Otherwise it walks home (gait 0).

State 9 handler: the target is the pool entry whose id is at +0xBC of the spawn record. The followed id is the record ordinal inside a region; the lookup scans the embedded block, then the slot regions in order, and takes the first match. If it is missing, the state-5 wander body runs in place each tick and the class state stays 9 (so the notice check of states 3 and 9 still applies and following resumes when the character reappears). Otherwise:
- Copy the target's gait number into its own gait, steer to the target's position and take a walk step. The gait number is copied unchanged for a ground row (a flying row remaps it, which no follower on disc uses). Entering state 9 loads the gait words: gait 0 plays animation 11, gait 2 plays animation 21, and gaits 1, 3, 4, 5 and 6 have no animation (id -1). The walk step plays the word of the current gait every tick. The gait numbers are the movement classes of the followed character: 0 walk, 1 run, 2 fast run (the move type set by SetAI_MovementType, SPEC-0056). High.
- If the distance is at least the spawn record's +0xC0 value, keep walking.
- Else, if +0xC4 is zero, play the idle pick and stop.
- Else, if the distance is below 6.0, play animation 34 and count the timer at +0xB10 down from 60 ticks.
- Otherwise walk toward the target.

On disc only the three Cogham Village Spearmen (region 843, record ordinals 4, 6 and 8) use state 9; they follow the characters with ids 3, 5 and 7 (Rose, Gershwin, Giovanni the Explorer) with stay distance 5.0 and +0xC4 = 0. The followed villagers patrol at fast run (19.30 units per second; move type 2, set once by the pack's slot thread), so the gait copied from them is gait 2, the fast-run gait (SPEC-0056). A Spearman therefore plays animation 21 while it follows (not 11): its clip is the 7-key looping run clip (the same one as its forward strafe), root travel 0.3334 units per tick, 20.00 units per second at play rate 1. The villagers run at 19.30, so a Spearman gains about 0.7 units per second on its leader and closes to the stay distance. Gait 0 (animation 11) would be its 5.00 units per second walk, 0.0833 per tick. Test vector: Spearman at distance 30 from Rose (move type 2, no status): animation 21, 0.3334 per tick toward her until the distance is below 5.0, then the idle pick (the +0xC4 = 0 branch). Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): a copied gait whose word is -1 (a run-gait villager, not on disc) is treated as a missing animation id.

The cue word (spawn record +0xC4) of a waiting state-8 enemy also decides whether it can be hit or drawn while it waits: the enemy is hidden (not drawn, not hittable, not auto-targetable) while its emerge sub-state is 0, the cue word is 0 and its current animation is the hide pick; with cue word 1 it is drawn and hittable while it hides, and a hit wakes it with doubled damage (SPEC-0036, SPEC-0046). The same rule hides a Display-in-default-1 enemy in state 14 at phase 1 (the two Tentacles, the Slorg and the Giant Slorg in their default state 8 are examples). The cue word is not only the switch for the shared stinger 4 sound when the group alarm wakes the enemy and the word is 0 (SPEC-0009, Stinger and emote-clip playback); no camera is started, and a wake by distance never starts it.

**Every way out of state 8 (R-0147; High).** A waiting enemy leaves state 8 only by one of these six routes. Nothing else writes another state to it: the notice check of Behavior 3 does not run in state 8, the level scripts have no other call that changes an enemy's class state, and the socialize alert (Behavior 10) is run only by enemies in states 5, 6 and 7 but is aimed at other enemies and does not exclude a peer in state 8 (route 6). An enemy that is hidden in state 8 itself never starts a chat.
1. **The emerge distance test** (sub-state 0, every tick the enemy runs). The distance is horizontal: the height difference is dropped, so a hero on a ledge directly above or below counts as standing on the spot. It is measured from the enemy's current position (its home point while it waits) to the active hero only; the partner hero is ignored. The comparison is strict: distance below the spawn record's float at 0xC0 times F1 (Behavior 18). Consequences: an emerge distance of 0 can never pass (a distance is never below 0), whatever F1 is, even with the hero at the same point. An emerge distance of 1.0 passes only when the hero is within F1 horizontal units: 0.3 to 1.75 depending on the hero's action state (0.7 idle, 0.75 slow walk, 1.5 walk, 1.75 run, 1.0 in any state not listed in Behavior 18). Because a hidden enemy is not solid (SPEC-0033, bodies pushing the hero: hidden characters are skipped), the hero can walk onto it, but passing a metre or two to the side leaves it hidden. A hero in the idle state therefore has to stand within 0.7 units of a 1.0 placement. The test does not check the hero's line of sight, the zone, the hero's tattered cloak or any chance roll. It runs only while the enemy is bound and its zone is active.
2. **The group alarm** (Behavior 3): the enemy plays animation 10 and goes to agro through sub-states 2 and the finish gate, whatever the emerge distance and the hero's distance. This is the only distance-independent wake into agro by itself (route 6 leaves hiding without entering agro).
