3. **A script call of SetAI_Agro** (SPEC-0030 row 115): this puts the enemy into state 2, not into agro, and requests animation 18; it does not run the emerge animation. From state 2 the enemy still has to pass its own notice check (Behavior 3) to reach agro; if it does not, it plays the lose-interest animation and goes to its default state, which for a placement with default-state word 8 is state 8 again (hidden at home). For a Display-in-default 0 row the state is 21 unless DisableInvisibility was called for the entity. On disc the call is made only by the modules of packs 678, 735, 757, 772, 800, 822, 886, 956, 1081, 1122, 1217, 1247, 1257, 1393, 1397 and 1414 (SPEC-0030 row 115); the placements listed below belong to none of them except 1081.
4. **Damage** (Behavior 37, step 6): a hit on an enemy that is not in state 19 doubles the damage and sets state 19. A hit can only land when the hittable predicate passes (SPEC-0036): a waiting enemy with cue word 0 (record word 0xC4) is hidden by the invisible-kind rule, so no swing, shot, Mote object or auto-target reaches it; with cue word 1 it is drawn and hittable. Every example placement below has cue word 0 except pack 1074 entity 11.
5. **The retreat route back to state 8** does not count as a wake: an enemy that has returned to state 8 starts again at sub-state 0.
6. **The socialize pull** (R-0184; High): an enemy of a row with the socialize flag that is in state 5, 6 or 7 and whose quiet countdown has run out scans the active character list; a hidden enemy in state 8 is a valid peer, because the peer test excludes only states 1, 2, 3, 19 and 21 (Behavior 10). The scan sends the first peer that passes (socialize flag, quiet countdown below 1, same target and mesh, separation at most the sum of the two pair values) to state 3 by the setter, which writes the state whatever the old state was. The pulled enemy leaves hiding without playing the emerge animation, does not enter agro, does not post an alarm, and its emerge distance, cue word and group play no part. When the chat ends it runs the setter with 0 and the pre-step returns it to state 8 (default word 8) at sub-state 0, where it walks home if it is more than 0.35 from home and hides again. Only rows with the flag can be pulled: the Bandit, Thief, Rogue and Thug family, the six Ixi rows, the Minion Warrior rows, the Skeleton Archer, Skull Spirit, Spyder and Werelupe rows listed in Behavior 10. Of the twelve placements below, none is a flagged kind.

**Placements with emerge distance 0 or 1.0 (disc; R-0147).** Of the 883 placements on disc with default-state word 8 and a class-17 kind, 60 have an emerge distance of 1.0 or less (42 at 0, 18 at 1.0), all with cue word 0 except one. Of these, 46 belong to an alarm group that has at least one member which does not wait in state 8 or has an emerge distance above 1.0, so the group alarm wakes them. Two (pack 1096 entities 1 and 3) belong to a group whose only members are those two. Twelve have no group; they wake only by the hero standing on them (emerge 1.0: the four Tentacles below) or never (emerge 0). A placement with emerge distance 0, no group, no script call and cue word 0 is an invisible, untouchable, non-solid decoration for the whole game: it never draws, never blocks, cannot be hit and never acts. The twelve: Skeleton Fighter, pack 906 entity 2; Slorg, entity 0 of the character region stored as resource 1271; the Restless Spirits and Spirit Swords of pack 1081 (entities 5, 7, 8, 9, 10 and 11; this pack's script calls SetAI_Agro only for entities 12 to 14); the Tentacles with emerge 1.0 and no group, entities 3 and 8 of pack 1129 and the same two of its never-entered twin, pack 1077 (SPEC-0066).

Worked examples (entity numbers are the pack's entity-id table, counted from 0; the numbering of a duel trace that counts from 1 for some packs shifts by one):
- Pack 1350: Minion Brutes entity 9 (group 7) and entity 15 (group 9), emerge 0, cue 0. Group 7 also holds a Brute (entity 8, emerge 0) and a Minion Magician (entity 10, emerge 10.0); group 9 holds a Brute (14, emerge 0) and a Magician (16, emerge 20.0). The Brutes wake when their Magician emerges, 0 to 45 ticks after the Magician enters agro (Behavior 3). Standing beside a Brute does nothing.
- Pack 796: Spyders entity 2 and 3, group 0, emerge 0. The group's third member is the Giant Spyder entity 1, in state 6 (patrol): it wakes the two by noticing the hero in the usual way and entering agro. Entities 26 (group 2) and 29 (group 4) have an emerge-0 Spyder and an emerge-10 Spyder as partners.
- Pack 1096: Slorgs entities 1 and 3, group 1, emerge 1.0, cue 0: a pair that wakes only when the hero stands within F1 units (0.7 idle) of either; the other wakes by the alarm. The entities written 3 and 4 in the request are entities 1 and 3 here (entity 3 is the second Slorg; entity 4 is a patrolling Slorg).
- Pack 1077 (never entered, SPEC-0066): Tentacles with emerge 1.0 and no group, entities 3 and 8 (entity 4 is a Slorg in state 7). Its twin 1129 holds the same records and is the one that plays.
- Pack 906: 18 entities. Only entity 2 has emerge 0; the other waiting Fighters (entities 3, 7, 13) have emerge 20.0, 20.0 and 15.0. The entity numbers 26, 46, 58, 79 and 99 of the request are not in this pack; no Skeleton Fighter with emerge 0 and no group exists outside entity 2 (the Fighters with emerge 0 elsewhere are in pack 1249, groups 3 and 6).
Default if unresolved (rewrite choice): when a port meets a waiting placement with emerge distance 0, no group and cue word 0, create no drawn body and no collision for it and let nothing wake it.

Confidence: High for the state 8 pre-step, the animation choice and the handler; Medium for the state 9 timer.

### 41. States 20, 22 and 23 (High)

- State 20: play the idle pick (Behavior 8).
- State 22: query animation 75. If refused, go to the default state. If it is not current, play it. When it finishes, go to the default state.
- State 23: query animation 81. If refused, go to the default-state value of the second block. If it is not current, play it. When it finishes, go to that same default-state value.

### 42. Attack execution, state 18 (High for the flow; Medium for the hit event and the aim)

Entry: only from the map search per tick (Behavior 9), on two paths. The agro attack choices (Behaviors 7 and 14) never enter state 18. Each tick:
1. If animation 137 is not current, play it.
2. If it is current and not finished, aim at the target (the pool entry for the target id) with blend 0.1. When animation event 32 fires and the sub-type fired flag is 0, set the flag and run the attack sub-type (Behavior 43).
3. When the animation finishes, set the recovery countdown to a whole number of ticks drawn from the row's attack timer min (0x68) to its max (0x64), both included, and go to agro (state 19).

The state can start from any class state, including 19 and 8 and the middle of an attack sequence (an unfinished sequence is abandoned). The spell fires on the first frame carrying event bit 0x20. At the clip's gate the recovery countdown is drawn and the agro setter runs with its full entry work (alarm broadcast of the group, outer sub-state 0, defence hold 0, all four slot cooldowns 0, attack-manager entry), so a caster becomes hostile after any cast, whether or not it noticed the hero. A hit during state 18 takes the non-agro path of the take-hit hook (damage doubled, agro). A row whose chance and range are both 0 never casts.

This path runs the map-search spells only; every attack slot, melee or missile, runs through the attack sequencer of Behavior 13. Confidence: High for the entry and steps 1 and 3; Medium for the blend and the event number.

### 43. Attack sub-types 0 to 6: spells (High)

The sub-type is chosen by the attacker's row map search type (the type that the search of Behavior 9 found). Each sub-type looks up its target: the enemy's current target id (the pool entry of the target id). Every sub-type first checks the target: if the target is missing, nothing is spawned and no damage is dealt. The candidate picker scans other characters in the active region list and takes five arguments: an animation id to exclude (-1 for none), a flag requiring current HP below maximum, a flag applying the candidate filter (not hidden, not script-held, not a petpet, not one of a fixed list of character keys), and a flag requiring a positive reanimation countdown. Type 2 (row 225) requires the positive reanimation countdown only. Type 3 (row 197) excludes characters playing animation 4 and applies the filter. Type 4 (row 198) excludes animation 11 and applies the filter. Type 5 (row 201) excludes animation 5 and applies the filter. Type 6 (row 199) requires HP below maximum and applies the filter. Type 7 (rows 216 and 217) excludes animation 0, applies the filter and runs only while the caster itself is wounded (no chance roll). Type 8 (row 213) applies the filter only. Candidates must be within the row's map-search distance (+0x120) with a clear sight line; one of at most ten is chosen at random.

Each sub-type submits one spell projectile to the enemy projectile pool (SPEC-0029 Behavior 9):

- launch point: the enemy's emit point (the same method as the missile launch point of Behavior 13: attachment frame 0 unless the clip frame's window bits 0x1 to 0x10 choose another); aim point: the target's position + (0, 1.5, 0);
- velocity: unit(aim - launch) x 1.0, so a spell flies at 1.0 unit per tick;
- lifetime: trunc(distance from the launch point to the aim point) + 5 ticks, so the spell reaches its aim point before it expires;
- damage word 3.0 (unused: spell kinds deal no damage) and radius 1.0;
- visual kind 23 (the second constant of every builder);
- effect ids: the table's first word (0x848 to 0x84E), then -1, 412, -1, 412, 412;
- owner: the enemy; element: the row's 0x3C value; area duration and radius: the slot's values at 0x44C + 4k and 0x45C + 4k;
- the target id is stored in the slot's target field, and the spell touches only that character.

| Sub-type | Map search type that selects it (row) | Projectile kind | First effect id | Effect on contact with the target (SPEC-0029 Behavior 9) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | 2 (row 225) | 2 | 0x848 | Sets the target's word +0xD14 (the reanimation countdown, Layout section 1) to -1 |
| 1 | 3 (row 197) | 3 | 0x849 | Applies status 4 (strength) |
| 2 | 4 (row 198) | 7 | 0x84E | Applies status 11 |
| 3 | 5 (row 201) | 4 | 0x84A | Applies status 5 (speed) |
| 4 | 6 (row 199) | 5 | 0x84B | Sets the target's HP to its maximum |
| 5 | 7 (rows 216, 217) | 5 | 0x84C | On spell contact, sets the target's HP to its maximum. At build time, if a projectile slot is free, the build step deals the caster's missing HP (maximum minus current) to the target as plain damage and submits the spell, and the spell's contact then sets the target's HP to its maximum; the caster's own HP is not changed. If the pool is full, neither damage nor projectile occurs. |
| 6 | 8 (row 213, Minion Wizard) | 6 | 0x84D | Re-picks the target's variant and monster row (SPEC-0029 Edge cases, run-time variant re-pick) |

Sub-type 6 and its target (R-0185; High). The contact step does not test the kind, class or row of the character it reaches: it always changes the variant and reselects the monster row, then loads the colour preset, so on any character it changes the row as well as the colours (the row is what a later spawn-default request reads, SPEC-0029 run-time variant re-pick). The only limit is who can be the target. The Wizard's picker applies the candidate filter, which rejects a character that is hidden, script-held, a petpet, one of the fixed list of placement ids, or flagged as townsfolk (the flag the character gets when its class is 6, which every villager row has; SPEC-0036 hittable predicate). A villager is therefore never a target and the re-pick never reaches one in play. Non-townsfolk characters that are not common enemies (Whinny, Mortog, Miamouse, Target Dummy) pass the filter and would have their row and variant changed. Rewrite rule: apply the full re-pick (variant, row, colours) to whatever character the spell reaches, and keep townsfolk out of the Wizard's candidate set.

Map search type 7 selects sub-type 5 directly (Behavior 39 and Behavior 9). The spell releases its slot on contact with its target, or on ground or world contact (SPEC-0029 Behavior 9). The slot's projectile type field (0x43C + 4k) is not an input of the spell builders; it is the visual kind of the missile spawners of Behavior 13.

Confidence: High for the table (confirmed on disc), the speed, the aim, the lifetime, the contact effects and the type-2 candidate predicates; Medium for the link of kind 2's word to the reanimation countdown.

### 44. Agro machine, class state 19 (Medium-High; High for the writers of the outer sub-state)

Words (object offsets): outer sub-state 0xAF8, inner sub-state 0xB30, sequencer state 0xB2C, hold timer 0xB34, strafe lock 0xCB0, route flag 0xAFC, desired distance 0xE40, retreat distance 0xE44, recovery countdown 0xB18, defence hold 0xAB8, hit-reaction kind 0xB28. d = horizontal distance to the hero; dir = horizontal unit vector from the enemy to the hero (y dropped before measuring; Behavior 13; Medium-High for every use in this machine). "Gait n" plays word n of the current gait animation set (gaits 5 and 6 are swapped in the lookup: gait 5 plays word 6 and gait 6 plays word 5).

Each agro tick, in order:

1. The recovery countdown goes down by one when positive.
2. If the base actor state is 20 (blocking) and the outer sub-state is neither 7 nor 8, the base actor state is set to 1.
3. Reactions and choices, in this order: missile defence, melee defence, lost-sight test, normal attack choice (Behavior 7), counter-attack choice (Behavior 14), distance-hide gate (Behavior 24).
4. The outer sub-state switch (below).
5. The agro tail: route flag and leash (Behavior 23).

Then the class-19 handler runs: the defence hold goes down by one; outer 0 plays the idle pick; the hold inner sub-states of outers 1, 3 and 4 face the hero (turn speed row 0x84, flyers row 0x88) and play their hold animation; the moving inner sub-states, outers 9 and 11, and outer 12 while it walks run the pursuit step (SPEC-0029 Behavior 25), which plays the gait animation.

**Outer sub-state entry (High).** One entry routine takes (event, parameter), does the entry work and stores the outer sub-state the event names: event 1 rush (inner 1, gait set 21, 21, 21, 23, 55; flyer set 101, 102); event 3 keep distance (inner 4, set 24, 24, 24, 26, 55; flyers 107, 108); event 4 strafe (inner 10, hold 0, set 28, 28, 28, 32, 55, 30, 29; flyers 103 to 106); event 2 attack (sequencer state = parameter, hit list cleared), or the wait in line instead (outer 12, set 2, flyer 98) when the attack manager says wait and the approach routine is not 3 (Behavior 45); event 10 hit reaction (kind = parameter; Hit reactions below); event 11 confused (set 69, flyer 69), ignored in outer 2. The flyer-set words are written only when row Can fly (0x94) is non-zero. This is not a hero routine: the hero has no action-state event switch (SPEC-0036).

**Outer sub-states.**
- 0 (set up): outer = the row's approach routine (0x38: 1, 3 or 4 on disc; any other value is kept as the outer sub-state, which has no case, so the enemy never moves by archetype or attacks by the normal choice); route flag = 1; play the idle pick. In agro the idle pick is id 141 for a flying enemy or a Can Fly 2 row and id 1 otherwise (outside agro it is 0, or 97 for an airborne Can Fly 1 flyer, Behavior 8); a table with no entry for the id plays clip 0 of its main set.
- 1 (rush; entered with inner 1 and gait set 21, 21, 21, 23, 55): if the route flag is 0: gait 0 and steer to the hero; inner = 1 when within 1.0 unit of the current walk waypoint, otherwise 0. With a route: inner 0: gait 2 when d > 2 x row 0x4E0, else gait 0; steer to the hero; d < desired gives inner 1. Inner 1 (hold, faces the hero, animation 22, flyers 155): d < row 0x4DC gives inner 2; d > row 0x4E0 gives inner 0. Inner 2 (back off): gait 3, steer to the point hero - dir x (row 0x4E0 + 2.0); d > desired gives inner 1.
- 3 (keep distance; entered with inner 4 and gait set 24, 24, 24, 26, 55): with no route, as outer 1 but with inner 4 near the waypoint and 3 otherwise. Inner 4 (hold, aim blend 0.25, animation 25, flyers 156): d > row 0x4E8 gives 3; else d < row 0x4E4 gives 5; else d < row 0x4EC gives 6. In the same tick the class handler aims again at the row's hold turn weight (row 0x84; row 0x88 for a flyer), after the 0.25 blend, so the effective blend is 1 - 0.75 x (1 - w): 0.78 for w = 0.7 and 1.0 for w = 1.0. When the retreat minimum (row 0x4EC) is not below the desired maximum (row 0x4E8), as on row 220 (both 20.0), the hold sends the enemy to retreat for every distance from the desired minimum up to the retreat minimum, and the walk-in (inner 3) sends it to retreat as soon as d falls below the retreat minimum, so the enemy paces between the retreat minimum and its retreat distance. Inner 3: gait 0, steer to the hero; d < desired gives 4; then d < row 0x4EC gives 6. Inner 5: gait 3, steer to hero - dir x row 0x4E8; d > desired gives 4; else d < row 0x4EC gives 6. Inner 6 (retreat): gait 2, steer to hero - dir x (row 0x4F0 + 2.0); d at least the retreat distance gives 4.
- 4 (strafe; entered with inner 10, hold 0 and gait set 28, 28, 28, 32, 55, 30, 29 as words 0 to 6, so with the swap above gait 5 plays 29 and gait 6 plays 30): the strafe lock goes down by one when positive. With no route, inner 10 near the waypoint and 7 otherwise. Inner 7: gait 0, steer to the hero; d < desired gives hold 0 and inner 10. Inners 8 and 9 (circle): gait 6 or 5, steer to own position + p x (-2.0 for 8, +2.0 for 9), where p = normalize(dir x (0, 1, 0)), the enemy's right as it faces the hero; the steering point only turns the body to face the hero, and the side clip then carries it the other way, so inner 8 travels along +p (to its right) and inner 9 along -p (to its left) (Behavior 51, item 7; Open question 82); the hold goes down by one and below 1 gives inner 10; the blocked flag (0xCC4) gives inner 10 and a strafe lock of 120; then d > row 0x4F8 gives 7, else d < row 0x4F4 with the lock below 1 gives 11. Inner 10 (hold, faces the hero, animation 31, flyers 132): the hold goes down by one; while it is still positive, the distance tests of inner 8 apply; otherwise r = int[1, 3]: 1 gives 10, 2 gives 8 (10 while locked), 3 gives 9 (10 while locked), the hold = int[60, 180], and then the distance tests run (they win). Inner 11 (back off): gait 3, steer to hero - dir x row 0x4F8; when d > desired or the enemy is blocked (blocked also sets the lock to 120): hold 0, inner 10.
- 2: the attack sequencer (Behavior 13).
- 5 and 7: melee dodge and melee block; 6 and 8: missile dodge and missile block (below).
- 9 (search after lost sight): while the sight line is blocked: the search point is the last-seen point (plus dir x row 0x4E4 for approach 3, plus dir x row 0x4F4 for approach 4). Within 0.5 units of the point the search counter (0xB54) goes up by one; otherwise gait 2, steer there, and the counter is set to 0 when a route exists, else it goes up by one. A counter above 120 runs the setter with 0. When the sight line is clear: outer 0.
- 10: hit reactions (below).
- 11 (confused; status 6 active, set by the enemy status tick, SPEC-0029 Behavior 27): the grounded countdown word (0xBF4) is set to -1 on every tick (so a flyer's grounded countdown cannot run while confused); the enemy aims at a random horizontal direction (x and z each float[-1, 1), normalized) with blend 0.05 (data word 0x2DD540) and plays 69; when d is at most own radius + hero radius + 0.25, the normal attack choice runs. When the status has ended: outer 0.
- 12: wait in line (Behavior 45).

**Melee defence (outers 5 and 7).** Not in outer 10, and not in outer 2 while the sequencer is in a state other than 0, 1 and 5. It is latched (latch word 0xB88): the latch is set whenever the hero swing predicate is true and the defence hold is below 1, even if the reach or arc test fails, and stays set until the swing ends, so it is evaluated once per swing. The hero's swing query is true while Tor is in action state 13 with attack sub-state 2 to 12, or in jump states 7 to 11 in air-attack phase 8 or 9, and while Roberta is in state 13 with sub-state 5 or 12. The enemy evaluates on the first tick the query is true, before the swing's active window opens. It acts when the defence hold is below 1, the enemy is within the hero's reach and inside the hero's facing arc. Two rolls r1, r2 = int[1, 100]. By the hero's swing class (quick, normal, heavy; SPEC-0036) the block chance is row 0xEC, 0xF0 or 0xF4 and the dodge chance row 0xF8, 0xFC or 0x100. r1 at most the block chance gives outer 7; otherwise r2 below the dodge chance gives outer 5. On either, the defence hold = row 0xE0. Outer 5 (dodge): animation 55 (flyers 140) to its end, then the defence exit. A dodge id the table lacks (the Death Knight lacks 138, 139 and 140) plays clip 0 of the main set and the state lasts until that clip's gate (79 ticks for the Death Knight). Outer 7 (block): while the hero swings, face the hero, play the block 56 (flyers 150) and set base state 20; a pending block-hit flag plays 126 (flyers 151) once; when the swing is over, the defence exit.

Swing classes and gates (R-0017, High for the mapping and the order; Medium for the reach and arc meanings). The hero's swing class comes from the hero's attack sub-state word (+0xF90) and its variant word (+0xF80: 0 Tor, 1 Roberta). For Tor, sub-states 5 and 6 are quick (class 0), 2, 3 and 9 normal (class 1), 4, 7, 8 and 12 heavy (class 2); sub-state 10 and every other value have no class and never block or dodge. For Roberta, sub-states 5, 6, 7 and 10 are quick, 2, 3, 8, 11 and 12 normal, 4 heavy, and 9 and 14 have no class. A class maps to its block and dodge rows: quick 0xEC and 0xF8, normal 0xF0 and 0xFC, heavy 0xF4 and 0x100. "Within the hero's reach" is the distance between the enemy and the hero (the offset routine of the hero query) being below the hero object's float at +0x14B8. The facing arc is the dot product of the hero's facing with the negated offset, which must exceed the hero object's float at +0x14B4. Both floats are set when the hero object is initialised (spawn and every respawn) and nothing else writes them in play: the reach is 8.0 units and the arc cosine is 0.5, a half-angle of 60 degrees, the same for Tor and Roberta and for every swing class (High for the values and the writer; Medium for the exact meaning of the offset in the dot product). Test vector: an enemy 7.9 units away whose negated offset has a dot of 0.51 with the hero's facing can block or dodge; at 8.0 units, or at a dot of 0.5, it cannot.

**Missile defence (outers 6 and 8).** Not in outer 10, not in outer 2 with the sequencer in state 5, and only when the defence hold is below 1. For each live hero shot (up to 6): the path runs from the shot's position to its position + velocity x row 0xE0 (the look-ahead in ticks). If the path meets the enemy's body capsule: int[1, 100] < row 0xE4 gives outer 6, then int[1, 100] < row 0xE8 gives outer 8 (the later one wins), and the defence hold = row 0xE0. Outer 6 (dodge) repeats the shot-path test on every tick it runs (the same look-ahead path against the body, over up to 6 shots). While neither clip 53 nor clip 54 (flyers 138, 139) is the current animation: with a shot meeting the body it plays one of them by int[0, 1]; with none it takes the defence exit at once. While one of them is current it waits for the clip to end and then takes the defence exit (High). Outer 8 (block): for each live hero shot it turns toward the shot's position with the aim-turn weight, plays the block 56 (flyers 150) and sets base state 20; when none is live, the defence exit.

Defence exit: outer 0, base state 1, defence hold = int[int(row 0x104), int(row 0x108)].

**Defence cycle and give-up of the keep-away kinds (R-0149; High for the mechanism, Medium for the play reading).**
- No timer ends the keep-away. The keep-distance machine (outer 3 and its inner 3 to 6) changes inner state only by comparing d with the row's distances. It has no tick counter, no cornered test and no wall test: the blocked flag (0xCC4) is read only by the strafe machine (outer 4), and the leash counter of Behavior 23 is the only counter it shares. The enemy never walks toward the hero to melee and never "gives up and closes in". A wall, a navmesh border or a ledge only stops the body (Behavior 51, items 12 and 13): a cornered enemy stays pinned in its back-off (inner 5) or retreat (inner 6) state, d stays small, and it keeps choosing attacks from the slots whose bands contain d (Behavior 7 runs in outer 3 in every inner state). A keep-away kind with no slot band at short range (the Minion Artillery: both slots start at 15) is harmless when the hero is within that band, and is still not hurt-proof.
- The ways an enemy in agro does leave it: the leash (Behavior 23: no route to the hero and the walk waypoint 15.0 or more from the hero for more than 180 ticks, which happens when the hero stands on a navmesh the enemy cannot route to); the lost-sight search (outer 9: the hero out of sight, the enemy walks to the last-seen point and leaves agro 121 ticks after it arrives); the distance-hide gate (outer 1 only, never for these kinds); death.
- The dodge is rate-limited, not endless. A defence (outer 5, 6, 7 or 8) is evaluated only while the defence hold is below 1. A dodge sets the hold to row 0xE0, the clip plays (the enemy cannot be hit while it plays: SPEC-0036, hittable predicate, test 2), and the exit then sets the hold to int[row 0x104, row 0x108]; the hold falls by 1 per tick. So after every dodge there is an open window of that many ticks (0 to 60 for the Minion Wizard rows, 60 to 120 for the Minion Artillery, Slorg and Giant Slorg, 30 to 120 for the Magician and Skeleton Archer, 30 to 60 for the Skeleton Wizard) in which every swing and shot lands. A melee dodge needs the hero within 8.0 units and the enemy inside the hero's 60 degree front arc, is decided once per swing, and is impossible while the enemy runs the attack sequencer in a state other than 0, 1 and 5 (strike, follow-through and blocked states, and charges); a missile dodge is impossible only in recovery (sequencer state 5). The missile roll has no latch: it is made again on every tick that a hero shot's look-ahead path meets the body, so a shot that is coming from some way off is dodged with probability 1 - (1 - p)^n over its n ticks of approach (practically always for p of 60 or more). Each dodge therefore costs the player about one shot or swing and then opens a window; it never makes the enemy untouchable for good.
- Per kind (dodge chances are percent, strict below the roll, so 90 means 89 of 100): Minion Wizard rows 211 to 216: melee dodge 90, missile dodge 60, dodge clip 57 ticks (melee) or 61 (missile), window int[0, 60]; row 214 also blocks (melee 100, 85, 75; missile 50). Minion Artillery rows 162 to 167: both 100, clip 41 ticks, window int[60, 120]. Giant Slorg (row 230, a rush kind that holds 9.8 to 21.2 away and spits): both 100, clip 57 ticks, window int[60, 120]; Slorg (row 229): melee 50, missile 75, same clip and window. Minion Archer: melee 50, 10 and 0, missile 10. Minion Magician: melee 90, 25, 25, missile 50. Skeleton Archer: melee 100, 100, 50, missile 90. Skeleton Wizard: melee 75, 50, 25, missile 50.
- Test vector (Giant Slorg): a swing at tick 0 with the hold at 0 and roll 50 starts the melee dodge (outer 5, hold 60); the clip ends at tick 57 and the exit sets the hold to 90, say; swings at ticks 58 to 146 all land (no defence is evaluated while the hold is 1 or more); the first swing at tick 147 or later, with the hold at 0, is dodged again on any roll up to 99. A hero swinging every 18 ticks lands 5 swings per window of 90.

**Lost-sight test.** Not in outer 9, and not in outer 2 with a grab-and-throw slot (flag 0x08): when the sight line to the hero is blocked, outer 9 starts and the last-seen point is the hero's position.

**Hit reactions (outer 10).** The take-hit hook chooses the kind (Behavior 37, step 7); a blocked charge chooses kind 2 or 3 (Behavior 13, state 9).
- Kind 0: animation 57; for a flyer, the grounded countdown starts and the navmesh is picked again (Behavior 6); then outer 0.
- Kind 5: animation 127, then outer 0.
- Kind 6: the enemy stops; animation 152, then outer 0.
- Kind 7: when animation 76 is not the current animation it is requested (a row whose table has no clip for 76 plays clip 0 of its main set), and a Can Fly 1 enemy that is airborne draws its grounded countdown from the row's ground timer range (Behavior 34, step 2; 300 to 900 ticks on the rows that use this kind) and picks the navmesh again, exactly as in kind 0. While 76 is current, the reaction waits until the character's ground gap (position height minus the ground height; 0 while its floor flag is set) is at most 2.0 (not a speed test), then plays 74 to its end when the table has it, then returns to outer 0. The character is not hittable while animation 74, 75 or 76 is current (SPEC-0036 Hittable predicate), so a reaction that is still waiting for the gap leaves it unhittable and still. Waiting is the only unbounded step: the grounded countdown runs in the pre-step regardless of the outer sub-state and, when it reaches 0, state 22 is written (outer sub-state 10 never blocks the Action-19 gate), so the wait ends at the latest when the countdown does. The change from 76 to 74 is the same clip and does not restart it. When the grounded countdown ends the flyer re-launches (state 22) and returns to its default state.
- Kind 2 (stun): animation 60 (flyers 158) until the stun countdown (0xBF0) is 0, then 61 (159), then outer 0. The outer-10 handler only reads this countdown; the only decrement instruction is in attack-sequencer state 11, which has no writer and is not stepped while outer 10 runs (Behavior 13). Thus this reaction’s countdown does not expire by itself (Edge cases).
- Kind 3: animation 62 (160), then 64 (161), then outer 0.
A new reaction is ignored while outer 10 runs kind 2 with stun type 1. Starting a reaction while the sequencer is in state 14 sets the recovery countdown and the slot cooldown and clears the selected slot.

### 45. Attack manager and wait in line (High for the list, the budget and the order; Medium-High for the wait-in-line steps)

There are no numbered attack slots. One shared list holds every class-17 enemy that is in agro. A weight budget decides each frame which of them may start an attack. A waiting enemy is held back from the sequencer start only.

- List: run-time memory at 0x35B320 (not on disc). A 16-byte header (count; an unused word; the weight used this frame; the capacity this frame), then up to 20 entries of 16 bytes (enemy id, a word 1, a distance float that starts at -1.0, the wait flag that starts at 0).
- Grant (add): on entering class state 19 (setter for state 19) and in the take-hit hook when it sends the enemy to agro. An id already on the list is refused, with a warning, and so is a 21st enemy. A refused enemy still fights, because a missing entry reads as "not waiting".
- Run: once per frame in the main frame loop, only while the list exists and a hero is selected. All enemies, of every area, share one list.
- Capacity C = 3.0 x (w + a), from the selected hero's gear, read again each frame.
  - w = weapon item id - 98 for Tor (Swords 98 to 105, so 0 to 7) or id - 111 for Roberta (wands 111 to 118, so 0 to 7).
  - a = body item id - 119 for Tor (armour 119 to 125, so 0 to 6) or id - 127 for Roberta (dresses 127 to 134, so 0 to 7). Item 126 gives 0 for either hero.
  - An empty slot gives -1. So C runs from -6 (nothing equipped) through 0 (starting gear) to 39 (Tor) or 42 (Roberta).
- Release (remove), in two passes each frame, with no timeout:
  - First pass: remove an entry whose enemy no longer exists, is not in class state 19, or meets the dead test. A kept entry stores its enemy's distance to the selected hero.
  - Third pass: remove an entry whose enemy no longer exists.
  - Removal copies the last entry into the freed place and shrinks the count. The pass then goes on with the next place, so the moved entry is skipped for that frame: its distance and wait flag keep the old values, and its weight is not counted.
  - Leaving agro releases the entry on the next frame. That covers retreat (state 14), death and reanimation (state 16). A released character in base state 42 (parked) keeps its entry while its class state is still 19. It is then measured from where it was parked, so it normally sorts last, but its weight still counts (Medium).
- Order (second pass): for i from 0 to n - 2, and for j from i to n - 1, swap entries i and j when both hold:
  - the enemy now in place i is eligible to move: not busy (sequencer states 0 to 3), not blocking, and its class-17 state-19 sub-state (+0xAF8) is not 10 (the hit-reaction entry sets this marker; it is distinct from outer-state word +0xB30);
  - distance j < distance i + 5.0 (strict).
  Entry i is read again after each swap. The result is not a plain sort. Within a group whose distances are less than 5.0 apart, the order turns over from frame to frame (Test vectors). A busy enemy is never moved by its own turn as i, so it keeps its place unless a free enemy before it swaps with it.
- Budget (third pass): used = 0. Walk the entries in order. Entry k waits when C < used and k > 0; otherwise it may attack. Then used += its row Difficulty value (0x3A: 0, 1, 2, 5, 8, 12 or 15 on disc). Its own weight is added after its own test, and the first entry never waits. So entry k attacks when the weights of the entries before it sum to at most C.
- Number of attackers: exactly one while C is negative. At C = 0 it is one, plus any entry that has only weight-0 enemies before it. With weight-8 enemies it is 1 at C = 0, 2 at C = 15 and 5 at C = 39. It is never more than 20. There is no per-class limit and no limit per attack type.
- Wait query: an enemy waits when its entry's flag is 1; an enemy not on the list never waits. Only three readers use it: the sequencer start (outer event 2), the wait-in-line state, and the aim scatter of spawned shots.
- Refused enemy (wait in line, outer sub-state 12): when outer event 2 would start the sequencer (Behaviors 7 and 14) while the enemy waits and its approach routine (0x38) is not 3, the event starts outer 12 instead. Approach-3 enemies are never held back; their shots get 3.0 more aim scatter while they wait (Behavior 13).
  - Entry: gait set 2 (flyers 98), and a stand-off point P is set as the move point. P = hero position + (u - R/2, 0, v - R/2), where u and v are drawn independently from the integers 0 to trunc(R) - 1 (0 when trunc(R) is 0), and R = row 0xB0 (on disc 0, 5, 10, 12, 15, 20, 25, 30, 40 or 50). If P does not locate on the enemy's navmesh, P moves halfway toward the enemy, up to 10 times; after the 10th failure P is the enemy's own ground point.
  - Each tick, while the countdown (object word 0xBF8, not reset on entry, normally 0) is 1 or more: face the hero, play the idle pick, and count down by one. The enemy keeps moving to P at the walking gait (movement mode 0), which plays gait word 0 of the set.
  - When the countdown is 0 or below and the enemy has arrived at P: play 73 once (163 for flyers). When it ends, or at once when the kind has no such animation, set the countdown to int[600, 800] and pick a new P in the same way with R = 50.0 (a fixed constant). Before arrival nothing more happens.
  - Every tick, after these steps: when the enemy no longer waits, outer 0 starts again (approach, then the normal attack choice).
- Hero switch: on the next frame the capacity comes from the new hero's gear and the distances are to the new hero. Entries are kept.
- The hero's own health is not tested. Victory poses come from the melee strike (setter 10), not from the manager.
- Animation pick (Behavior 8): idle variants 115 to 119 and walk 97. Flying variant 142.
- Animation sets: the six words written by the set helper are the gait animation set of the current outer sub-state (Behavior 44).

### 46. Row data for attack execution and map search (High for the counts; confirmed on disc)

- 109 class-17 rows in resource 530, out of 259 rows in the table. The counts in this section are for these 109 rows only.
- Attack slots per row, counting only the slots whose type is not 0xFFFF (31 of the 109 rows have gaps between their used slots, so the highest slot number is not the count): 0 on 2 rows, 1 on 15, 2 on 42, 3 on 35 and 4 on 15. Slot types: melee on 208 used slots, missile on 56 used slots.
- Projectile type field values over the 264 used slots of these 109 class-17 rows: 0 on 222, 23 on 38, 10 on 2, 6 on 1 and 1 on 1.
- The map search flag is set on 15 rows; their types are listed in Behavior 39.
- Reanimating rows: 224 (Skeleton Archer; approach routine 3; 50 hit points), 226 (Dumb Skeleton; approach routine 1; 50 hit points) and 227 (Skeleton Fighter; approach routine 1; 75 hit points).
- Steal rows (all 259 rows; steal fields of SPEC-0029 Layout section 4, rules in SPEC-0029 Behavior 11a and 11b). Neopoint steal (selector 0) on row 152 (Thief, slot 2: 10 to 100 Neopoints, 50 %), row 153 (Rogue, slot 2: 10 to 100, 50 %) and row 154 (Thug, slot 3: 20 to 200, 50 %). Item steal (selector 1, 100 %) on row 136 (Crokabek, slot 2) and row 138 (Swamp Crokabek, slot 2). Row 137 (Mountain Crokabek, slot 2) has the steal bit with selector 0 and an amount range of 0 to 0 at 100 %, so its steal takes nothing. No other row has the steal bit.
- No per-area or per-act multiplier: no routine scales a common enemy's row values by the area, the act or the story stage. Enemies met later are harder only because other rows (types) are placed there, in larger numbers (SPEC-0029, Scaling).

### 47. Respawn and kill memory (High for the kill bit, the bind exclusion, the reanimation bypass and the release rule; Medium-High for the save link)

The streaming of characters into a block and the kill bits are specified in SPEC-0006 (character activation, streaming and kill memory). For common enemies they give these respawn rules:

1. A killed common enemy never comes back. When a bound character is found dead, the kill bit of its spawn record is set. Each world keeps 256 kill bits. A record whose bit is set is never bound again by streaming. The bits are stored into the world's own saved record (the per-world record of SPEC-0025) when the world is left, loaded from it when the world is entered, and saved with the game. So an ordinary enemy does not respawn on re-entry, after a timer, or after save and load. Summon-reserved records are not ordinary placements: streaming never binds them, and a later summon by key ignores their kill bit and binds the record again, as a new character at full health, once the corpse is released (SPEC-0029 Behavior 31, Reuse of a reserved record).
2. Exception, reanimating rows: rows 224 (Skeleton Archer), 226 (Dumb Skeleton) and 227 (Skeleton Fighter) ignore the kill bit when they are bound. They rise again in place after their reanimation countdown (Behavior 35), and they are bound again after being streamed out and back in. While such an enemy lies with its countdown above 0, the dead test counts it as dead and its kill bit is set each tick; the bind ignores it (Open question 81, resolved).
3. An enemy released while alive (the hero walked more than 280 units away, SPEC-0006) is not killed. Its spawn record takes the character's current position and facing, and a later bind brings it back at that point at full health (the bind starts fresh from the monster row).
4. A forced bind of a record clears its kill bit (ActivateEntity, SPEC-0030, and a few engine routines such as the spider nest). DeactivateEntity kills permanently: it runs the death hook and sets the kill bit.
5. A game load does not flush the bits of the world being left into that world's saved record. Kills made since the world was entered are lost when a game is loaded, which matches loading an older save.
6. A new game clears every world's bits (the per-world records are reset).

### 48. Movement: locomotion animations, travel speed and turning (High for the mechanism and the animation ids; Medium-High for which id each state sets)

1. **Travel by root motion.** A ground enemy moves only by the root motion of the locomotion animation it is playing (SPEC-0040 Behavior 14D). Class 17 has no speed value of its own: the speed of each gait is the root travel of that enemy's own clip (per tick = the clip's root step of the current key times the clip speed; SPEC-0005, SPEC-0040 Behavior 14C item 1), so it differs from enemy to enemy and is not a row value. "Steer toward a point" in this spec sets the move direction only. Flying enemies add a row speed (item 4).
2. **Locomotion animation ids.** Entering a class state or an agro outer sub-state loads a set of animation ids, one per movement class (the gait words of Behavior 44); the movement step plays the id of the current class.
   - Class states 5, 7 and 8: class 0 plays 2, class 2 plays 21. Class state 6: class 0 plays 4, class 2 plays 21. Class state 9: gait 0 plays 11, gait 2 plays 21 (Behavior 40). Class state 1: 20.
   - Agro outer sub-states: rush, keep distance and strafe use the gait sets of Behavior 44 (rush 21, 23 back, 55; keep distance 24, 26 back, 55; strafe 28, 32 back, 55, sides 29 and 30); outer 11 (confused) plays 69; outer 2 (the sequencer) plays 2 while it walks the map-search path (Medium).
   - Flyers use their own ids: 98 (wander), 99 (patrol), 100 (class state 9), 154 (class state 1), 101 and 102 (rush), 107 and 108 (keep distance), 103 to 106 (strafe).
   - Missing ids: the attack phases and the fidgets are checked against the table and skipped when missing (Layout section 6). Any other request of an id that the enemy's table lacks starts clip 0 of its main set (SPEC-0005, Lookup miss), and the enemy then travels with that clip's root motion.
3. **Ground speeds (examples, play rate 1, units per second).** The patrol speed (animation 4, flyers 99) of every kind that patrols on disc is listed in Behavior 49: 1.88 to 11.26 for ground kinds, so the walk of a kind is much slower than its rush. Werelupe Basher rush (21) 23.58; Skeleton Fighter rush 15.01; Spyder walk (2) 11.26 and rush 9.38; Ixi Archer rush 20.0; Minion Warrior strafe forward (28) 12.5; Ixi Spearman (row 159) strafe forward (28) 20.0; the other Ixi Spearman rows (Ixi Henchman, Dark Ixi Spearman) strafe forward 5.67. The back ids (23, 26, 32) move along the facing's reverse and the side ids (29, 30) along the side axis, each at the root speed of its own clip. The clean side takes every speed from the enemy's clips, not from a table.
4. **Flyers.** On top of the root step, a flying enemy moves along its movement axis at 2 x Flying Idle Velocity (row 0x8C) units per second outside agro and 2 x Flying Agro Velocity (row 0x90) in agro (SPEC-0040 Behavior 14D item 7). Row speeds, idle / agro: Minion Grunt 6 / 14, Minion Archer 8 / 16, Crokabek 6 / 16 (Crokabek's flying clips carry no root travel, so these are its whole speeds).
5. **Attack speed words.** The wind-up, attack and recovery speed words of a slot (Layout section 2) are direct stores to the per-tick speed scale P by the class coroutine, before the same-update clip re-time (Behavior 1). The current slot value therefore sets the clip rate that tick; the later animation pass evaluates at that rate, and its updated root step is used by the following motion-record pass. Because the row value is stored after status processing, it replaces the P value accumulated from earlier status factors for that update.
6. **Chase stop.** While the enemy is in agro outer sub-state 2 with the sequencer in states 0 to 3, its step is discarded on any tick that it is within the sum of the radii (its record radius plus the active hero's record radius, 3D distance) of the hero (SPEC-0040 Behavior 14D item 4). It does not walk into the hero while it approaches, winds up and strikes.
7. **Turning.** Each tick the enemy's facing is set from its steered move direction M by its locomotion mode (the facing helper of the movement step, SPEC-0040 Behavior 8). Modes 3, 4 and 8 (back gaits) face -M. Modes 5 and 9 face up x M, and modes 6 and 10 (side gaits) face M x up, where up = (0, 1, 0). Every other mode faces M. The blend is the value the steering step passes (0.3 in the navigation step) for back and other modes. For side modes it is 0.1, or 1.0 when the current facing is already above dot 0.9 with the target (High for the rule; Medium for the mode numbers of the gait sets). For ground enemies the gait number of a sub-state is the locomotion mode; flyer forms use their own clip ids (Behavior 44). Test vector: enemy at (0, 0, 0), hero at (0, 0, 10). Inner 8 (gait 6, move (1, 0, 0)): facing (0, 0, 1), toward the hero; its id-30 clip then carries it toward (-1, 0, 0). Inner 9 (gait 5, move (-1, 0, 0)): facing (0, 0, 1); its id-29 clip carries it toward (+1, 0, 0) (Behavior 51, item 7). Back step away (gait 3, move (0, 0, -1)): facing (0, 0, 1). The steering blend weight of the step that steers is the only other turn limit (for example 0.1 in the sequencer's facing step, Behavior 13). Ground Turn Speed (row 0x84) is not read for ground movement; row 0x84 (and row 0x88 for flyers) is read only as the aim turn speed of the agro hold sub-states (Behavior 44).
8. **Timing.** The AI requests the animation during the NPC update. The animation pass later in that same character pass starts it and evaluates its first root step; the next motion-record pass consumes that step. Thus the first step comes one tick after the request, then every tick moves one full step, with no acceleration (SPEC-0040 Behavior 14D item 5; High for the order, Medium-High for exact clip-start boundary cases).

Test vectors (synthetic, computed from the rules above): a Werelupe Basher that rushes for 60 ticks after its first step covers 23.58 units; a Spyder that walks for 120 ticks after its first step covers 22.52 units. Chase stop: enemy radius 1.0, hero radius 0.5, sequencer state 0, distance 1.4: no step this tick; distance 1.6: the step is taken. The same enemy at distance 1.4 in outer sub-state 1 (rush, not the sequencer) takes its step.

### 49. State 6, patrol: route, point order, pauses, speed and return (High for the per-tick rules, the point order, the pause arithmetic, the speeds and the return rule; Medium-High for the invalid-index case and the off-mesh goal rule)

Spawn-record words (SPEC-0006, kind 3): +0xB8 path index (s32); +0xBC loop mode (s32: 0 loops, any nonzero walks back and forth; only 0 and 1 occur on disc); +0xC4 and +0xC8 the pause range at a point (ticks, either order); +0xCC the random-stop chance (percent); +0xD0 and +0xD4 the random-stop range (ticks, either order). Run-time fields of the enemy: route-installed flag (0), point count, first entry, point offset (0), direction flag (forward), reached flag (0), hold timer (-1), fidget counter (0) and fidget phase (-1); the values in brackets are the creation values. The townsfolk patrol (mode 6, SPEC-0056) runs the same route, advance, arrival and random-stop code with its own walk id (8) and fidget id (11).

**Entering the state.** The setter loads the gait set (gait 0 plays animation 4 and gait 2 plays 21; a flyer's gait 0 plays 99). Like every setter call it clears the words at 0xB00 and 0xB08 and draws 0xB04 as 120 plus a whole number from -60 to 60 (no common-enemy routine reads them; they are not socialize timers); it never writes the socialize countdowns (Behavior 10). It changes none of the route fields, so entering the state again after any other state continues the route where it was.

**Per tick, after the pre-step (High).**
1. Route set-up. While no route is installed: the navigation triangle caches (current and target triangle) are cleared, and when 0 <= index < the pack's path count the route is installed: point count = B - A + 1 for the path's first and last entry, first entry = A, hold timer = -1, reached flag = 1. Set-up happens once in the life of the enemy object (a streaming release and later bind creates a fresh object). With an invalid index the clear repeats on every tick and no route is ever installed. The route fields then keep their creation values (reached flag 0, point offset 0, hold timer -1, direction forward, first entry 0, because the first-entry word is not written by the creation reset and an object starts with it clear; Medium for a reused object slot), so the current point is entry 0 + 0 of the pack's entry list (the 0x50-byte locator records at the world's entry table; the same table the paths index), whether or not the pack has a path list. R-0139: the answer is the entry list, not path 0: pack 753 (the Gelert Assassin's) has no path list, and the Lower Burrows pack's only path starts at entry 0 as well. Consequence on every tick: step 3 never runs (the reached flag is 0), the walking tick targets entry 0, and on arrival within 0.5 horizontally the hold timer is drawn from int[+0xC4, +0xC8] and the reached flag becomes 1; the route-advance step that clears that flag runs only for an installed route, so the flag stays 1 and each later walking tick (the one tick after every hold) is again an arrival that draws a new pause. The enemy walks from its placement to entry 0 once (124 units for the Gelert Assassin, 406 for the Elite, 150 for the Tentacle) and then idles there with the fidget, for ever; nothing advances.
2. A hold timer above 0 goes down by 1.
3. With the reached flag set: the point offset is advanced (below), the flag is cleared, and the walk target is set to the new point. The point is the position (x, y, z at +0x30) of entry (first entry + offset) of the pack's entry list; an entry index below 0 or past the list is read as entry 0.
4. A hold timer below 1 makes this a walking tick; otherwise it is a hold tick.

**Walking tick.** Gait 0. The walk target is set to the current point again, and the navigation step of SPEC-0056 runs: it requests the gait animation, or the idle pick when the enemy is already inside the step's own 0.25 arrival distance, and steers. Then the horizontal distance (the vertical lane ignored) from the enemy to the point is tested:
- below 0.5: the reached flag is set, the hold timer is drawn from int[+0xC4, +0xC8], and the fidget counter is drawn separately from the same range;
- otherwise, only when the awareness countdown (Behavior 2, step 2) is 0, that is on one tick of every awareness cycle (the row's awareness timer plus 2 ticks), a roll int[1, 100] is made; when it is below +0xCC the hold timer is drawn from int[+0xD0, +0xD4] and the fidget counter separately from the same range. This random stop does not set the reached flag and does not change the point, so the walk resumes toward the same point. A stop range of 0 to 0 is a stop of length 0 (the roll is still made).

**Hold tick.** No steering and no navigation step run, and nothing turns the enemy: it keeps the facing of its last steering. With the fidget counter below 1:
- phase -1: request animation 6 (flyers 142) without a table check and set the phase to 0;
- phase 0: when the playing animation has reached progress 0.99 (Behavior 2 gate), draw the fidget counter again from int[+0xC4, +0xC8], set the phase to -1 and request the idle pick.
With the fidget counter 1 or more: request the idle pick (Behavior 8) and, only when the enemy's table has animation 6 (flyers 142), subtract 1 from the counter. A table without that animation never counts down, so it only plays the idle pick.

**Pause arithmetic.** A pause draw P holds the enemy for P - 1 ticks: the arrival tick is a walking tick, the hold ticks follow, and the tick on which the hold timer reaches 0 is a walking tick again. P of 1 or less gives no hold. The next point is chosen on the first tick after arrival (step 3), at the start of the pause, not at its end. The enemy takes one more root step after the arrival tick (the walk request of the arrival tick is still in effect, Behavior 48 item 8), so it stops about 0.3 to 0.5 units short of the point; with no pause it walks on without stopping and cuts each corner at 0.5.

**Point order.** The offset starts at 0 and the reached flag is 1 after set-up, so the first advance (on the first tick) makes point 1 the first target. Point 0 is first reached only when the path wraps or turns back.
- Loop mode (+0xBC = 0): offset + 1, and from the last point back to 0. A path of n points targets 1, 2, ..., n-1, 0, 1, ... (n = 4: 1, 2, 3, 0, 1, ...). The wrap point is not repeated.
- Back and forth (+0xBC nonzero): the direction starts forward. Forward: offset + 1 while below n - 1; at n - 1 the direction turns back and the offset becomes n - 2. Back: offset - 1 while above 0; at 0 the direction turns forward and the offset becomes 1. The end points are not repeated (n = 4: 1, 2, 3, 2, 1, 0, 1, 2, 3, ...).
- One-point path: loop mode targets the single point for ever. Back and forth turns back at once and uses offset -1, which is the entry before the first one (entry 0 when the path starts at entry 0), then offset 0: the enemy alternates between entry A - 1 and entry A (pack 1369: two Giant Slorgs alternate between entries 0 and 1; three more with loop mode 0 stay on entry 1).
- A path pause is drawn at every point, including the middle points of a back-and-forth walk and the wrap point.

**Speed.** The enemy has no speed value. It travels by the root motion of the clip it plays (Behavior 48), at play rate 1.0, so the patrol speed of a kind is the average root speed of its animation 4 (flyers 99). There is no run gait in the patrol. Speeds in units per second of the kinds that patrol on disc (the average over the clip; every one of these clips has speed byte 2):
- Spyder, Giant Spyder 11.26; Werelupe Basher, Bone Thrower, Elite 7.56; Tentacle 7.15; Minion Warrior 6.01; Skeleton Archer 5.92; Skull Spirit 5.10; all Ixi rows (Archer, Henchman, Spearman, Dark Ixi Archer, Dark Ixi Spearman) 5.00; Thief 4.25; Bandit and Thug 3.96; Plant Gunner, Plant Monster, Spore Monster 3.92; Sludgy 3.75; Drackonack and Giant Drackonack 3.28; Skeleton Fighter and Dumb Skeleton 3.11; Dark Knight 3.00; Flower Monster 2.97; Giant Slorg and Slorg 2.90 (the Slorg table has no animation 4, so clip 0 plays); Minion Brute 1.88.
- Flyers add 2 x the row's Flying Idle Velocity (row 0x8C) to the clip's root speed: Crokabek and Mountain Crokabek 6.00 (their clip 99 has no root travel); Minion Wizard 4.31 (2.51 + 1.80); Minion Magician 1.00; Restless Spirit 2.00 (its table has no animation 99, so clip 0 plays, with no root travel; the current-animation word still reads 99, so the row speed applies, Behavior 48 item 4). A Crokabek grounded by a hit (Can Fly 1, retarget countdown above 0) walks with the ground clip at 1.25.
- Status effects scale the speed through the per-tick speed scale (slow x 0.5, speed x 2.0; Behavior 1). On a slope the ground projection of the step shortens the horizontal speed by the cosine of the slope angle (SPEC-0040 Behavior 14D).

**Turning.** The facing follows the steered direction each tick (Behavior 48 item 7) with the navigation step's blend weights (0.3 when settled in the goal's triangle and 0.25 or more from the goal, otherwise 0.1; SPEC-0056). Because the step is root motion along the current facing, a turn is an arc, not a pivot on the spot; a hold does not turn the enemy toward the next point.

**Navigation.** The patrol does not walk straight lines. The navigation step finds a route over the enemy's current navigation mesh with the mesh's route table (SPEC-0056). A point that lies in no triangle of that mesh makes the goal the centre of the triangle nearest to that point (three-dimensional centre distance, SPEC-0056, route points off the walker's mesh): the enemy walks to that centre, arrives within 0.25, idles there and the route never advances, because the point itself is never within 0.5. The patrol has no stuck handling: only the 0.5 arrival test, a random stop or a notice ends the attempt (High for the off-mesh goal rule; Medium for the per-placement outcomes). On disc most routes lie inside the mesh of the placement; 33 of the 207 routed placements that can appear have at least one point outside every mesh triangle of the right kind (a few within 0.5 of a border, most 2 to 14 units away), so the port needs the border contact and must not teleport the enemy. The enemy picks its mesh once at the start and again only after a hit reaction (Behavior 6), so a point that lies only on another mesh cannot be reached. Characters push each other through the motion-record contacts (SPEC-0040); the patrol has no door, push or yield logic.

**Invalid index (Medium-High).** With +0xB8 below 0 or at or past the path count (disc: one Werelupe Elite in the Werelupe Burrows Lower, index -1, and one cut Tentacle), the set-up never succeeds, the advance does nothing and the reached flag stays set after the first arrival. The enemy walks to entry 0 of the pack's entry list, whatever and wherever that is (for the Elite: 406 units from its placement), pauses there by the pause range, and then keeps arriving at once and pausing again for ever. It does not stand where it is placed.

**Interruptions and return (High for the code, Medium-High for the chain).**
- The notice check and the alarm-group check (Behavior 3) send the enemy to state 2 (state 21 for a Display-0 row), the socialize alert (Behavior 10) to state 3 and a hit (Behavior 37) to agro (state 19). Each of these ends, in the end, by running the setter with 0 (the search giving up, the end of the chat, the agro leash or the lost-sight search, Behaviors 5, 10, 23 and 44), and the pre-step then runs the setter with the default word, 6. Nothing on the way writes the route fields, so the patrol resumes with the same offset, direction, reached flag and the remaining hold timer. An enemy that was walking goes on to the point it was heading to, from wherever it now is, over the mesh; it does not return to the nearest point, to point 0 or to its placement. An enemy that was resting finishes the rest in place; the reached flag is still set, so the next point is chosen on the first tick of the resumed state.
- The hold timer and the fidget counter do not run down in other states (only this state's handler changes them).
- A row whose Display in default is 0 (Minion Warrior, Minion Brute, Minion Wizard and Minion Magician patrols, which are invisible until they notice the hero) enters state 23 when the setter runs with 0: it plays animation 81 and then runs the default word, 6. Every return to the patrol replays animation 81 first.
- Streaming release (the hero farther than 280 units, SPEC-0006 Release and rebind): the position and facing are saved into the record, because state 6 is not the guard state 7. A later bind creates a new object at the saved position with a fresh route: offset 0, forward, and the first target is point 1 again. The route state itself is not saved.
- The global AI pause (state 20) returns to the previous state when it clears.

Test vector (from the disc, ideal ground: straight lines, facing already toward the target; the real tick numbers differ by a few because of the turn blend and the variation of the root step inside the clip cycle). An Ixi Henchman (row 160; Cogham Steppes, loop mode 0, path of 5 points, pause 120 to 600, no random stop, speed 5.00, i.e. 0.0833 per tick) is placed at (290.44, 249.30, 2251.80). The points are P0 (322.72, 260.04, 2310.41), P1 (331.95, 260.04, 2311.32), P2 (340.39, 260.04, 2292.97), P3 (346.54, 260.04, 2275.30), P4 (338.90, 260.04, 2264.93), with horizontal lengths P0-P1 9.28, P1-P2 20.20, P2-P3 18.71, P3-P4 12.88, P4-P0 48.27. With pause draws 300, 150, 450, 200, 120, 600: tick 0 installs the route and advances the offset from 0 to 1 (target P1, 72.56 away) and requests animation 4; the first root step is applied on tick 1; tick 867 sees the distance 0.434 (below 0.5): arrival, hold timer 300; tick 868 advances to P2 (20.02 away) and holds until tick 1166; tick 1167 walks; arrival at P2 on tick 1402 (hold 150), at P3 on 1775 (hold 450), at P4 on 2377 (hold 200); tick 2378 advances to P0 (48.06 away); arrival at P0 on tick 3148 (hold 120); tick 3149 advances to P1 (9.18 away); arrival at P1 on tick 3373. The target order is P1, P2, P3, P4, P0, P1.
Test vector (back and forth). A Dark Knight (row 139; Crossroads, loop mode 1, two points, pause 150 to 300, random-stop chance 50 with stop range 0 to 0, awareness timer 10, speed 3.00, i.e. 0.0501 per tick) is placed on P0 (1405.56, 80.59, -489.12); P1 is (1356.57, 80.59, -404.16), 98.07 away. With pause draws 200, 260, 150: tick 0 advances 0 to 1 and walks toward P1; arrival at tick 1950 (hold 200); tick 1951 advances (turn back, offset 0) toward P0, 97.64 away; arrival at P0 on tick 4091 (hold 260); tick 4092 advances (turn forward, offset 1); arrival at P1 on tick 6292 (hold 150). The awareness countdown is 0 on ticks 11, 23, 35 and so on (cycle 12), the only ticks on which the random-stop roll is made, and with a stop range of 0 to 0 it changes nothing. A Skeleton Archer (row 224; Dungeon Ruins, two points 83.58 apart, no pause, speed 5.92) arrives at ticks 837, 1677, 2517 and 3357 and turns round at each end without stopping.

### 50. One tick of a common enemy and its contact with the hero, in order (High)

This section only collects the order that Behaviors 1, 2, 7, 13, 14, 37, 44 and 45 give piece by piece, with SPEC-0007 (Order of work inside one tick) and SPEC-0036 (Tick order of the tests). Everything happens once per pass.

**Order inside one pass.**
1. Each active enemy, in region-list order (the current world's own region, then its slot regions, then the second world's), runs three calls:
   a. The update hook: speed scale P to 1.0; status tick; then, in the base states that step the coroutine, one step of the think loop (target search when the target is -1; the pre-step, Behavior 2; the handler of the class state). For class state 19 the pre-step's switch runs the agro machine: recovery countdown, block-state fix, missile defence, melee defence, lost-sight test, normal choice, counter choice, distance-hide gate, the outer sub-state switch (the sequencer of Behavior 13 with its strike test or spawner; the sequencer's speed word is stored to P here), and the agro tail; then the class-19 handler (defence hold down by one, hold or pursuit animation requests). After the switch the playing clip is re-timed from the speed scale times P.
   b. The motion tick: contacts, gravity and the integrator, with the root step stored by the previous tick's animation pass.
   c. The animation pass: starts the clip requested in (a) on this same tick, samples it, stores the next root step and poses the skeleton and the attachment frames.
2. The projectile pool, then the attack manager (Behavior 45). The pool's shots hit the hero in its own step.
3. The selected hero: its update (attack window from its current frame, the swing and shot tests, the action-state switch), its motion tick and its animation pass. The hero's swing and shot tests meet the enemy's bone capsules as step 1c of this tick posed them.

**What each reader sees.**
- Everything an enemy's think reads about the hero (position, pose, bones, state, health, guard timer, swing queries) is the hero as the previous tick left it. A hero swing that lands on tick T changes the enemy at once (health, reaction state and alert flag are written inside the hit call), but the enemy's think sees it from tick T + 1; the animation of the reaction starts in the animation pass of tick T + 1.
- An enemy strike on tick T acts inside the call: the hero's take-hit, status, knockback and state 17 or 48 are in place before the hero's update of tick T. The hero's update then runs in that state, so a swing the hero would have started or continued on tick T is not started or continued, and a hero in a charge or power step stays in it for the take-hit but is moved to state 48 by a knockback above 0 (the state write of the knockback does not test the busy rule; Medium-High). A same-tick exchange is therefore decided in the enemy's favour: the hero's tick-T swing test never runs once the enemy's strike has landed in item 6 of the same pass.
- The strike test, the spawn events and every attack-active read see the frame of the enemy's own last animation pass. A clip requested by the think of tick s starts in tick s and its first sample is frame 0; at play rate 1 and speed byte 2 the cursor the think of tick s + j sees is 0.25 j (j at least 1), so a window of frames a to b is seen on ticks s + 4a to s + 4b + 3, and a finished one-shot clip is seen finished from tick s + 4(F - 1) + 1.
- The weapon anchor and axis the strike test reads are the attachment frames of the same pass (the previous tick's pose, one tick behind the drawn frame), and the hero's capsules are the hero's bones of the previous tick.
- An enemy that dies on tick T (the hero's call writes base state 16) is not stepped from tick T + 1: an attack in progress simply stops and no later window tick is tested.
- The shared random stream is consumed in the order the enemies run and, inside one enemy, in the order of the steps above. A port that needs to match draw counts should keep exactly these orders: pre-step (awareness has no draw; the alarm timer reload; the notice rolls A then B; the map search chance roll) before the agro machine's draws (defence rolls r1 then r2, shot dodge rolls, the normal choice roll of each slot reached, the counter roll, the hide gate roll), then the sequencer's draws (aim scatter e, x, z; theft rolls, status roll) and the end path (recovery, then slot cooldown). The agro machine's own order is: missile-defence rolls, melee-defence rolls r1 then r2, the normal choice (one roll per slot that passes its band, cooldown and cone tests, highest slot first; then the cooldown draw and the redraw of Behavior 4), the counter roll, the hide-gate roll.

**Worked tick table (melee slot, strike clip B with 21 keys, no wind-up clip, window frames 5 to 8 on weapon 1, speed words 1.0, hero on the walk mesh).**
- Tick c: the normal choice picks the slot (cooldown drawn, distances redrawn) and starts the sequencer in state 0; the outer switch of the same tick runs it, and with the hero and steer point on the walk mesh it goes to state 1 (a move to the next state takes effect the next tick).
- Tick c + 1 (this is s): state 1 finds no wind-up clip, requests B and sets state 2. B starts in this tick's animation pass.
- Tick s + 1: state 2 runs the strike test; the window is closed (frame 0). Ticks s + 20 to s + 35: the window is open (frames 5 to 8) and each tick makes the contact test against the hero's capsules. Tick s + 20 is the earliest tick a hit can land.
- A landing on tick s + 22 puts the hero in state 17 (or 48) within the tick; the sequencer is in state 3 from tick s + 23. The hit list now holds the hero, so later window ticks do nothing more; if the clip had a second window and no follow-through clip, state 3 re-enters state 2 on the first tick of that window and clears the hit list.
- Tick s + 81: B passes its 0.99 gate (F = 21: 4 x 20 + 1) and the sequencer goes to state 5 (from state 2 or state 3). Tick s + 82: with no recovery clip, state 5 runs the end path: outer sub-state 0, the recovery countdown and the slot cooldown are drawn.

### 51. Movement framework for implementers (shared by every class-17 kind; High unless marked)

This section collects, in one place, every rule a port needs to move a common enemy. Behaviors 44, 48 and 49 and SPEC-0040 Behavior 14D hold the detail; each kind's numbers are in its "Movement" table (SPEC-0063, SPEC-0064, SPEC-0065, SPEC-0017). Time: 60 ticks per second. Units: world units.

1. **Where the speed comes from.** A ground enemy has no speed value. Every move it makes, walking, running, backing off, circling, charging, dodging, flinching and dying, is the root travel of the clip it plays (SPEC-0040 Behavior 14D). Per tick the step is the root record of the current key times the clip speed c = R x 0.5 / S, where S is the clip's speed byte (2 on nearly every enemy clip, so c = 0.25 key per tick) and R = 1.0 times the per-tick scale P. A key's root record is the travel over that whole key, so one pass of a clip moves exactly the sum of its key records, and the mean speed is mean(key record) x c x 60 units per second. The step is turned by the enemy's facing basis (forward along the facing, x along B1 = up x facing). There is no acceleration, no deceleration, no speed cap and no per-row speed scale.
2. **What changes the rate.** Only P: slow x 0.5 and speed x 2.0 (status), stun 0, and the attack wind-up, attack and recovery speed words while the sequencer runs (Behavior 48 item 5). The distance to the hero never changes the play rate, and no walk clip is sped up to "run". R scales the travel and the animation together.
3. **Walk or run.** There is no walk or run choice in agro. Every state loads the same clip id for gaits 0, 1 and 2 (rush 21, keep distance 24, strafe 28; flyers one id for class 7), so the "gait 2 when d is above twice the rush maximum" branch of Behavior 44 changes nothing that can be seen. Outside agro the wander, sentry walk-home and emerge walk use id 2, the patrol id 4 and the named-target follow id 11; these are slower clips (each kind's table).
4. **Flyers.** On top of the root step, a flyer adds v/30 per tick along its class axis (2v units per second), v = Flying Idle Velocity (row 0x8C) outside agro or Flying Agro Velocity (row 0x90) in agro, only while the playing clip is the gait clip of its current class; in sequencer states 7, 8, 9 and 12 the term is +facing x v/30 whatever the clip. Most flying gait clips have no root, so the row speed is the whole speed. Height (SPEC-0040 Behavior 14D item 7): Can Fly 1 aims at the hero's height plus min(Max Fly Height, 0.2 x the horizontal distance), at least 2.0 above the ground under the goal; Can Fly 2 aims at the hero's own height, capped at ground + Max Fly Height, with no 0.2 term and no lower bound. The height moves 0.1 per tick (6 units per second).
5. **Steering.** "Steer to a point" sets a goal for the navigation step of SPEC-0056; it never moves the body. The step routes over the enemy's navmesh with the mesh's route table and turns the facing toward the next steering point.
6. **Turning while moving.** Each tick facing = normalise(w t + (1 - w) c), with c the old facing and t the steered direction: w = 0.3 once the enemy is in the goal's triangle, 0.1 on a route edge, off the mesh and on the first tick in the goal's triangle. A 90-degree turn at w = 0.3 leaves 66.8, 48.2, 34.2, 24.1 degrees after ticks 1 to 4. Because the step follows the facing, a turn is an arc, never a pivot on the spot. Back gaits (modes 3, 4, 8) face away from the move direction; side gaits (modes 5, 6, 9, 10) face across it (SPEC-0040 Behavior 8a) and snap (w = 1.0) once the target facing is within a dot of 0.9.
7. **Circling (strafe inners 8 and 9).** The steering point (own position -2p for inner 8, +2p for inner 9, p = normalise(dir x up), dir toward the hero) only turns the body to face the hero; the side clip then carries it sideways. Id 29 (inner 9) moves along +B1 and id 30 (inner 8) along -B1 on every strafing kind of SPEC-0064, and B1 = up x facing = -p, so inner 8 travels along +p and inner 9 along -p: each circle runs away from its own steering point. Because the facing re-snaps to the hero every tick and each step is tangent, the circle's radius grows by about s squared / (2d) per tick (s the step, d the distance): negligible, so in practice the enemy orbits the hero at a constant distance.
8. **Holding.** A hold sub-state has no travel clip (or a clip with no root) and turns toward the hero by the row's Ground Turn Speed (row 0x84) per tick, flyers row 0x88; the keep-distance hold first blends 0.25 and then row 0x84, an effective 1 - 0.75 (1 - w). A hold does not stop the steering of the previous tick: the enemy moves one more full step after it decides to hold (item 9).
9. **One extra step.** The think of tick t sees the position after the steps of ticks 1 to t - 1. When it switches to a hold or arrives, the motion of tick t still uses the old clip's step (the new clip's first step lands on tick t + 1, and a hold clip has none), so every stop overshoots its threshold by one step.
10. **Chase stop.** Only while the attack sequencer is in states 0 to 3 is the step dropped inside own radius + hero radius (3D). In rush, keep-distance and strafe sub-states the enemy may walk into the hero; the character push separates them (item 13).
11. **Received knockback.** None. No hero swing, shot or Mote gives an enemy a velocity (SPEC-0029 Behavior 10). A hit first snaps the enemy's facing to the attacker (weight 1.0, Behavior 37 step 11) and then starts the hit-reaction clip (57, flyers 152; 60 and 61 stun; 62 and 64 heavy; 127), whose own root travel is the flinch: a slide straight away from the attacker, from 0 (Bandit, Thug) to about 15 units (Minion Grunt and Archer). The force-start of step 13 restarts the clip on every hit, so each new hit starts a new slide.
12. **Ledges and falls.** A region character never leaves its navmesh: border edges act as walls and a position off every triangle is put back to the last on-mesh position in the same tick (SPEC-0056, Wall confinement), so enemies stop at ledges whose drop is a mesh border. The 2.0 edge guard of SPEC-0040 Behavior 14E reverses only the current tick's step and only while the ground gap measured before that step is above 2.0; a body that is already off an edge (spawned there, or carried by a slide where the mesh continues) drops straight down under gravity (128 units per second squared) with its horizontal steps cancelled. Flyers that are airborne are never guarded.
13. **Walls and bodies.** Region characters meet navmesh borders, cell list B, movable blocks and other characters, not the world's wall segments. Against another region character the subject of the tick is pushed out by the whole overlap. The hero and an enemy separate only in the hero's own tick (SPEC-0033 Behavior 53). A border push sets the blocked flag for 120 ticks (strafe lock, wander arrival, charge end).
14. **Slopes.** While the gap is below 1.0 the step is projected onto the ground plane, so on a slope of angle a the horizontal speed is cos a times the flat speed; friction 0.6 holds a body at rest up to 30.96 degrees, and steeper ground slides it (SPEC-0040 Behavior 14A).
15. **Held one-shots and corpses.** A finished, non-looping clip that stays selected keeps moving the body: its step is twice the last key's record times c, every tick (SPEC-0040 Behavior 14D item 10). This covers one-shot gait clips that outlast their key count (the Rogue's back-off) and every corpse: the motion tick still runs in base state 16, so a death clip whose last key has root keeps sliding the corpse backward until a navmesh border, the edge guard or the release stops it (per-kind values in the kind tables).
16. **Giant variants.** The size selector scales the skeleton's bone translations only. The root record is never scaled, so a giant variant that shares a clip with its small kind moves at exactly the small kind's speed with longer strides drawn (SPEC-0040 Behavior 14D item 9).
17. **Missing gait ids.** A gait id the kind's table lacks plays clip 0 of the main set with clip 0's own root (Behavior 48 item 2): a kind whose clip 0 walks creeps while it should hold, and a kind whose clip 0 is an idle cannot move in that gait at all.
18. **Leash and give-up.** No route to the hero and the walk waypoint at least 15.0 from the hero for more than 180 ticks returns the enemy to its default state (Behavior 23), which walks it back by the default state's own gait (patrol resumes toward the point it was heading to, Behavior 49; a sentry walks home with id 2).

Clips with frame-stored roots. 97 main-family clips on disc use the older frame layout (mode 0; SPEC-0005). Their root record is the last eight bytes of each frame record, not the separate root table. Among the enemies they are the skeleton death clip 58 (0.82 back over 112 ticks, last key 0), the Restless Spirit approach 101 (0.0427 per tick forward) and drain loop 34 (no travel), the Ixi spear rows' idle 0 and intro 13 (no travel) and the Flower Monster and Poison Flower confused gait 69 (6.14 forward over 220 ticks, then 0.0836 per tick held). Reading the root table of these clips gives large false drift.

Worked example (chase from 30 units; Bandit, row 151; flat ground, hero standing, facing already on the hero). The rush clip 21 has a root of 0.840 per key at speed byte 2: 0.210 units per tick, 12.60 per second. The desired distance is drawn 3.6 to 5.4 (rush band 3 to 6); take 4.5. Tick 0: inner 0 requests id 21 at d = 30.0. Tick 1: first step. The think of tick t sees d = 30 - 0.21 (t - 1). Tick 123 sees 4.38 (below 4.5): inner 1 requests the hold clip 22 (no root). The motion of tick 123 still takes the id-21 step: the Bandit stops at 4.17 from the hero, 123 steps and 2.05 seconds after the request. With a desired distance of 3.6 it stops at 3.33, still outside its radius plus the hero's (2.0 + 1.175 = 3.175).

Worked example (flinch). A Thief (clip 57: 11 keys, root -6.02 over the clip) hit from the north while facing east snaps to face north on the hit tick and slides 6.02 units south over the next 44 ticks, 0.137 units per tick on average (the keys are not equal: about 0.10 per tick at first, 0.22 per tick in keys 3 to 5, 0.04 at the end).

### Routines of other classes (not class 17)

The routines of Behaviors 10b, 16, 17 and 27 to 33 are not run by class 17. The class routine table (SPEC-0029 Layout section 3) gives their owners: Behavior 10b is reached only from the class-6 controller; Behaviors 16, 29 and 30 belong to class 7 (Whinny); Behavior 17 to class 21 (Miamouse); Behaviors 27 and 33 to class 34 (the live petpet controller, SPEC-0047); Behavior 28 to class 36 (Target Dummy); Behavior 31 to class 35 (the level-script path walk, SPEC-0056); Behavior 32 to class 29. They keep their numbers here for reference. A class-17 enemy uses the idle picker of Behavior 8 (holds of 120 to 600 ticks), not the idle coroutine of Behavior 28. Confidence: High for the owners.

### 10b. Group leader handover, class 6 (Medium)

Owner: class 6 (the paired-partner creature, SPEC-0029 Layout section 3). The handover is reached only from the class-6 controller; it is not class-17 behaviour.

Each enemy record keeps two cooldown counts (+0xA94 and +0xA9C) and a leader id field (+0xAA0). Each call decrements both counts by one. The handover search runs only when the second count, after its decrement, is below 1.

The search runs over the global list of characters:

1. Skip the enemy itself.
2. The other character must pass the candidate-eligibility test. It fails when its social-disable word (+0xC98) is nonzero. Otherwise its current animation request word (+0x3B0) must be one of 0, 1, 2, 8, 9, 10, 11, 0x70 to 0x74, 0x3B, 0x3C and 0x6B (a character playing any other animation is not eligible), and its AI state word (+0xA34) must not be 3. A pass continues to the navmesh and range tests (High; this is an inclusion list, the same list as SPEC-0056, Villager pair; Open question 60).
