3. The other character must be on the same navmesh as the enemy (the current navmesh index word of each character is equal).
4. The distance from the enemy to the other character (the horizontal length of the shared distance helper) must be below the float at +0x44 of the enemy's monster row.

On the first match, the search stops and the handover happens:

- The enemy's leader field (+0xAA0) takes the other character's id (the value at its +0x14C).
- The shared AI-state setter sets the enemy's state to 3, then sets the other character's state to 3.
- The other character's leader field takes the enemy's own id.

The pairing reset runs when the linked partner is missing, is no longer in state 3, or is on a different navmesh; it also runs when the current pairing sub-state is 3. It sets the pairing sub-state to 0, requests AI state 0, plays the idle animation the idle picker chooses, and resets the second cooldown (+0xA9C) with a closed integer draw from 10 times the frame-rate word to 30 times that word. At the normal frame rate of 60, this is 600 to 1800 ticks (10 to 30 seconds). Separately, when the shared sub-step setter is called while the pairing sub-state is 0 to enter sub-state 1 or 2, it resets the first cooldown (+0xA94) with a closed integer draw from 5 times the frame-rate word to 12 times that word (300 to 720 ticks at 60 Hz). It then stores the requested sub-state. Calls made while the current sub-state is nonzero do not redraw this timer. After storing the sub-state the setter rolls a random idle gesture for the new role and plays it (a 16-way roll for role 1 and for role 2, each over its own list of gesture animation ids; a rolled id the character lacks is replaced by 0). The setter is part of the class-6 pairing behavior; the known reset is runtime behavior, not a level-module write. The apparent targetless call on the enemy is the shared AI-state setter with state 3, like the following call on the matched character (Open question 61).

### 16. Chase-to-point controller, class 7 (High for the entry, the pick base, the distances and the mode logic)

Owner: class 7 (Whinny, SPEC-0029 Behavior 5). The shipped monster row is resource-530 row 253, selected by type 109, variant 0; its default behavior is class 7. This controller is the Whinny's flee mode together with the lead entry of SPEC-0029 Behavior 5; the pool is the list of pens, and the words at 0x2DB268 and 0x2DB278 are the pens' entrance ids and occupied flags.

The Whinny main loop keeps one block per Whinny and picks one routine per tick, in this order: the penned routine when the penned flag (+0x30) is set; the lead routine when the lead flag (+0x34) is set; otherwise the wander routine (Behavior 29) when the flee selector (+0x0C) is 0, and this controller when it is not 0. Behavior 29 sets the flee selector to 1 when the hero comes within 5.0. Offsets below are the block's: the flee selector (+0x0C), the lead timer (+0x08), the wander mode word (+0x10), the point (+0x20, four floats), the lead flag (+0x34), the pen index (+0x38), the pen entrance id (+0x04), the run timer (+0x40) and the run mode (+0x44; 0 pick, 1 run to the step point, 2 run to the safe point).

Let v and d be the result of the shared distance helper taken from the hero to the Whinny: v is the horizontal unit direction from the hero to the Whinny (away from the hero) and d is the horizontal distance (SPEC-0029 Behavior 2; the helper sets the height part of the direction to 0 and gives the horizontal length).

Each tick of the controller:

1. Pen entry (runs first, on every tick of the controller). The pens are searched in order 0 to count - 1 (count at 0x2DB250, at most 4; the shipped setup calls provide counts 1 or 4). A pen accepts when the pen count is 1 or its occupied flag is 0, and the Whinny's position lies inside that pen's entrance navmesh (the inside test of SPEC-0018: x and z in a triangle, height ignored). On the first accepting pen the controller writes: flee selector 0; lead timer 30 ticks (0.5 s); point = the pen point; wander mode 0; lead flag 1; pen index; entrance id; occupied flag 1. The setup setters clear all occupied flags when they load the list. For a one-pen list, selection ignores the occupied flag; for a multi-pen list, a selected flag stays set through the lead and penned flow. These routines do not clear it on arrival or exit, so multi-pen claims last until a setter reloads the list. It sets the Whinny's navmesh to the entrance id, sets its move target to the pen point, and ends the tick. From the next tick the lead routine runs; it reads the lead timer, the point and the pen index. The run mode (+0x44) is not written here, and nothing reads it again, because a Whinny that starts to lead stays in lead until it is penned and a penned Whinny never flees again.
2. Otherwise the Whinny runs (movement mode 2). If d is above 8.0 the flee selector is set to 0 (8.0 exactly does not), so the wander routine runs from the next tick; the run step below still runs this tick.
3. Run step by mode:
   - Mode 0 (pick). Candidate = the Whinny's position + 4.0 x v, a point 4.0 units from the Whinny directly away from the hero. If the candidate is walkable (the walk-mesh query returns nonzero) and the Whinny's blocked flag (character +0xCC4) is zero: mode 1, run timer = the whole part of (0.25 x the frame-rate word), point = candidate. Otherwise: mode 2, run timer 25 ticks (0.417 s), point = a far centroid pick of radius 10.0 from the hero's position over the Whinny's own navmesh (SPEC-0029 Behavior 28: random triangle centroids until one lies more than 10.0 from the hero horizontally). Then it steers to the point.
   - Mode 1 (run to the step point). The run timer goes down by 1. If it is then below 1, or the horizontal distance from the Whinny to the point is below 2.5, or the blocked flag is nonzero: mode 0 and the blocked flag is cleared. It steers to the point in every case.
   - Mode 2 (run to the safe point). The run timer goes down by 1. If the horizontal distance to the point is at least 2.0 and the timer is at least 1, it keeps running. Otherwise mode 0 and the blocked flag is cleared. It steers to the point in every case.

Distances to points are horizontal: the routine sets the height difference to 0 before it takes the length, the same rule as the shared helper.

Constants: 30 ticks (lead timer), 4.0 (step length), 8.0 (flee end), 0.25 (times the frame-rate word, for the mode 1 timer), 2.5 (mode 1 arrival), 10.0 (safe-point radius), 25 ticks (mode 2 timer), 2.0 (mode 2 arrival).

With the frame-rate word at 60 (its start-up value, SPEC-0007), the mode 1 timer is 15 ticks (0.25 s).

Confidence: High for the routine order, the pen entry, the pick base and the distance rule; High for the constants and the mode transitions.

### 17. Approach controller, class 21 (High)

Owner: class 21 (Miamouse). The shipped monster row is resource-530 row 248, selected by type 104, variant 0; its default behavior is class 21. This controller is the flee state, main state 2, of SPEC-0029 Behavior 28, which is the full rule; this section restates it with the words of this spec. The done word at +0x00 is the Miamouse main state word, and the mode word at +0x04 is the flee sub-state. The flee entry of Behavior 28 sets main state 2 and sub-state 0, so a new flee always starts in mode 0.

It keeps the point at +0x20. The touched flag is the word at +0x238 of the character's motion record (the sub-object at character +0x16C); it is set when the motion is blocked (SPEC-0029 Behavior 28). Every tick of the state the Miamouse runs (movement mode 2) and steers to its point.

Let v and d be the result of the shared distance helper taken from the hero to the Miamouse: v is the horizontal unit direction away from the hero and d the horizontal distance. Let p be the horizontal distance from the Miamouse to its point. A far centroid pick of radius R takes random triangle centroids of the Miamouse's navmesh until one lies more than R from the hero horizontally (SPEC-0029 Behavior 28).

Mode 0:

1. If the touched flag is set: mode 1 with a far centroid pick of radius 10.0, and play clip 0.
2. Then, if d is at most 20.0: candidate = the Miamouse's position + 4.0 x v (4.0 units directly away from the hero). If the candidate is not walkable: mode 1 with a new radius-10.0 pick. If it is walkable: steer to the candidate this tick and leave the record unchanged.
3. If d is above 20.0: mode 2 with a far centroid pick of radius 30.0.

Steps 1 and 3 can both run in one tick. With the flag set and d above 20.0, the last write wins, so the controller ends in mode 2 with the radius-30.0 point (Edge cases).

Mode 1:

1. Steer to the point.
2. If p is at least 0.5 and the touched flag is clear, keep running (no change).
3. Otherwise, if d is below 20.0: mode 0. If d is 20.0 or more: mode 2 with a new radius-30.0 pick.

Mode 2:

1. Steer to the point.
2. If p is below 0.5, or the touched flag is set: the main state becomes 0 (idle). The mode word stays 2; it is not read again until the next flee entry resets it to 0.
3. Otherwise keep running. No hero distance test is made in this mode.

Constants: 20.0 (flee band), 10.0 and 30.0 (pick radii), 4.0 (step length), 0.5 (arrival).

Confidence: High for the modes, the constants, the arrival test and the exits.

### 27. Class-34 main loop and state selector (Medium)

Owner: class 34 (SPEC-0029 Layout section 3), not class 17. Class 34 is the controller of the live (summoned) petpet (SPEC-0047).

The main loop runs once per enemy and takes one step per tick. Its state word starts at 0. Each tick:

1. The selector (below) runs and may change the state.
2. The handler of the current state runs. State 0 plays animation code 0. States 1, 2, 3 and 6 are described below; states 4 and 5 are Behavior 33.
3. When the game-state code is 0 (Resolved, High; proactive (open close-out): SPEC-0047 and SPEC-0026; codes 1 to 8 hold the counter and code 9 stops the update), the cooldown word at +0xE34 goes down by 1.
4. A per-tick update runs (not described here) and the loop yields one tick.

Selector words: cooldown A at +0xE30 (a request for state 4 when nonzero), cooldown B at +0xE34 (ticks; set by the state 4 handler), and the touched flag at +0x238 of the actor's motion record (Resolved, High; proactive (open close-out): the flag set by any contact that writes a contact normal and cleared at the start of each movement update, SPEC-0018 and SPEC-0040; it is not a stun word, and "stun word nonzero" below means "the petpet touched something this tick"). Let s be the current state.

- If s is 3, nothing changes.
- If the stun word is nonzero: when s is not 5, the state becomes 3. Otherwise nothing changes.
- If the stun word is zero and cooldown A is nonzero: the state becomes 4 and cooldown A is cleared.
- If the stun word is zero, cooldown A is zero, and s is neither 4 nor 6:
  - If a record flag at +0x2C is nonzero, or cooldown B divided by (0.5 x tick-rate word x 60) is at least 1.0:
    - If cooldown B is below 1, the state becomes 5.
    - Otherwise, if the hero is in a jump state (hero states 7 to 11 and 43 to 46) or the hero is moving (the moving test of SPEC-0033: hero states 2 to 6 and 32, or states 22, 39 and 40 while action 85 is current; High), the state becomes 2 (follow). Otherwise, unless the state is already 1, the state becomes 1 with a new point (the hero's position plus a random offset of 3.0 to 10.0, with a random sign, on each of x and z), its timer unset and its hold 0.
  - Otherwise the state becomes 6 and the record flag at +0x2C is set to 1.

Test vector (synthetic): cooldown B = 0, record flag 0, stun word 0, cooldown A 0, state 0: the selector moves to state 6 and sets the flag. With cooldown A = 1 in any state other than 3, the state becomes 4.

Cooldown B is the petpet's remaining time in ticks (High): the loop lowers it by 1 per tick, and the selector's ratio r = cooldown B / (0.5 x tick-rate word x 60) is 1 at 1800 ticks, 30 seconds at 60 ticks per second. So the petpet plays its state-6 warning once when 30 seconds are left (the record flag at +0x2C is never cleared again), and leaves (state 5) when the time runs out.

States 1, 2, 3 and 6 (High for the steps; Medium for the meaning of the animation codes):

- State 1, wander near the hero: SPEC-0029 Behavior 12.8. The petpet reacts once with animation 8 to each new character that comes within 10.0, walks (animation 2) to its point, waits there 180 to 600 ticks, then draws a new point near the hero. While it waits it plays animation 7 when r is below 1, otherwise animation 1 or 6 by the roll of that section.
- State 2, follow the hero: SPEC-0029 Behavior 12.13. The follow point is the hero's position plus 4.0 times the hero's facing direction plus 1.5 times its right-hand vector; the petpet idles (animation 0), walks (2) or runs (3) by the gait bands 3.0, 1.5, 7.0 and 5.0.
- State 3, recover when stuck (entered when the touched flag is set): the petpet plays animation 0. Let c = dot(camera view vector at +0x20, unit direction from the camera point at +0x30 to the petpet). While c is at least 0.5 (the petpet is inside the 60-degree view cone) nothing else happens. When c is below 0.5, a point P = the hero's position + 10.0 x the horizontal unit reverse of the camera view vector (10.0 units from the hero toward the camera) is tested: the ground under P (probe started 8.0 above P) must lie within 2.0 of the hero's height. If it does, the petpet is placed at P, facing the hero, and the state becomes 1. Otherwise it stays in state 3 and tries again next tick. The selector never moves the petpet out of state 3.
- State 6, time warning: the petpet plays animation 7 until it has finished, then the state becomes 0.

Test vectors (synthetic):

- Cooldown B = 2000, flag 0, hero standing: r = 1.11, so the selector enters state 1. Cooldown B = 1500, flag 0: r = 0.83, state 6 and the flag is set; after animation 7 ends, state 0, and the next selector tick with the flag set enters state 1 (hero standing) or 2 (hero jumping).
- State 3 with the camera view vector (0, 0, 1) and the hero at (0, 0, 0) on flat ground at height 0: P = (0, 0, -10). The ground under P is at height 0, within 2.0, so the petpet is placed at (0, 0, -10) facing +z and goes to state 1.

### 28. Idle coroutine, class 36 (Medium)

Owner: class 36 (Target Dummy). This is the routine of SPEC-0029 Behavior 29: code 3 is the hit reaction, code 1 the twitch and code 0 the idle. Both specs draw the period as a closed range: both ends are included.

A separate coroutine plays idle animation codes for an enemy. At start it draws a period P in [400, 800] ticks (closed integer draw, both ends included; 400 and 800 are fixed constants) and sets a counter c to 1. Each tick:

1. If the pending-hit flag is set (reading it clears it), it plays animation code 3, sets c to 1, restores the actor's hit points to the maximum, draws a new period P, and yields.
2. Otherwise, if animation 3 is not the current animation or has finished playing, then:
   - when c is a multiple of P, it plays animation code 1 and adds 1 to c;
   - otherwise, it plays animation code 0 unless animation 1 is current and still playing, and adds 1 to c.
   While animation 3 is still playing, nothing happens and c does not change.
3. It yields one tick.

Result: animation code 0 is played on most ticks, code 1 on one tick in every P, and code 3 while busy. The animation codes here are the call codes of the actor's animation control. They are not the idle variant indices 0x73 to 0x77 of Behavior 8 (Resolved, Medium-High; proactive (open close-out): a separate code space of the Target Dummy's animation control; a clean side keeps one table per class).

### 29. Approach and attack bands, class 7 (controller record; Medium)

Owner: class 7 (Whinny).

This controller is the wander branch of the Whinny main loop. The loop keeps one block per Whinny and chooses a routine by three words of that block, tested in order: the penned flag at +0x30 (if nonzero, Behavior 30 runs); the lead flag at +0x34 (if nonzero, the lead routine of SPEC-0029 Behavior 5 runs); and the flee selector at +0x0C (if zero, this controller runs; otherwise Behavior 16 runs). The block is the controller record below, and Behaviors 16, 29 and 30 all work on it (Resolved, High; proactive (open close-out)). The controller record holds: a state word at +0x00, a timer at +0x08 (ticks), the selector word at +0x0C, a mode word at +0x10 (0 approach, 1 hold), the point at +0x20 (four floats), and claim words at +0x3C, +0x40 and +0x44.

Let d be the distance from the actor to the target. Each tick:

0. Pen check (before everything else). When the pen count is exactly 1 and the actor's position lies inside that pen's inner navmesh, the actor becomes penned (penned flag set, its move-target mesh set to the inner mesh, walk mode) and the tick goes on with the steps below.
1. Close band. If d is below 5.0, the state word becomes 2, the flag at +0x0C becomes 1, the claim words are reset (+0x3C to 0, +0x40 to -1, +0x44 to 0), and the controller returns.
2. Re-pick check. If the mode is 0 or the timer is above 0, and the steering flag at +0x238 of the actor's sub-object is clear, go to step 3 with the current mode. Otherwise the mode is cleared, and a new point is set from the target's position helper, and the timer is drawn in [120, 400] ticks (closed integer draw, both ends included). Then go to step 3 with mode 0.
3. Band or wait:
   - If the mode is 0 and the steering flag is clear: when the distance measure of this step is above 10.0, the movement class is set to 2 (fast approach); otherwise when it is above 3.0, the movement class is set to 0 (walk approach); otherwise the mode becomes 1 (hold). Then the target point is set and the actor steers toward it.
   - Otherwise (mode nonzero, or steering flag set): the actor plays animation code 0 and the timer goes down by 1.

The distance measure in step 3 is the horizontal distance (height ignored) from the actor to the controller's point, not the target distance of step 1. The two measures differ, so the 3.0 hold is reachable: the actor reaches within 3.0 of its point while the target is still 5.0 or more away (Resolved, High; proactive (open close-out)). The 10.0 and 3.0 bands and the 5.0 close band are High; the 120 to 400 tick timer is Medium.

### 30. Wait-and-approach step, class 7 (penned routine; High for the rule and the 1.5 reach)

Owner: class 7 (Whinny).

Runs when the penned flag (+0x30) of the Whinny's block is nonzero. It works on that same block (the controller record of Behavior 29): a timer at +0x08, a done word at +0x10 and a point at +0x20 (Resolved, High; proactive (open close-out)). Each tick:

1. If done is nonzero:
   - If the timer is below 1: the point is set from the target's position helper, the timer is drawn in [120, 400] ticks (closed integer draw, both ends included), and done becomes 0. Continue to step 2.
   - Otherwise the actor plays animation code 0, the timer goes down by 1, and the step returns (the wait).
2. Approach (done is 0): let d be the horizontal distance (height ignored) from the actor to the point. If d is at most 1.5, or the steering flag at +0x238 of the actor's sub-object is set, done becomes 1. Otherwise the movement class is set to 0. Then the target point is set and the actor steers toward it.

Test vector (synthetic): with done 0 and d = 1.5, done becomes 1 on that tick. With d = 1.6 it stays 0 and the actor walks.

### 31. Charge and wait coroutine, class 35 (High for the identity; Medium-High for the phase details)

Owner: class 35, the level-script path walk (SPEC-0029 Behavior 1; SPEC-0056, Path walk). Resolved (High; proactive (open close-out)): this coroutine is the class-35 routine itself; it is started whenever a script path walk requests class 35. Phase A is the walk along the path and phase B is the hold when the hero comes near, which is the same hold as the walk-to-point classes (SPEC-0056, Walk-to-point routine, step 5). The "charge" wording is a label for the animation code 30 played once after the 180-tick wait. The authoritative description is SPEC-0056; the phases below are kept as a second view.

A coroutine with two phases. At start it requests the character preset 0, sets up the route (the path of the placement, SPEC-0056 Path walk) and clears the path progress (point index 0, reached flag clear). It keeps a near flag (clear), a wait counter and a standing flag (both 0). Each tick is one of phase A or phase B, then one tick of waiting.

Phase A (walk). Runs while the near flag is clear. First the near test runs: the hero is within 6.0 (horizontal) of the actor and in front of it, that is, the dot product of the unit direction from the actor to the hero with the actor's body forward vector is above 0.0 (a dot of exactly 0.0 fails). When it passes, the near flag is set, the wait counter and the standing flag are cleared, and phase B runs in this same tick (no step is taken). Otherwise:

- With the reached flag set: if the point index is the last one (point count minus 1), the walk is done and the coroutine ends after this tick's wait. Otherwise the point index goes up by 1, the reached flag is cleared and the navigation target becomes the new point (no navigation step this tick).
- With the reached flag clear: the navigation target is the current point and the navigation step runs. Then the horizontal distance (height ignored) from the actor to the point is taken, and when it is below 0.5 the reached flag is set.

Phase B (hold, then charge). Runs while the near flag is set. Each tick:

- If the standing flag is set, the actor plays the animation the idle picker (Behavior 8) chooses.
- Otherwise, while the wait counter is below 3.0 times the tick-rate word (180 ticks at 60 ticks per second), the counter goes up by 1 and the actor plays the idle picker's animation.
- Otherwise, if animation 30 is not the current animation, the actor plays it. If it is current, the standing flag is set when it has finished playing (the clip plays once; afterwards the idle picker's animation plays every tick).
- Then the hold test runs on the hero: the near flag is cleared (back to phase A on the next tick) when the horizontal distance to the hero is above 12.0 (12.0 exactly stays), or when the dot product of the unit direction to the hero with the actor's body forward vector is below -0.1.

The wait counter and standing flag are cleared each time phase B is entered, so a second hold repeats the 180-tick idle and the animation 30.

Confidence: High for the steps and constants; the hold is the same one as the walk-to-point classes.

### 32. Random-point walk, class 29 (Medium)

Owner: class 29. No monster row has class 29 and nothing requests it, so this walk never runs on the disc (High; see Open questions). The routine is a walk to a series of random points around a base point, not a walk along a stored list of points.

It reads three fields of the actor's pattern block (the block at +0x970 of the actor): a count n (the word at block +0x00), a radius r (the float at block +0x10) and a base point (four floats at block +0x20). It keeps a counter k, starting at 0. The source of these three fields is not specified; no row or request fills them, so none of it is reachable on the disc and a clean side omits this walk (unknowable).

At start, if the actor has no current navmesh, it selects one by the position lookup (SPEC-0018) once. Then, while k is below n, one round runs:

1. Draw the goal: the base point with a uniform random offset in [-r, r] added to x and to z (two separate draws of a real number from 0 to 2r, minus r); the height is the base point's. No walkability check is made.
2. If there is still no navmesh, the round skips the walk (nothing moves) and goes to step 4.
3. Otherwise the actor's move target mesh is set to the mesh and a navigation path to the goal is requested. Then each tick: the horizontal distance (height ignored) from the actor to the goal is taken; if it is below 1.0 the actor is placed exactly on the goal (position written, no animation) and the walk ends; otherwise one navigation step toward the goal runs. In every tick of this walk k goes up by 1, and the walk also ends after a tick that began with k already at or above n. One tick of waiting follows each walk tick.
4. Pause: while k is below n and fewer than 60 ticks have been waited, wait one tick (up to 60 ticks). Then wait one more tick and add 1 to k.

When k reaches n the coroutine ends. Because k counts walk ticks as well as points, a small n limits the whole walk, not just the number of points (Medium). No pause follows once k has reached n.

### 33. Class-34 states 4 and 5, eat and leave routines (Medium-High)

Owner: class 34, the states 4 and 5 of the Behavior 27 main loop. These are not class-17 states: class-17 state 4 is the stunned state (Behavior 5). The food rules are also in SPEC-0047.

State 4 (eat the nearest food; animation codes 2 and 4):

1. Search the world item pool for the nearest item of item category 12 (any distance, no readiness test). The search uses the three-dimensional squared distance from the petpet to the item and returns the unit direction to the item with the distance in its fourth lane. If no item is found, the state becomes 0. If the item or its configuration is missing, nothing happens that tick. If the item's type code is 0x91 (the Bagguss anchor), nothing more happens that tick.
2. Otherwise the shared food timer is read, and then the item is reserved as the current food (this clears the timer and the slow flag). The timer is therefore at most one tick's step when it is read, which is below 2.5 seconds.
3. Eat test: the three-dimensional distance to the item is below 2.0, or the timer read in step 2 is below 2.5. Because of step 2 the second part is true on every tick after the first, so the petpet eats at once wherever it stands (High; it walks toward the food on one tick at most, and only when a reservation made by another caller has aged 2.5 seconds with the item 2.0 or more away). If animation 4 is not current, the petpet plays it. Once it is current and has finished, the low-time latch is cleared, the food's value in minutes is read, the item is removed from the pool, the reservation is cleared (-1), the state becomes 0, and the time left T is raised: when T is below value x tick-rate word x 60 (value in minutes, as ticks), both T and the full time F are set to the truncated product (SPEC-0047).
4. Walk branch (only on the rare tick above): the petpet turns toward the horizontal unit direction to the item with weight 0.3 and plays animation 2 (the walk clip moves it by root motion).

State 5 (leave in view of the camera; animation code 3; turn weight 0.1). The test uses the camera (the object behind the run-time word 0x35C7B4), not the hero:

1. A view test runs: c = dot(the camera's vector at +0x20, the unit direction from the camera's point at +0x30 to the actor) must be at least 0.5, that is, the actor is inside a cone of 60 degrees half-angle about the camera's view direction (the same camera vectors as the aim-scatter cone of Behavior 13). The touched flag at +0x238 of the actor's motion record (the contact flag of Behavior 27; Resolved, High; proactive (open close-out)) must also be clear.
2. If either test fails, the petpet is deactivated (SPEC-0047, Deactivate) and the handler returns, leaving the state unchanged.
3. Otherwise the actor turns toward the horizontal reverse of the camera's +0x20 vector (the camera's forward vector times -1, height dropped, renormalised) with turn weight 0.1 and plays animation code 3 (the run clip moves it by root motion). So the pet runs toward the camera until it leaves the cone or touches something (High).

### 52. Spawn facing and what overrides it (every class-17 kind, summons and bosses; High unless marked)

**The rule.** A placed enemy's authored heading is the unit vector at data +0x10 of its kind-3 placement record (SPEC-0006, SPEC-0056 for the same decode on class-6 characters). It is a direction, not an angle. Heading in degrees = atan2(x, z) from the first and third lanes, counted from +Z toward +X and mapped to 0 to 360: +Z is 0, +X 90, -Z 180, -X 270. The inverse is (sin h, 0, cos h). The y lane never changes the heading. Every way of putting an enemy into the world uses this one decode, and nothing adds a world rotation, offset or flip. At the bind it does three things:
- The body's forward axis is the vector normalised with y kept (written once, on the bind tick; on the 36 placements with a nonzero y lane, at most 0.1175, the first drawn tick tilts by at most 6.75 degrees).
- The stored facing is the vector with y set to 0, normalised. Every turn blends from it and every facing test reads it (the notice view cone, the melee arcs, the guard test of the hero's block).
- A home facing keeps the raw vector (object +0x3F0). Only the mode-7 sentry reads it.
From the next tick on, the body's forward axis is rebuilt every tick from the stored facing, with no turn-rate limit, except while the base state is 16 (dead), 33 (script hold), 51 or 55, when the body keeps whatever heading it has (Medium-High).

Disc: 1,989 class-17 placements are streamed in (summon byte 0). Only 279 face exactly +Z; 1,710 have another vector, 906 distinct vectors in all, and each 30-degree sector holds between 108 and 213 of the 1,710. A port in which most enemies share one heading is not reading this field (faults below).

**Which route sets the heading.** All routes end in the same bind.

| How the enemy appears | Heading it starts with |
| --- | --- |
| Streaming bind (hero within 280 units, nearest record first) and forced bind (ActivateEntity, SPEC-0030 row 116; this includes every boss, the Tentacles of Juppie and the Henchmen placed by scripts) | The record's own vector, decoded as above. The ground probe may replace the record's y; it never touches the heading. |
| Bosses | They are ordinary placed records bound by the same path; no boss has its own spawn heading. A script may then move one (table in SPEC-0062, "Where each boss starts"). |
| Hidden or emerging enemies (default-state word 8; 863 of the 1,989, all the Coliseum fighters) | The record's vector. They stand in the hide pose with that heading and do not turn while they wait (state 8, Behavior 40). |
| Summon-reserved records (208 on disc, bound only by spawn-by-key, SPEC-0029 Behavior 31) | The caller's heading, which replaces the record's before the bind. The lobbed summon (Behavior 13) supplies the projectile's flight direction at the landing point (Medium); a broken world object supplies its own direction vector (Medium); the Darkest Faerie's bursts take their positions and headings from her controller (SPEC-0054). 180 of the 208 records hold position (0, 0, 0) and the placeholder (1, 0, 0), heading 90, which is never used; a port that ignores the caller's heading makes every summon face +X. |
| Rebinding after a release (hero more than 280 units away) | The heading the body had at the release (the record is overwritten, SPEC-0006), except mode 7, whose record keeps the authored vector. |
| MoveEntity, locator form, face on (SPEC-0030 row 112) | The move writes the body's axes from the locator's facing and leaves the stored facing alone. With control option 1 (base state 1) the body returns to the stored heading on the next tick. With any other option (base state 33) the body keeps the locator heading until the script releases the entity, then returns to the stored heading unless its behaviour has turned it by then (Medium). The coordinate form and face off change no heading. No script call sets a character's facing directly, apart from WalkEntityAndOrient's end turn (row 108). |
| Petpet | SPEC-0047, Spawn. |

**What takes the heading away from the authored value, and when.** "Tick 0" is the bind tick: the think loop's first pre-step runs on it. Blend weight w is the per-tick fraction of Behavior 48 and the aim rule of SPEC-0040 Behavior 8.

| Override | Takes over | Rate |
| --- | --- | --- |
| None: default word 0 (121 placements), hidden waiting (word 8), standing sentry (word 7 with +0xC4 = 0) | Never by itself. These enemies hold the authored heading until one of the rows below fires. | none |
| Wander (word 5, 460 placements) | The first tick on which no goal is held and the pause timer is below 1, normally tick 0 or 1 (Medium: the pause timer's creation value is not recorded). Steering faces the goal; a pause keeps the last heading. | Behavior 48: 0.3 when settled in the goal's triangle, otherwise 0.1 |
| Patrol (word 6, 222) | Tick 0: the route is set up and the first target (point 1) is chosen on that tick; the first root step is applied on tick 1. A hold keeps the heading of the last steering (Behavior 49). | as wander |
| Follower (word 9, 9) | Tick 0, steering to the followed character. | as wander |
| Sentry returning home (word 7) | When farther than 0.5 from home it walks home (steering). At home with +0xC4 nonzero it aims at the raw authored vector every tick; with +0xC4 = 0 it stands and fidgets with no turn, so it keeps the heading of its walk home. | row aim weight (below) for the aim; steering weights for the walk |
| Notice (states 5, 6, 7, 3, 9; state 0 reaches them through the default word) | First possible on tick T, T being the awareness timer of the row (row +0xC8: 10 Dark Knight and Spirit Sword; 15 Thug; 30 most kinds; 50 Minion Wizard, Skeleton Archer, Skeleton Wizard; 60 Grunt, Archer, Magician, Brute, Artillery, Bandit, Ixi Archer and Ixi Henchman row 157, Restless Spirit, Death Knight, Bone Thrower), then once every T + 2 ticks. The roll, the distance band and the view cone are in Behavior 3. A pass enters state 2, whose handler runs on the same tick and aims at the hero's position from then on. | row aim weight (below) |
| Group alarm (Behavior 3) | Within 15 to 45 ticks after a group mate enters agro; same state 2 aim. A waiting enemy plays its emerge clip instead. | as notice |
| Emerge and agro (state 8 to 19) | The wake test runs from tick 0 (Behavior 40); the emerge clip starts the tick after it passes, and the first agro tick follows the clip. In agro the hold sub-states turn toward the hero and the moving sub-states steer. | row aim weight; steering weights |
| Hit | On the tick the damage lands, even on a hidden enemy (it wakes): turned to the attacker with weight 1.0 (Behavior 37, step 11). | 1.0 (snap) |
| Agro sequencer aims (wind-ups, keep-distance holds, charge) | From the first tick of the sub-state concerned (Behaviors 44 and 13, Movement framework). | row weight, 0.25, 0.1 or 1.0 as listed there |
| Leash, lost sight, retreat home (Behaviors 23, 38, 44) | An enemy sent back to its default state walks home first when its word is 8 or 7 (steering). A hider stops within 0.35 of home and then keeps the heading of its approach, not the authored one. | steering weights |
| Boss controllers and scripts | Per boss, from the fight start or the intro (SPEC-0062 table and each boss spec). | per spec |

Row aim weights (row +0x84 on the ground, +0x88 for a flyer): Minion Grunt 1.0 / 0.8, Minion Archer 0.5 / 0.1, Minion Brute 0.2, Minion Warrior 1.0, Minion Wizard 0 / 0.7, Minion Magician 0 / 0.5, Minion Artillery 0.1, Crokabek 0.5 / 0.5, Dark Knight 0.1, Spirit Sword 1.0, Slorg and Giant Slorg 1.0, Dumb Skeleton, Skeleton Fighter and Skeleton Archer 1.0, Ixi Archer and Ixi Henchman (row 157) 0.7, other Ixi 1.0, Werelupe Basher, Elite and Bone Thrower 0.9 to 1.0, Spyder 0.9, Giant Spyder 0.5, Sludgy 0.2, Tentacle 1.0. A weight of 0 on the ground means the hold sub-states never turn that kind (the Wizard and Magician fly). Ticks needed for the vector blend v = w t + (1 - w) c, renormalised, to bring the heading within 5 degrees of the target, from 30 / 90 / 150 degrees off: w = 0.1: 18 / 30 / 41; w = 0.25: 7 / 11 / 15; w = 0.3: 6 / 9 / 12; w = 0.5: 3 / 5 / 5; w = 1.0: 1 / 1 / 1.

**Telling "authored until noticed" from "always at the hero".** An enemy of word 0, 8 or 7 (with +0xC4 = 0) that has not noticed the hero and has not been hit shows its authored heading for as long as it stands there. A wanderer or patroller shows it for about one tick, then follows its goal. The hero-facing turn exists only in the Notice, Group alarm, Emerge and agro, Hit and Sequencer rows; there is no per-tick "look at the hero" in any idle state. The notice view cone reads the stored facing, so the authored heading also decides how often an enemy standing with its back to the hero notices it.

The six Coliseum ring points of one round (east to north-west) are all within 3.6 degrees of facing the centre: 270.47, 223.35, 179.80, 90.23, 47.72 and 0.00 against 270.00, 223.76, 180.00, 90.00, 44.46 and 0.25 for the direction to the centre. They have emerge distance 40.0, cue word 1 and group 255, so they stand visible in the hide pose, facing inward, until the hero is within 40 x F1 units (28 for an idle hero) or hits one.

**Likely port faults that put every enemy at one heading, and how to spot each.**
1. The heading is taken from the hero, the camera or the world's entry-0 spawn facing instead of the record's vector. Spot: the six Coliseum fighters of one round show six different headings; any single value for all six is this fault.
2. The vector is read as an angle, or turned into a heading with atan2(z, x). Spot: facing (1, 0, 0) must give 90, (0, 0, -1) must give 180, and (0.9998, 0, 0.0212) must give 88.79; atan2(z, x) gives 0, -90 and 1.2.
3. The stored facing starts at a model default (+Z) instead of the flattened record vector, so the first-tick rebuild overwrites the authored heading. Spot: correct on tick 0, every enemy at +Z from tick 1. Also wrong: building the body from the record only on the bind tick.
4. Hidden or idle enemies are given a per-tick look-at-hero or a steering call with no goal. Spot: a Coliseum fighter must keep its inward heading while the hero stands 30 units away idle (above 28), and a word-0 enemy must not turn until it notices the hero.
5. A zero steering direction resets the heading. In the original a zero move direction leaves the stored facing unchanged (the blend of a zero target with weight w only rescales the current vector, and the renormalisation restores it). A port that divides by zero or falls back to +Z when the direction is zero turns a standing enemy to +Z or to garbage the first time its steering runs with no goal. Spot: a word-5 enemy inside its pause must keep its heading.
6. Summons keep the record's placeholder instead of the caller's heading. Spot: every spawned Grunt of the Coliseum summon round faces 90.
7. The release write-back is skipped or writes zero. Spot: walk 300 units away and back; an enemy of word 5, 6 or 8 must return with the heading it had when released (the record is overwritten), and a word-7 sentry with its authored vector.
8. A sentry (word 7) turns to the hero when it should not, or fails to turn back: with +0xC4 = 0 it must never turn (stand and fidget); with +0xC4 nonzero it must re-aim at the authored vector at its row weight after a chase.
9. The ground probe or slope rebuild recomputes the body from a fixed reference and loses the yaw. Spot: the same heading on flat and sloped ground (vector with y 0).
10. MoveEntity with face on is made permanent, or ignored. A scripted move with control option 1 shows the locator heading for one tick only; with option 0 (state 33) it persists until release.

## Edge cases and error handling

Class 17:

- Notice band: a hero closer than the band's near edge is noticed as soon as roll A and the sight gate pass, with no chance roll (Behavior 3, step 5). A hero beyond the far edge is never noticed. With the sight line blocked, the near and far edges use F2, which is 0.0 in some hero action states (Behavior 18), so the blocked band is then empty.
- Notice interpolation uses the un-scaled band values while the range test uses the scaled ones (a reproducible quirk).
- Halving: when the line-of-sight test fails and the Always-has-line-of-sight flag is 0, the band is recomputed from the un-halved values, which discards any socialize halving (reproduce as written).
- Charge distance draws: the charge distances of all four slots are drawn from the selected slot's charge range (reproduce as written). At creation the selected slot is -1, so the draw reads the two row words just below slot 0's charge range: 0x258 (the last charge-type word, read as a float) and 0x268 (reproduce as written).
- Counter charge: the counter-attack choice does not redraw the distances, so a counter charge runs to the charge distance of the last draw, which used the range of the slot the normal choice picked last (or the creation-time words). A slot with a charge range of 0 to 0 picked last gives a counter charge of length 0.0, which ends at once unless the hero is already within the melee range (Test vectors, Poison Spyder 6).
- Trigger range is strict on both sides: a hero exactly at the trigger minimum or maximum does not trigger the slot.
- Normal attack trigger chance endpoints: the roll is an integer from 1 to 100 and must be strictly below the integer threshold. Thus threshold 100 passes for 99 outcomes and fails on 100; thresholds 0 and 1 never pass. For example, the Flower Monster row's 100-percent slot is not guaranteed by this roll. This differs from the counter-attack choice, whose 0-to-100 roll is also compared strictly below its chance (Behavior 14).
- Slot cooldowns: a slot's cooldown is set when the slot is chosen and set again at the sequencer's end path (Behavior 13). The cooldowns count down only inside the normal choice and only while the hero is attackable; while the hero is not attackable they do not move.
- Retarget with countdown -1 never retargets.
- Timer units: every countdown in this spec is in ticks. A row value of 120 is 2 seconds. Do not multiply row timer values by 60 again.
- Death disappear timer (row 0x80): a count of ticks (Behavior 34 and 36).
- Approach routine other than 1, 3 or 4: the agro machine keeps that value as its outer sub-state, which has no case, so the enemy has no archetype movement and the normal choice never runs (its outer sub-state gate fails). Only 1, 3 and 4 occur on disc.
- Display in default 0: the setter for state 2 gives state 21 unless the enemy's invisibility-off word is set. That word is 0 at creation and only DisableInvisibility (SPEC-0030 row 121) sets it, at 25 disc sites; with it set, the setter and SetAI_Agro enter state 2 normally (High). For these rows the setter stores no search countdown and no last-seen point; state 21 runs on the creation values (countdown -1, point at the origin; the point changes only through a lost-sight search). The alarm check also sends these rows to state 21, so they need their own notice pass, and only members in state 8 emerge regardless of distance (Behavior 3).
- Invisible kinds and hits (Medium-High): an enemy with Display in default 0 whose invisibility-off word is clear is not a hit candidate while it is in its default state, or in state 21 while it is not playing animation 82. The exclusion does not apply while the selected hero's outfit slot holds the Tattered Cloak (item 126): the enemy is then hittable in every state the base hit rule allows. Once DisableInvisibility has run for the enemy, the exclusion never applies.
- Row 0x4FC (strafe erratic) is never read.
- Sequencer states 10 and 11 have no writer and are never reached.
- Attack speed words (High for same-update clip rate): the wind-up, attack and recovery speeds (rows 0x1BC, 0x1CC, 0x1DC + 4k) are stored directly to P by the behavior coroutine after reset/status processing and before the clip re-time (Behavior 1). The written value sets that update's clip rate, replacing earlier status multipliers. The animation pass then evaluates at that rate; its root step is consumed by a later motion-record pass (Behavior 48, item 5).
- Sequencer spawn with the projectile pool full: the spawner creates nothing, but the shot counter still goes up and the pending flag is set as usual, so a full pool costs the enemy that shot (Behavior 13).
- Shot counter and trail: the trail effect entry follows the shot counter, so a shot lost to a full pool still advances the trail entry of the next shot (Behavior 13).
- Sequencer spawn count 0: one projectile is still fired; the counter (1) is not below the count (0), so the loop then goes to state 3 (Behavior 13).
- Flight mode other than 0, 1 or 2 on a missile slot without flag 0x02: state 2 spawns nothing but still counts the shot (Behavior 13).
- Speed ramp outside the trigger band: u is not clamped, so the ramp speed leaves the range [v_end, v] when the target is outside the band (Behavior 13; reproduce it).
- Homing missiles (flight mode 2) live twice the straight lifetime but fly at no more than 1 unit per tick after the first tick (SPEC-0029 Behavior 9).
- Spells (Behavior 43) deal no damage on contact except for sub-type 5's later full-health effect; sub-type 5 also deals immediate missing-health damage when it builds the spell, and only if a projectile slot is free.
- Stun from a blocked charge never counts down (quirk): hit-reaction kind 2 waits for the stun countdown (0xBF0) to reach 0, and nothing counts that countdown down. The enemy stays in the stun until a new hit starts another reaction (allowed when the stun type is not 1) or a code-6 counter-attack starts the sequencer. The Drackonack rows 144 and 145 reach this when their charge is blocked. Reproduce.
- Leash steer-and-stop branch is almost dead (quirk): the agro tail's band test needs the selected slot index 1, but the normal choice sets the selected slot to -1 earlier in the same tick in outer sub-states 1, 3 and 4. It can run only when the hero is not attackable, because then the normal choice returns before clearing the slot and a slot 1 left over from the last attack stays selected (Behavior 23; Medium-High). A rewrite that keeps the slot-clearing order reproduces both.
- Counter-attack choice: trigger codes 0 and -1 never pass, and a chance of 0 never passes. The roll is inclusive from 0 to 100, so a chance of 100 passes on 100 of the 101 rolls. One roll is drawn per tick, for the last eligible slot only; a slot that is already the selected slot is not rolled. Reproduce as written.
- Both choices in one tick: the counter-attack choice runs after the normal choice, so when both pick a slot in the same tick, the counter choice's slot and start state replace the normal choice's, and the distances keep the normal choice's redraw (Medium).
- Counter code 1 needs only the hero recover window (table-B bit 0x800 on the active hero's clips); it is eligible in any outer sub-state.
- Missile defence: the block roll is made after the dodge roll, so when both pass the block (outer 8) wins.
- Melee defence: the block test is "at most" and the dodge test "below": a block chance of 0 never blocks (r1 is at least 1), and a dodge chance of 100 always dodges when the block fails.
- Distance-hide gate on approach-4 rows (spyders, rows 233 to 236): never fires, because the gate runs only in outer sub-state 1; the row flag still makes state 8 hide in place.
- Stun on alignment 4: a stun status is not applied to an enemy whose row alignment (0x3C) is 4 (Medium).
- Animation lookups (Behavior 19): -1 means no animation. The caller must not play index -1.
- Action-19 gate (Behavior 21): a nonzero class test makes the gate return 0. A 0 result (blocked) makes the pre-step skip the target re-pick and the write of state 22; a pass does both (Behavior 21; Open question 54).
- Value tables (Behavior 26): the lookups are described as 1-based. The result for other indices is not described.
- Second death while reanimating: a second death while the reanimation countdown is active does not restart it.
- Refused rise: a rise refused by the animation query heals to full health and returns to agro at once.
- Flying hit values of 3 or more: a flying enemy with a flying hit value of 3 or more gets no hit reaction on a hit.
- Sub-type 5 damage: sub-type 5 applies its damage to the target before its projectile is built, but only when a projectile slot is free. If the projectile pool is full, there is no damage and no projectile.
- Class state after death: non-reanimating enemies keep their class state after death, because the think loop is not stepped in base state 16 (Behavior 1).
- Lying reanimating enemy: it cannot be hit while in class state 16, and it is never released by its death countdown (Behavior 35).
- Hit-count threshold: never reached in play, since the threshold stays -1 (Behavior 37, step 12).
- Released at full health (Behavior 47): an enemy damaged to 1 hit point and then streamed out comes back at full health where it was released.
- Record without an entity entry (Behavior 47; SPEC-0006): a script-slot record with no entry in the pack's entity table has no kill bit, so it is bound again after a kill whenever its slot streams.
- Footstep memory never reset (quirk): the monster's footstep step (shake and rumble on table-B bits 0x80 and 0x100; SPEC-0029 Behavior 32) remembers the last frame it handled, so a frame is handled once. The reset of that memory on an animation change never happens, because the request compares the new id with the stored id after storing it, so the two are always equal. A new clip whose first footstep frame has the same number as the last frame handled in the old clip skips that footstep.
- Missing animation ids: only the slot attack ids and the fidgets are skipped when missing; any other missing id starts clip 0 of the main set (Layout section 6). On the one 138-entry table, ids 138 to 165 (for example the flyer emerge code 165) all start clip 0.
- Counter-attack against Roberta: trigger codes 1 to 3 never pass while Roberta is the active hero, because her clips carry no recover bit (Behavior 14).
- Strike test, contact: the weapon cylinders are tested against the hero's bone capsules only (Tor nine, Roberta one, all small), with no distance, arc, facing, height or sight test and no hittable query (Behavior 13, Weapon test). A port that tests a distance from the enemy's centre to the hero's centre is wrong in both directions: the reach is wherever the clip puts the attachment frames.
- Strike test, zero damage: a strike whose computed damage is 0 does nothing (no take-hit, no hit effect, no status, no theft, no knockback); on the open path the hero is not added to the hit list, so it is tested again on the next window tick (Behavior 13, step 6). This includes status-only melee slots whose damage word is reduced to 0 by armour.
- Strike test, guard: only a hero with dot(hero facing, attacker facing) at most -0.5 (or Roberta spinning) is blocked. A guarding hero hit from the side or behind takes the full strike and flinches. Unblockable slots (flag 0x01) ignore the guard and also ignore the spin.
- Strike test, hit list: the check comes before the weapon test; the add comes after the take-hit; a blocked non-spinning strike adds the hero twice (the list has ten places, so repeated multi-window blocks fill it); the list is cleared at every attack start (event 2 of the outer entry) and whenever state 3 re-enters state 2 for a new window. A hero that dies or is knocked away is still in the list until the next attack.
- Strike test, return and transitions: no contact in state 2 waits for the strike clip to finish (progress 0.99) and then goes to state 5; a hit goes to state 3 on the tick it lands even when the clip finishes on that tick; a blocked result goes to state 4 (state 9 in a charge) and plays the hero-bank sound cue on that tick.
- Loop strike (state 14) and the grab throw bypass the strike test: the loop strike calls the hero's take-hit directly on its timer with no guard test, no hit list, no knockback and no hit effect; the grab throw reports a hit with damage 0 (Behavior 15).
- Redraw of the distances: the retreat distance is drawn only by approach routine 3, before the desired distance; see Behavior 4 for the draw order.
- Same-tick exchange: the enemy's strike lands in the region loop, before the hero's update of the same tick (Behavior 50), so the enemy that lands on tick T pre-empts the hero's swing of tick T, and a hero swing that lands on tick T reaches the enemy's think on tick T + 1.

Routines of other classes (kept for reference; not class 17):

- Class 7, pen entry (Behavior 16, step 1): the entry does not write the run mode word. The 30-tick value is the lead timer, read by the lead routine from the next tick. A Whinny that leads never flees again, so the stale run mode is never used (High).
- Class 7, chase timers: the timer is reduced before the end test, so a timer of 1 at the start of a tick ends mode 1 or mode 2 in that tick (Medium).
- Class 7, chase and blocked flag: mode 1 and mode 2 end by clearing the actor's blocked flag as well, so a run that ends for any reason releases the flag (Medium).
- Class 21, flee with the touched flag set and a far hero (Behavior 17): with the flag set and d above 20.0, the controller enters mode 1 (with a radius-10.0 pick) and then mode 2 in the same tick, and the record ends in mode 2 with the radius-30.0 point (High).
- Class 21, flee mode 2 exit (Behavior 17): the exit sets the Miamouse main state to 0 and leaves the mode word at 2; the next flee entry resets it to 0 (High).
- Class 21, flee boundaries: mode 0 takes the away step when d is at most 20.0 (inclusive); mode 1 returns to mode 0 when d is below 20.0 (strict); a point distance of exactly 0.5 is not an arrival (High).
- Class 34, selector: cooldown B is compared with a threshold that uses the tick-rate word and a factor of 0.5; cooldown B counts ticks of remaining petpet time, so the ratio is 1 at 1800 ticks, 30 seconds (Resolved, Behavior 27).
- Class 7, band controller: the 3.0 hold is reached only if the distance of step 3 of Behavior 29 is measured differently from the 5.0 test. Reproduce as written.
- Class 7, wait-and-approach: a target point within 1.5 units ends the approach on the same tick, before any steering in that tick.
- Class 29, random-point walk: no monster row has this class, so the walk never runs on the disc. Dormant rule (Medium): with no navmesh found, nothing moves, but the pause after each point still runs.
- Patrol (state 6, Behavior 49): a hold of 1 tick or less does not stop the enemy; a pause draw P gives P - 1 hold ticks. A random stop does not advance the point. A one-point path in back-and-forth mode alternates between the entry before the path and the first entry. An invalid path index sends the enemy to entry 0 of the pack, where it rests for ever. A fidget counter does not count down for a table without animation 6 (flyers 142). A Display-0 row replays animation 81 on every return to the patrol. The route restarts at point 1 only after a streaming release and rebind.
- Class 34, state 4 with no object: the state returns to 0 on the same tick.
- Class 35, charge and wait: the wait length depends on the tick-rate word, so a different tick rate changes its length.
- Class 6, leader cooldown at the limit: a second count of 1 becomes 0 after the decrement, so the search runs. A count of 2 becomes 1, so no search runs.
- Class 6, no match: the cooldowns still decrement, and no leader field or state changes.

## Test vectors

Synthetic and disc-based, hand-checkable:

- Socialize pull of a hidden enemy (disc, pack 986, the Minion corps; High for the rule, Medium for the same-mesh premise): entity 8 is a Minion Warrior (row 208) in default state 7, group 1; entity 10 is a Minion Warrior (row 205) in default state 8, group 1, emerge distance 10.0, cue word 0, so it is hidden. Both rows carry the socialize flag and the pair value 20.0. Entity 10 stands at (899.98, 121.38, 42.24) and entity 8 at (897.75, 121.38, 52.38): horizontal separation sqrt(2.23^2 + 10.14^2) = 10.38, within the pair reach 20.0 + 20.0 = 40.0. On the first tick after both are bound, with the hero beyond notice range and both on one mesh, entity 8's quiet countdown goes from 0 to -1 and its scan finds entity 10 (state 8 is not excluded, quiet countdown 0 is below 1): entity 10 is set to state 3 and entity 8 follows into state 3 in the same tick, whatever the hero's distance (entity 10's emerge distance of 10.0 plays no part). Each walks toward the other and stops when its own separation falls below 6.0; at that stop it draws a talk time from [120, 180) ticks and counts it down by 1 per tick, so a draw of 150 ends the chat 150 ticks after the stop; the end draws quiet [420, 480) and a fresh talk value, and entity 10 returns to hiding at home (state 8, sub-state 0) while entity 8 returns to its sentry post. Entity 10 never entered agro and its group was never alerted.
- Proximity retarget: P = (0,0,0), F = (1,0,0), Q = (6,0,0), r = 1 gives gate length 0 < 1 and A = (−3,0,0); Q = (7,0,0) gives gate length 1, so strict equality does not update the target.
- Notice band: Dumb Skeleton, Awareness 65, band 3 to 5 (chance 50 at 3, 1 at 5), hero action state with F1 = 1.0, sight line clear. A hero at 2 units: noticed when roll A is at most 65 (closer than the near edge). At 3 units: chance 50. At 4 units: chance 25.5 (interpolated; before the cone or hidden halvings). The same hero at 4 units in hero action state 1 (F1 = 0.7, band 2.1 to 3.5): not noticed (beyond the far edge).
- Cone: with View angle 0.707 and a hero at 45 degrees off facing (cosine 0.707, not below 0.707), no halving; at 60 degrees (cosine 0.5), the chance is halved.
- Approach band: approach 3, desired min 15, desired max 25, random integer 50 (f = 0.5): desired = 20 units. Retreat band 20 to 30: a draw of 25.
- Trigger: Dumb Skeleton has two melee slots with trigger band 0 to 10 units. Slot 2 is tested first (arc 0.71, chance 40, delay 60 to 120 ticks), then slot 1 (arc 0.92, chance 60, delay 30 to 240 ticks). A hero at 0 units: no slot (strict minimum 0). At 5 units, facing cosine 0.95, roll 30: slot 2 is chosen (30 is below 40 and 0.95 is above 0.71); its cooldown is drawn in [60, 120] ticks, and the sequencer starts in state 0 (no charge).
- Priority: an enemy with slots 1 and 4 both passing selects slot 4.
- Timer: a search timer draw of 90 ticks is 1.5 seconds; a recovery draw of 150 ticks is 2.5 seconds.
- Idle holds: an idle variant drawn for 300 ticks changes after 5 seconds.
- Strafe hold (synthetic): outer sub-state 4, inner 10 with the hold at 1. The hold becomes 0, r = 3 and the strafe lock is 0: inner 9 with a new hold from 60 to 180 ticks. With the lock at 50, r = 3 gives inner 10 again. Circling into an obstacle (blocked flag) gives inner 10 and a lock of 120 ticks.
- Sequencer approach and aim: melee slot range 10, distance 9.5, hero and steer target on the walk mesh: state 1 at once, whatever the facing. With the hero off the walk mesh and a facing cosine of 0.96 (at least 0.95): state 1. With a facing cosine of 0.9: stays in state 0 and turns with blend 0.1. At distance 12 with both on the mesh: the enemy pursues and stays in state 0.
- Horizontal facing tests (R-0198; High): an enemy at the origin facing +X, hero at (4, 3, 0). The horizontal direction is (1, 0, 0) with d = 4.0, so the cosine is 1.0: state 0 test (at least 0.95) passes, the normal choice's arc test (step 5c) passes for any arc below 1.0, and the counter-attack arc test passes likewise. A 3-D reading would give 0.8 (5.0 away) and fail all three at arcs of 0.95 and 0.92, and pass only arcs below 0.8 (0.71 for example).
- Same, real values (Slorg, monster row 229, the kind of pack 757 entity 3 and pack 990 entity 22; table slot 0 missile, band 5.0 to 20.0, arc 0; slot 1 melee, band 0 to 6.0, arc 0.9239 (22.5 degrees), melee range 3.0, chance 75; slot 2 melee, band 6.0 to 30.0, arc 0.9239, melee range 12.0, chance 50): the Slorg faces the hero straight on and the hero stands 5.0 away horizontally and 4.0 above, off the walk mesh. Normal choice: slot 2 fails its band (5.0 is not above 6.0), slot 1 passes (band, cosine 1.0, roll below 75). State 0: melee, d = 5.0 above the range 3.0, so it pursues, and the hero being off the mesh sends it to the facing test: 1.0 passes, state 1. In 3-D the distance is 6.4 and the cosine 0.78, so slot 1 fails its band, slot 2 passes its band but fails its arc, and no attack starts. For a straight-facing Slorg no height makes a horizontal test fail; a 3-D reading would fail the 0.95 test above a height of 0.329 times the horizontal distance (1.64 at 5.0) and the 0.9239 arc above 0.414 times it (2.07 at 5.0). What does end the attack at a large height difference is the lost-sight test: the two sight points more than 50.0 units apart in height start outer 9 at once.
- Sequencer spawn count: count 3. Spawn event active on ticks 1, 2, inactive on tick 3, active on ticks 4 and 5, inactive on tick 6, active on tick 7. Tick 1 fires shot 1 (counter 1, pending set). Tick 2: still pending, nothing. Tick 3: the pending flag clears. Tick 4 fires shot 2 (counter 2). Tick 6 clears the flag; tick 7 fires shot 3, the counter reaches 3 and the loop goes to state 3.
- Sequencer spawn count 0 (synthetic): one projectile is fired on the first active spawn event, then the loop goes to state 3.
- Minion Archer (row 190, slot 3; flight mode 0): launch speed 2.0 and range 70 give a lifetime of 35 ticks. Launch (0, 2, 0), hero at (0, 0, 20), no scatter: aim (0, 2.5, 20), velocity (0, 0.04998, 1.99938) per tick, damage 10 x 1.0 = 10, status 7 (fire) at chance 100 with value 10 to 30. One attack fires one arrow: the strike clip has a single spawn-event window (key 14, active on s + 56 to s + 59 at speed 1.0), and a second shot needs a second window, so the spawn count of 2 is never reached (full flight in SPEC-0029 Test vectors).
- Flower Monster seed (row 149, slot 0; flight mode 1): v 0.5, v_end 0.1, band 3 to 60. Launch (0, 1, 0), hero at (20, 0, 0): d = 20, dy = -1, u = 0.70175, s = 0.30302, T = 66.003 ticks, vy = 0.27820, lifetime trunc(60 T) + 1 = 3961 ticks (full flight in SPEC-0029 Test vectors).
- Homing (row 213, slot 1; flight mode 2): launch speed 1.0 and range 100 give a lifetime of 2 x 100 = 200 ticks. Each tick the velocity becomes 0.75 of the old unit direction plus 0.25 of the unit direction to the hero + (0, 2, 0) (SPEC-0029 Test vectors).
- Spell lifetime (Behavior 43): a launch-to-aim distance of 12.7 gives 12 + 5 = 17 ticks at 1.0 unit per tick.
- Aim scatter (synthetic): row inaccuracy 0, enemy inside the camera cone, not waiting in line: e = 0 and the aim point is the target point. The same enemy outside the camera cone (dot 0.8): e = 5.0.
- Sequencer loop: state 13 sets a countdown of 60 ticks (one second) when the hero is within the melee range; state 14 counts it down and damages the hero on its 61st tick, when the countdown is 0, then reloads it from the loop interval range.
- Counter-attack, one slot only (synthetic): slot 2 is the only eligible slot. Its trigger code is 6 and its chance is 40. The enemy is in agro outer sub-state 10 (hit reaction), the hero is at distance 6 inside the bounds 0 to 10, and the facing cosine is 0.95 (above the arc 0.71). A roll of 39 chooses slot 2 and a roll of 40 does not.
- Counter-attack, chance 100 (synthetic): a roll of 99 chooses the slot; a roll of 100 does not.
- Counter-attack, recover window (disc): against Tor's swing id 14 (its frames OR to 0xDC1), a slot with code 1 is eligible on the frames that carry 0x800; against Roberta's id 14 (frames OR to 0x1A1, no 0x800) it is never eligible.
- Slot ids (synthetic): slot k = 2 gives wind-up 43, strike 44, follow-through 45, blocked 46, recovery 47. An enemy whose table has 44 but not 43 skips the wind-up and starts the strike; a walk request for id 2 on a table without it starts clip 0.
- Counter-attack, trigger tests (synthetic): with the hero recover window open, a slot with code 2 is eligible in outer sub-state 6 and not in 7; a slot with code 3 is eligible in outer sub-state 8 and not in 5. Without the window neither is eligible. A slot with code -1 is never eligible. With slots 1 and 3 both eligible, slot 3 is the one rolled for.
- Spacing (synthetic): a candidate 5.0 units from the hero and 6.0 from the nearest other pool object passes. At 4.9 units from one object it fails.
- Animation lookup: group B, index 3 gives 0x1D; group D, index 2 gives 0x14; group E, index 1 gives -1.
- Attack code (synthetic): selected attack slot 2 gives 0x2E in group 2 if the availability test passes, and -1 if it fails.
- Band factors: hero action state 4 gives F1 1.75 and F2 0.9. An action state not in the table (for example 7) gives F1 1.0 and F2 0.5.
- Leash and distance-hide gate: see Behavior 23 and Behavior 24.

Routines of other classes (classes 7, 21, 34, 35, 36, 29 and 6; synthetic; invented distances):

- Chase pool (synthetic): the pool search returns index 5 and the owner id at index 5 is -1. The controller takes point 5, sets the pick timer to 30, the counter to 0, the owner id to -1, and sets the claimed flag for index 5.
- Chase mode 1 timer: with the frame-rate word 60, the timer starts at 15 ticks. With the word 120, it starts at 30 ticks.
- Chase mode 1 arrival: a horizontal distance of 2.5 to the point keeps the run going (the test is strict, below 2.5). A distance of 2.4 ends it at once.
- Chase mode 1 timer end: a timer of 1 at the start of a tick is reduced to 0 and the run ends that tick.
- Chase mode 2: the timer starts at 25 ticks. A distance of 2.0 keeps the run going while the timer is at least 1; a distance of 1.9 ends it.
- Chase counter: a horizontal distance of 8.1 to the target sets the counter to 0. A distance of 8.0 does not.
- Chase pick: with the candidate walkable and the blocked flag clear, mode 1 is chosen. With the candidate not walkable, mode 2 is chosen with a 25-tick timer. With the candidate walkable but the blocked flag set, mode 2 is chosen.
- Approach mode 0 (synthetic): steering flag clear, d = 20.0, candidate walkable: the actor moves toward the candidate and the mode stays 0. With d = 20.1: mode 2 with the point 30.0 units out.
- Approach, flag set and d = 25 (synthetic): the record ends in mode 2 with the 30.0 point.
- Approach mode 1 (synthetic): direction test 0.6 and flag clear: keeps running. Direction test 0.6 with the flag set: not kept. With d = 15 then: mode 0.
- Approach mode 2 (synthetic): direction test 0.4: the done word becomes 0 and the mode word stays 2.
- Band controller, close band: d = 5.0 is not below 5.0, so it does not close. d = 4.9 sets the state to 2 and the flag to 1.
- Band controller, approach: with mode 0 and the steering flag clear, d = 10.0 selects movement class 0 (not above 10.0). d = 10.1 selects movement class 2.
- Re-pick timer: with mode 1 and the timer at 0, the mode is cleared and a new timer is drawn in [120, 400] (both ends included). With the timer at 1 the actor waits one tick (animation code 0) and the timer becomes 0.
- Wait-and-approach: done 0 and d = 1.5 gives done 1 on that tick. d = 1.6 keeps done 0 and the actor walks.
- Charge and wait: with the counter at 180 (at 60 ticks per second) the phase B wait ends. Distance 12.0 with a direction test of 0 keeps phase B. Distance 12.1 returns to phase A.
- Patrol list: with three points, the actor pauses 60 ticks after the first and second arrivals and does not pause after the third.
- Idle coroutine: with period P = 400 and counter 400, the actor plays animation code 1 on that tick.
- State 5: a direction test value of 0.49 runs the release helper. A value of 0.5 steers at speed 0.1.
1. Leader handover (synthetic): enemy id 5 and other id 9 on the same navigation mesh (equal mesh index) and within range; the candidate has +0xC98 = 0, +0x3B0 = 3 and +0xA34 = 0, with the second cooldown at 1. After the call both AI states are 3, the enemy's leader field is 9, and the other's leader field is 5.
2. Leader candidate filter (synthetic): with +0xC98 = 1, candidate is rejected; with +0xC98 = 0 and +0x3B0 = 0x70, candidate is rejected; with +0xC98 = 0, +0x3B0 = 3 and +0xA34 = 3, candidate is rejected. Values +0xC98 = 0, +0x3B0 = 3 and +0xA34 = 0 pass this filter and proceed to the group and range tests.
3. Leader cooldown (synthetic): second cooldown at 2 becomes 1, and no search runs. The cooldown at 1 becomes 0, and the search runs.
4. Pairing reset (synthetic, at 60 Hz): a draw of 600 sets +0xA9C to 600 ticks (10 seconds); a draw of 1800 sets it to 1800 ticks (30 seconds). In either case, the pairing sub-state becomes 0 and the AI state becomes 0.

- Reanimation: a Dumb Skeleton (row 226) killed at tick 0 draws a countdown N in [8000, 12000]. It lies down with animation 58 for N + 1 ticks, then rises when clear. With N = 2: animation 58 on ticks 1, 2 and 3, and the rise starts on tick 4 if the area is clear.
- Death timer: row 183 has a death disappear timer of 1. If its death clip reaches progress 0.95 on tick T, the countdown is 0 at T and -1 at T + 1, so the enemy counts as gone from T + 1.
- Take-hit, agro: an enemy in agro (state 19) takes a hit of 6 with the hit-lock timer at zero. Damage 6 applies, and the agro outer sub-state becomes 10 with hit-reaction kind 0.
- Take-hit, non-agro: a hit of 6 on an enemy in state 17 with hit-lock 0 doubles to 12, sets state 19 and applies 12. With the same inputs but hit-lock nonzero, state 19 is still set and the internal value is doubled, but HP does not change; the next hit in state 19 is not doubled.
- Flying hit choice (synthetic): a flying enemy with a flying hit value of 1, a retarget countdown of 0 and no animation playing takes a hit with the caller's extra flag at zero. It clears the stored flag and starts hit-reaction kind 7 (outer sub-state 10). With a flying hit value of 0 it starts kind 6 instead.
- Sub-type 5 (synthetic): an attacker with maximum HP 50 and current HP 20 that reaches its event, with a valid target and a free projectile slot, deals 30 immediate damage and submits one spell. With a full pool it deals no damage and submits no spell. With no valid target it also produces no damage or spell. If the created spell later hits, the target is set to maximum HP.
- Map search type to sub-type (synthetic): search type 3 selects sub-type 1; search type 7 selects sub-type 5.
- Map-search type 2 target (synthetic): a visible class-17 candidate in state 16, playing its reanimation death clip rather than animation 4, with countdown 9000 and HP 0 of 50 passes the picker when within the caster's map-search distance. State 16 and the positive countdown do not disqualify it; setting its HP to 50 or selecting animation 4 does.
- Kill memory (synthetic, Behavior 47): kill the enemy of record 3 of a world, leave the world and enter it again. Record 3 is not bound again. Repeat with a Dumb Skeleton (row 226) on record 3: it is bound again.
- Release and return (synthetic, Behavior 47): an enemy with 50 maximum hit points at 10 hit points is released when the hero is 281 units away; when the hero comes back to 279 units, with a free object and the block below its cap, it is bound at the release point with 50 hit points.
- Game load (synthetic, Behavior 47): enter world A, kill record 3, then load a save made before entering A. Record 3 of A is alive again.
- Arrival (synthetic): a type 1 target at distance 0.8 whose type word is 0xBE and whose subtype is not 0x11 is consumed with a consume flag of 4. The target index is cleared and the idle pick plays.

Poison Spyder (row 235), a worked example with disc values (High for the row and placement values):

Note on this example: its slot numbers count from 1, so slot 1 is table slot 0 and slot 3 is table slot 2 (slot numbering as corrected in SPEC-0017 Open question 17). The Poison Spyder's own action table has no clip for the wind-up ids (33, 43), the follow-through id (35), id 37 or ids 40 and 48 to 52. The sequencer skips each of those phases in the same tick (Behavior 13). So the swing is the strike clip plus the end path, and the charge goes straight to its run clip, then the bite clip (SPEC-0017, spider animation ids and durations). Where the vectors below name a wind-up (A) or follow-through (C) phase, that phase takes no ticks for this enemy.

Row and placements: 70 placements in resources 697, 698, 796, 865, 903, 904, 948, 1080, 1244, 1245, 1250, 1257 and 1265; default-state word 8 on 47 of them, 7 on 14, 5 on 6, 0 on 2 and 9 on 1. Hit points 16, approach routine 4, difficulty value 8, display in default 1, distance hide flag 1 (distance 50.0, chance 90). Awareness 100 every 30 ticks, view angle 0.707, sense range 5 to 30, chance 75 at the near edge and 25 at the far edge. Missile defend time 15, missile dodge 100, missile block 0; melee dodge 25, 50 and 75 (quick, normal, heavy), melee block 0; defence timer 1 to 30. Strafe desired distance 4 to 15. Attack timer (recovery) 15 to 30. Slot 1: melee, band 0 to 8, chance 25, arc 0.9239, melee range 3, delay 30 to 60, no charge (charge range 0 to 0), poison chance 25 for 600 to 1800 ticks. Slot 3: melee with charge type 2, band 10 to 35, chance 50, arc 0.707, delay 0 to 0, charge distance 30 to 40, counter attack trigger 1 with chance 75, poison chance 50.

Vectors (synthetic positions; hero in action state 1, so F1 = 0.7):
1. Hidden in state 8 with the spawn record's emerge distance 10.0 and an idle hero (F1 = 0.7): a hero at 7.0 horizontal units leaves it hidden; at 6.99 units sub-state 1 starts; on the next tick animation 10 plays; when it has finished the enemy is in agro. The same hero 30 units above it, at 6.99 horizontal units, also starts it. With emerge distance 0 no distance starts it (hero at 0.0 units included). With emerge distance 1.0 and a running hero (F1 = 1.75) the threshold is 1.75; with an idle hero 0.7 (0.69 starts it, 0.7 does not).
2. Wander state: band 3.5 to 21. A hero at 3.0 units (closer than 3.5) with roll A 40: noticed. At 10 units in the cone: chance 65; roll B 64 notices, 65 does not. At 21.5 units: never.
3. First agro ticks: on the entry tick the outer sub-state is 0; on tick 2 it is 4 with inner 10 and hold 0; on tick 3 the hold becomes -1, r = 2 gives inner 8 with a new hold (for example 100). At d = 9 no slot passes (9 is not below 8 for slot 1, and not above 10 for slot 3).
4. Choice: d = 12, facing cosine 0.8, roll 30: slot 3 passes (30 below 50); its cooldown is 0; the desired distance = 4 + 11 r / 100 (r = 50 gives 9.5); the charge distances come from float[30, 40) (for example 35.0); the sequencer starts in state 6.
5. Charge: A to its end, then B and state 7. With the hero reached at 2.9 units (within the melee range 3): state 8, C with strike tests, C finished: state 12, E, then outer 0 with the recovery drawn from 15 to 30. With the hero staying 5.0 units away: after 35.0 units travelled, state 12.
6. Counter: the hero recover window open with slot 3 eligible and not selected: roll 74 chooses it, 75 does not. If the last normal choice was slot 1, slot 3's charge distance is 0.0 (slot 1's range is 0 to 0), so the counter charge ends at once unless the hero is within 3.0 units.
7. Melee dodge: on a normal swing, r2 = 49 gives outer 5 with the defence hold 15; r2 = 50 gives no reaction. On a heavy swing r2 = 74 dodges.
8. Missile dodge: a hero shot whose 15-tick path meets the body: roll 99 gives outer 6; roll 100 gives no reaction.
9. Leash: no route, the walk waypoint 15.0 units from the hero for 181 ticks: the setter runs with 0 and gives state 0 (display in default 1); on the next tick the pre-step runs the setter with the default-state word 8; state 8 hides in place because the distance hide flag is 1.
10. Bite poison: roll 24 poisons, 25 does not; a duration draw of 1234 ticks gives 20 seconds (1234 / 60, truncated).

Rogue (row 153), attack cadence with the hero standing still and within reach (disc values; High for the clip and tick arithmetic, Medium for the whole cycle, whose approach and roll waits vary). Row 153: approach 4, strafe band 6 to 20, recovery 15 to 30. Slot 0: band 0 to 10, chance 50, arc 0.924, delay 30 to 60, knockback 0. Slot 1 unused. Slot 2: band 5 to 20, chance 50, charge kind 2, charge distance 15 to 25, delay 60 to 120, steal flag. Slot 3: band 5 to 15, chance 25, arc 0, delay 0 to 0, unblockable, knockback 0. Every speed word is 1.0, so every clip runs at 0.25 keys per tick. The table ids are in Layout section 6.
1. Slot 3 swing. Tick s is the first tick of state 1. Id 48 has no clip, so B (id 49, clip 38) is requested in tick s and the state is 2. Strike windows are open in ticks s + 16 to s + 31, s + 40 to s + 51 and s + 60 to s + 71. Hits land in ticks s + 17 (the first window tick on which the weapon touches an idle, level Tor straight in front; the window opens on s + 16), s + 41 (the weapon already touches such a hero on s + 40, but state 3 sends the sequencer back to state 2 only in tick s + 40, so the strike test first runs on s + 41; a window whose predecessor missed is tested while state 2 is still running and hits on its touching tick) and s + 61 (the same one-tick delay after the second hit; the weapon touches on s + 60). B passes its gate in tick s + 81 (state 5); in tick s + 82 E (id 52) is missing, so the end path runs: outer 0, recovery N = int[15, 30], slot 3 cooldown 0. The next choice can run from tick s + 82 + N (s + 97 to s + 112), once the hero is attackable again.
2. Slot 0 swing. B (id 34, clip 8, looping) requested in tick s; one window, ticks s + 20 to s + 51, one hit on the first tick the weapon touches an idle level hero straight ahead (s + 20 for a hero 2.0 or less ahead, s + 23 at 2.5, s + 26 at 3.0 to 4.5 with the chase stop at 3.175, s + 27 at 5.0, s + 28 at 5.5, s + 29 at 6.0; none from 6.5), not simply s + 20; gate in tick s + 59; end path in tick s + 60; next choice from s + 60 + N.
3. Slot 2 charge. State 6: id 43 is missing, so B (id 44, clip 40) starts at once with state 7. When the hero is within 5 units, state 8 requests C (id 45, clip 39) in tick c; one window, ticks c + 36 to c + 43, one hit (with the steal) on the first touching tick, not on the window start: c + 38 for an idle level hero 4.5 to 6.0 ahead when C starts, c + 39 at 3.5 or closer, c + 40 from 6.5, no touch at 4.0 (SPEC-0063, Rogue); gate in tick c + 81 (state 12); id 47 is missing, so the end path runs in tick c + 82.
4. Whole cycle for repeated slot-3 swings: the approach of state 0 (slot 3 needs d above 5, state 0 walks until d is at most 5), one tick from state 0 to state 1, 82 ticks of swing, N ticks of recovery and the roll wait (chance 25 per tick). About 100 ticks at least and about 105 to 120 ticks usually, so one swing every 1.7 to 2.0 seconds with up to three hits each. Playing clip 0 for the missing ids 48, 50 and 52 instead (one 100-tick cycle each) gives about 300 to 390 ticks per swing; that is wrong.

Ixi Henchman, ranged (row 157; the five wave Henchmen of pack 800 and one camp Henchman), a missile cadence with the hero standing still (disc values; High for the clip and tick arithmetic, Medium-High for the whole cycle). Row 157: approach 3 (keep distance, desired band 10 to 40), recovery 30 to 120. Slot 0: missile, band 10 to 40, chance 85, arc 0.924, delay 60 to 120, speed words 1.0, flight mode 0 (2.0 units per tick, range 40, so a 20-tick life), damage 5, spawn count 1. Slot 1: melee, band 2 to 12, chance 100, arc 0.707, range 10, speed words 1.3, 1.3 and 1.0, delay 60 to 180. Slot 2: missile, flight mode 1, band 15 to 80, chance 25, delay 60 to 240.
